# Animo v0.1.5 — Ambiguity Resolution Decision Log

**Date**: 2026-05-09
**Phase**: 2-4
**Predecessor spec**: v0.1.4
**Successor spec**: v0.1.5

This document records the final answer to every TBD listed in the v0.1.4
roadmap §4.7.1. All 17 ambiguities are resolved here. The matching changes
to spec, schema, runtime constants, and tests are summarized at the end.

The phrase "fail-loud" below means: prefer an exception that surfaces the
problem at the call site over a silent recovery that masks it. This is the
philosophy stated in spec §16.

---

## Q1 — `Affect("hunger", float.NaN)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentException`.

**Rationale**: Accepting a NaN delta would corrupt the Need value at the
next clamp (NaN compared to any boundary stays NaN), and that NaN would
propagate through every Influence and Action score in the same frame. A
silent Warning would let one bad frame poison the entire engine. Fail-loud
at the call site keeps the bug local to the caller.

---

## Q2 — `Affect("hunger", float.PositiveInfinity)` (and -Infinity)

**Resolution**: Clamp to the [0, 100] boundary like any other delta.

**Rationale**: `Affect` is the documented external-stimulus window
(spec §11.2). +Inf is naturally read as "an enormous push toward 100"
and -Inf as "an enormous pull toward 0". Neither produces a NaN under
the standard clamp; both yield a sensible saturation. No exception is
needed.

---

## Q3 — `Affect("undefined_need", +10)`

**Resolution**: Log a Warning via AnimoLog and treat as a no-op.

**Rationale**: The Need set is decided at composition time and stored as a
flat `float[]` (spec §16.2 Zero-Allocation Hot Path). Adding a Need at
runtime would invalidate the cache and the index map. A Warning surfaces
the misuse to the developer; the no-op keeps the game running.

---

## Q4 — `Affect("", +10)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentException`.

**Rationale**: An empty string is API misuse, not user content. Fail-loud.

---

## Q5 — `Affect(null, +10)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentNullException`.

**Rationale**: `Animo` compiles with `#nullable enable` (spec §15.3).
`null` for a non-nullable parameter is API misuse.

---

## Q6 — Composition produces empty `actions[]`

**Resolution**: Validator emits Error A011a.

**Rationale**: A persona with zero usable actions cannot drive an agent.
The composition pass already runs before the engine starts, so this is
catchable statically. A011 already covers the pre-composition case; the
post-composition case is the same logical failure.

---

## Q7 — Duplicate entries in `kind_ids` (e.g. `["goblin", "goblin"]`)

**Resolution**: Composer dedupes, **keeping the last occurrence**;
Validator emits new Warning **A033**.

**Rationale**: Spec §8.3 defines the cascade as **last-wins** at every
level (scalar, dictionary, array-by-id). Dedup must preserve that
semantics. With `["goblin", "scout", "goblin"]`, the third entry is
the canonical winner — applying `goblin` last is what the literal
order says to do. Keeping the *first* occurrence (and dropping the
final `goblin`) would produce the same composed result as
`["goblin", "scout"]`, where `scout` becomes the final overrider —
the opposite of what the JSON literally requested.

Implementation note: walking `kind_ids` left-to-right and, for each
id, recording the *latest* index restores the desired effective list
without changing the cascade engine.

**Phase_2_4_2 correction**: Phase_2_4_1 originally said "first
occurrence". That was a self-contradiction with §8.3 — corrected
here in v0.1.5 before any code shipped.

**New rule**:
> **A033** — `kind_ids` contains a duplicate id. Composer dedupes
> (keeping the last occurrence to preserve §8.3 last-wins cascade
> semantics) and the Engine continues, but the JSON should be
> cleaned up. **Warning**.

---

## Q8 — Negative `commitment.bonus`

**Resolution**: A028 is extended into a range Error: `commitment.bonus`
must be in `[0.0, 50.0]`. Negative values are an Error; values above 30
remain a Warning (existing A028 behavior).

**Rationale**: A negative bonus would cause the *current* action to lose
score every frame, producing pathological "anti-commitment" — the engine
would actively try to leave whatever it just chose. There is no game
design that wants this; it is always a typo or a sign-flip bug.

**Schema impact**: `commitment.bonus` JSON Schema range is now
`{minimum: 0, maximum: 50}` (was unconstrained). The 50 ceiling matches
the existing A028 Warning cliff plus a safety margin so the JSON Schema
itself never blocks a legitimate Warning case.

---

## Q9 — `Lock(duration: 0)`

**Resolution**: Treated as immediate `Unlock`. `is_locked` stays `false`,
`locked_behavior` stays `""`. No exception. No Warning.

**Rationale**: Equivalent to "lock for zero seconds, then immediately
release", which is the same observable state as not locking at all.

---

## Q10 — `Lock(duration: -1.0)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentException`.

**Rationale**: Negative duration is meaningless. Fail-loud.

---

## Q11 — `Live(dt: 0)`

**Resolution**: No-op. The engine returns immediately without advancing
any of the 5 steps. `behavior` is unchanged.

**Rationale**: Frame skips are normal in Unity (paused frames, editor
step-throughs). Returning quietly is correct.

**Already covered**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/TimeEdgeTests.cs`
Case01.

---

## Q12 — `Live(dt: -0.5)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentException`. (Stricter than the roadmap's
"clamp to 0 with Warning" suggestion.)

**Rationale**: Negative time has no physical meaning here. The roadmap's
clamp-with-Warning is a silent recovery that papers over a real bug in
the caller — typically a sign-flip on a frame-time variable. Fail-loud
catches the bug now instead of in production.

**Already covered**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/TimeEdgeTests.cs`
Case02 (decided in Phase_2_3_2).

---

## Q13 — `Live(dt: float.NaN)`

**Resolution**: Throw `ArgumentException`.

**Rationale**: NaN dt would poison every Need on Step 1 and propagate
to every Action score, leaving the engine unrecoverable. Fail-loud.

**Already covered**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/TimeEdgeTests.cs`
Case03 (decided in Phase_2_3_2).

---

## Q14 — `Lock` called while already locked

**Resolution**: **Replace**. The new duration overwrites the remaining
duration; the new mode overwrites the old mode. `locked_behavior` is
re-snapshotted to the *current* `behavior`.

**Rationale**: "Replace" is the simplest semantics to predict from the
caller's side. "Extend" composes silently, which makes runaway durations
hard to debug. Replace also lets a caller adjust mid-lock without
needing to call `Unlock` first.

---

## Q15 — `Unlock` called while not locked

**Resolution**: No-op. No exception, no Warning.

**Rationale**: Idempotency. Game code calling `Unlock` defensively at the
end of an animation should not crash if the lock already auto-released.

---

## Q16 — `force_reset = true` during Hard lock

**Resolution**: `behavior` does **not** change (Hard lock is observed by
Step 5). The named Need value **is** updated by `Affect`. Lock freezes
the *behavior selection*, not the Need state.

**Rationale**: Need state must continue to evolve so the engine can pick
correctly the moment the lock releases. Otherwise a long lock would
strand the agent in a behavior that no longer matches its needs.

**Debug API added** (Phase 2-4): `Engine.GetNeed(string need)` returns
the current value of the named Need. Read-only, intended for tests and
inspector tools, not the hot path. Spec §9.1 amended.

---

## Q17 — `Affect` called from a background thread

**Resolution**: Animo's API is documented as **main-thread-only** for
v0.1.5. Behavior on misuse is **undefined** (no thread-safety contract).
DOTS / Job System support is filed for v1.0+.

**Rationale**: Animo is a MonoBehaviour-driven library. Unity's lifecycle
guarantees `Update()` runs on the main thread, and idiomatic gameplay
code calls `Affect` from `Update`, `OnTriggerEnter`, etc., all of which
are main-thread. Adding lock or queue overhead to the hot path to defend
against a use case nobody currently has would violate spec §16.1
(Zero-Allocation Hot Path) for no real benefit. The documentation will
state the contract clearly so callers do not stumble in.

---

## Phase_2_4_3 — Lock-related sub-questions (Q-S1, Q-S2, Q-S3)

While confirming the Phase_2_4_2 corrections, three further gaps in the
Lock specification surfaced. They are resolved below before any Phase 3
implementation begins.

## Q-S1 — During Soft Lock, what is the "current action" that receives `commitment.bonus`?

**Resolution**: `locked_behavior`. The frozen visible action receives the
bonus during a Soft Lock, just as `behavior` does during normal play.

**Rationale**: Soft Lock's purpose (spec §24.2.1) is "the visible action
is frozen but the inner state keeps moving so the latest score is
reflected on next unlock." If the bonus tracked the internal score
leader, the bonus would chase a moving target the player cannot see, and
the lock-end transition would feel unstable: at unlock, the bonus
suddenly evaporates from the leader (because `behavior` is no longer
that), and a different action wins by score. Routing the bonus through
the *visible* action keeps the bonus mechanism behaviorally identical
across Hard, Soft, and unlocked states; only Step 5's output behavior
differs by mode.

Concretely:

| State | "current action" for Step 4 commitment.bonus |
|---|---|
| unlocked | `behavior` |
| Hard lock | `locked_behavior` (= the `behavior` snapshotted at Lock time) |
| Soft lock | `locked_behavior` (same) |

## Q-S2 — Does Step 3 (Threshold check + Bus.Publish) run during Lock?

**Resolution**: **Yes**, in both Hard and Soft Lock. Steps 1–4 run
exactly as the no-lock case; only Step 5's output is gated by Lock mode.

**Rationale**: Spec §24.3.1 already states "Steps 1–4 keep running."
The implementation must not collapse this principle to "everything
after `is_locked` is skipped". A wounded boss in the middle of a Hard-
locked attack animation must still scream when fear crosses its
threshold — Germio listens for that scream to spawn allies, play SFX,
or trigger cutscenes. Skipping Step 3 under Lock would silently break
every gameplay system that hangs off Animo's Bus signals.

Concretely:

| Step | Hard lock | Soft lock | unlocked |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — natural decay | runs | runs | runs |
| 2 — EffectiveNeeds | runs | runs | runs |
| 3 — Threshold + Bus.Publish | **runs** | **runs** | runs |
| 4 — score (with `commitment.bonus` on `locked_behavior`) | runs | runs | runs |
| 5 — switch decision | **skipped** (behavior frozen) | **skipped** (behavior frozen) | runs |

(Soft Lock and Hard Lock differ only in semantics around future
features; for v0.1.5 their public observable difference is documentary.
Both freeze `behavior` output. Spec §24.2.1's note that "the latest
score is reflected on next unlock" applies to both modes.)

## Q-S3 — Where in `Live(dt)` does the lock timer decrement and auto-release happen?

**Resolution**: At the **very start** of `Live(dt)`, before Step 1.

```
Live(dt):
  // Lock-timer phase (NEW — pinned in v0.1.5 by Q-S3)
  if (is_locked) {
      _lock_remaining -= dt;
      if (_lock_remaining <= 0f) Unlock();
  }
  // Step 1 — natural decay
  // Step 2 — EffectiveNeeds
  // Step 3 — Threshold + Bus.Publish
  // Step 4 — score
  // Step 5 — switch (skipped if still is_locked)
```

**Rationale**: Putting the timer at the head of the frame means the
Lock-check between Step 4 and Step 5 sees the *current* lock state for
this frame. So on the frame where `_lock_remaining` reaches zero, the
new `behavior` is selected by Step 5 in the **same** frame — no
1-frame stall waiting for the next `Live(dt)`.

For Zelda-style hit-stun and combo cancels (spec §20.1), this 16.6ms
matters: stale-by-one-frame outputs feel sticky to the player. Pinning
the decrement up front gives the engine maximum responsiveness without
any cost.

This also makes `Lock(0)` (Q9) trivial to implement consistently:
`Lock(duration: 0)` sets `_lock_remaining = 0`, the next `Live(dt)`
notices in the timer phase and calls `Unlock()` — no special path.
(Or, equivalently, `Lock(0)` calls `Unlock()` directly; the observable
state is identical because no `Live` has run yet.)

### Spec changes (Phase_2_4_3 patch on top of Phase_2_4_1/2)

- §24.3.1 expanded: Step 3 Bus.Publish explicitly runs during Lock.
- §24.4 amended: explicit "current action" routing table during Lock.
- §9.2 flow diagram: lock-timer-decrement node added at the head of
  `Live(dt)`, before Step 1.

### Test changes (Phase_2_4_3)

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/LockEdgeCaseTests.cs` — added:
  - `Case08_SoftLock_CommitmentBonusFollowsLockedBehavior` (Q-S1)
  - `Case09_HardLock_ThresholdStillPublishes` (Q-S2)
  - `Case10_SoftLock_ThresholdStillPublishes` (Q-S2)
  - `Case11_LockExpiresMidFrame_SwitchHappensSameFrame` (Q-S3)
- `Tests~/MiniUnity/MockBus.cs` — fixture is already adequate
  (`published_signals` ordering test in MiniUnity self-test 2 confirms it).

---

## Phase_2_4_4 — API surface sub-questions (Q-S4, Q-S5, Q-S6)

A second batch of spec gaps surfaced after Phase_2_4_3. These sit in
the *user-facing API surface* — places where C# idiom or operational
reality would lead an honest implementer into a bug. They are pinned
here so the Phase 3 implementer cannot get them wrong.

## Q-S4 — `ScenarioRunner.Run(events: ...)` should not key by `float`

**Resolution**: Change the `events` parameter from
`Dictionary<float, AffectEvent>` to `IReadOnlyList<TimedAffectEvent>`,
where `TimedAffectEvent` is a value-type carrier of `(float time,
AffectEvent ev)`. The runner sorts by `time` and consumes events
through a single forward pointer:

```csharp
namespace Animo.Tools {
    public readonly struct TimedAffectEvent {
        public float        time { get; }
        public AffectEvent  ev   { get; }
        public TimedAffectEvent(float time, AffectEvent ev) { ... }
    }

    public class ScenarioRunner {
        public TraceResult Run(
            string                            agent_id,
            float                             duration,
            float                             dt = 0.1f,
            IReadOnlyList<TimedAffectEvent>?  events = null
        );
    }
}
```

**Rationale**: `Dictionary<float, _>` is a documented C# anti-pattern.
The runner advances time by `t += dt` (floating-point accumulation),
so the apparent `time = 10.0f` after 100 steps of `dt = 0.1f` is in
fact `10.000003f` or similar. `Dictionary.ContainsKey(10.0f)` returns
`false`, the event never fires, and the test scenario passes
silently — exactly the kind of flake that wastes a day of debugging.

The forward-pointer model fires the event as soon as `current_time >=
events[next_index].time`, which is robust to any IEEE-754 drift. It
also preserves the "events at the same instant fire in the order the
caller wrote them", which a Dictionary cannot guarantee.

**Spec changes**: §26.3 `ScenarioRunner` API signature updated;
`Dictionary<float, AffectEvent>` removed; `TimedAffectEvent` added
to `Animo.Tools`. §26.4 examples rewritten with the list form.

**Phase scope**: ScenarioRunner is implemented in Phase 6; this Q is
a spec-only fix here (no test added in the Phase 2 Red baseline).

---

## Q-S5 — `force_reset` flag latching across multiple `Affect` calls in one frame

**Resolution**: `_force_reset_pending` is **set with OR-latch**, never
cleared by a subsequent `Affect`. The only place that clears it is
"After Step 4" inside `Live(dt)`.

```
On Affect(need, delta, force_reset):
    Needs[need] += delta; clamp to [0, 100]
    _force_reset_pending |= force_reset    // OR-latch, never assignment

On Live(dt) — after Step 4:
    _force_reset_pending = false           // single point of clearing
```

**Rationale**: The natural reading of the v0.1.4 flow diagram showed
the `true` branch only — the `false` branch was silent. A junior
implementer writes `_force_reset_pending = force_reset;` (plain
assignment) and the subsequent within-frame call
`Affect(_, _, force_reset: false)` clobbers a previously latched
`true`. Whether the emergency interrupt fires becomes order-dependent
and effectively random. The OR-latch makes "I asked for an emergency
this frame" stick until Step 4 honors it.

Concrete failing scenario this prevents:

```csharp
// Frame N — game systems publish two stimuli in arbitrary order
Store.Instance.Affect(agent_id: "g1", need: "fear",   delta: +30f, force_reset: true);   // emergency!
Store.Instance.Affect(agent_id: "g1", need: "hunger", delta: +5f);                       // routine tick
// Without OR-latch: hunger call clobbers fear's emergency flag.
// With OR-latch:   emergency fires in Step 4 as intended.
```

**Spec changes**: §9.7.2 flow updated to show explicit OR-latch and
the single clear point. §9.7.4 added: explicit "multi-call within one
frame" contract.

**Test added**: `AffectEdgeCaseTests.Case10_ForceResetLatches_NotClearedByLaterFalseCall`.

---

## Q-S6 — `Store.Register` called twice with the same `agent_id`

**Resolution**: `Store.Register(agent)` with an already-registered
`agent_id` **emits a Warning and is a no-op** (the original
registration is preserved). No exception is thrown.

| Situation | Behavior |
|---|---|
| `agent_id` not yet registered | register normally, no log |
| `agent_id` already registered (same instance) | no-op, no log |
| `agent_id` already registered (different instance) | **Warning, no-op, original kept** |
| `agent_id` not present at `Unregister` | **Warning, no-op** (already specified) |
| `agent_id` not present at `Affect` | **Warning, no-op** (already specified) |

**Rationale**: Three options were considered:

1. **Throw `InvalidOperationException`** — strictly fail-loud, but
   `Awake` runs during scene load. An exception from `Awake` in Unity
   can leave the scene in a half-initialized state and is much harder
   to recover from than a Warning. Rejected.
2. **Overwrite (last-wins)** — has appeal because it mirrors §8.3
   cascade semantics, but here the "old" instance is still in the
   scene with an `Animo.Engine` reference. Replacing the registration
   silently makes `Affect` route to the new instance while the old
   instance's `Update` still drives a stale `behavior`. Two ghosts
   diverging in lockstep is a debugging nightmare. Rejected.
3. **Warning + no-op (keep first)** — predictable: the agent that won
   the race owns the channel for its lifetime; the duplicate is
   visible in the log. Adopted.

This matches the existing `Affect` and `Unregister` policy of
"Warning + no-op for unknown / impossible cases", giving the Store
API a consistent posture: **never crash the scene, always log the
anomaly, keep going**.

**Spec changes**: §11.2 Specs table extended with the re-register row.

**Test added**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/Store/StoreRegisterDuplicateTests.cs`
(new file, 4 cases). Note: this is the *first* Store test file in the
suite — it requires a small `Animo.Store` stub in `Scripts/`.
---

## Phase_2_4_5 — Frame-1 / startup sub-questions (Q-S7, Q-S8, Q-S9)

The third batch of spec gaps cluster around the moment the engine
*starts running*: `Agent.Awake` of the Unity object, the first
`Live(dt)` call, and the very first Step 5 selection. Each of these
gaps causes a different category of bug — a hard crash, a Bus storm,
or a silently non-deterministic NPC default — that is invisible until
ship.

## Q-S7 — A016 (Warning) + `binding` null causes `Agent.Awake` NRE

**Resolution**: Two coordinated fixes.

1. **Composer normalizes `binding`**: when the input persona has
   `binding == null`, Composer fills in a default `Binding` instance
   whose templates are the engine defaults from `Animo.Const`
   (`DEFAULT_ON_ACTION_CHANGE` etc.). The Validator still emits
   **A016 (Warning)** so the JSON author hears about the omission,
   but the post-Composer `Persona` always has a non-null `binding`
   with non-null template strings.
2. **Sample code in §16.5 uses null-safe defaults**: even though the
   Composer guarantees non-null after composition, the sample is
   updated to `_persona.binding?.on_action_change ?? Const.DEFAULT_ON_ACTION_CHANGE`
   — defense in depth against handwritten Persona instances that
   bypass the Composer (e.g. tests that build a Persona directly).

**Rationale**: A016 was a Warning all along (spec §13.1) so a
JSON-only author can ship a Persona with `binding == null`. Spec §7.3
already promises engine defaults exist (`animo_{agent_id}_{behavior}`).
But the §16.5 String Cache sample dereferenced
`_persona.binding.on_action_change` unconditionally — guaranteed NRE
in `Awake`, the same crash class Q-S6 went out of its way to prevent
for `Store.Register`. The two fixes together restore the §16.1
Zero-Allocation Hot Path: the cache loop in `Awake` is branch-free
on the canonical path (Composer fills defaults), and the fallback
operator only kicks in on direct-Persona-construction edge cases.

**Spec changes**:
- §7.3 default table cross-referenced from §16.5 sample.
- §16.5 sample code rewritten with null-safe fallback.
- §10 Composer responsibility table extended: "fill missing `binding`
  with `Binding(default templates)`".
- §13.1 A016 row clarified: "Composer fills with defaults; Validator
  warns. Internal state always non-null after composition."

**Test added**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/BindingDefaultFillTests.cs`
(new file, 3 cases) — pins that `Composer.Compose` returns a `Persona`
with non-null `binding` and non-null template strings even when the
input Persona has `binding == null`.

---

## Q-S8 — Frame 1 "newborn scream": `_previous_needs[i] == 0` causes false-positive thresholds

**Resolution**: The `Engine` constructor **seeds the threshold-state
machine from the spawn-time Need values**. No threshold can fire on
the very first `Live(dt)` for a Need that is *already* above its
trigger threshold at spawn — only a real upward crossing fires.

Concretely (state-machine wording per §12.3.2):

```
On Engine ctor:
    foreach threshold t:
        if (Needs[t.need_index] >= t.trigger_threshold)
            _threshold_state[t] = Above
        else
            _threshold_state[t] = Below
```

Equivalently (compare-with-previous wording per §9.2 Step 3):

```
On Engine ctor:
    Array.Copy(_needs, _previous_needs, N);
    // first Live(dt) sees _previous_needs[i] == _needs[i],
    // so no Need has "risen" — no spurious trigger.
```

**Rationale**: C# `new float[N]` zero-fills. A Persona spawned with
`fear: 80` would, on its first `Live(dt)`, see "fear rose from 0 → 80
this frame" — every NPC with a high spawn-time fear screams the
moment the scene loads. Germio listening for `fear_critical` would
get a Bus storm at scene start. The seeding rule says: the spawn
state is the baseline; thresholds fire only on changes that happen
*after* spawn.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.2 Step 3 description amended: "the very first `Live(dt)` after
  Engine construction sees `_previous_needs == _needs`, so no Need
  is reported as having risen."
- §16.6 Affected Classes row for `_previous_needs` extended:
  "**Initialized in Engine ctor with the spawn-time Need values**
  (v0.1.5, Q-S8). Never reset to zero."

**Test added**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/Step3_ThresholdTests.cs`
gains `Case_FrameOne_HighSpawnNeed_DoesNotFireSpuriousThreshold`.

---

## Q-S9 — Step 5 tie-break when all action scores are equal (e.g. all-zero spawn)

**Resolution**: **Declaration order wins.** When two or more actions
share the maximum score, the action whose `id` appears earliest in
the persona's `actions[]` array is selected.

```
On Step 5 with multiple actions at max score:
    selected = actions.First(a => a.score == max_score)
    // i.e. the index-0 candidate among ties
```

Special case for the very first `Live(dt)`: `behavior` is empty
(spec §9.1). On that frame, `commitment.bonus` is added to no action
(the "current action" doesn't exist yet). Step 4 produces scores
without commitment cushion; Step 5 selects per the rule above.

**Rationale**: Three options were considered:

1. **Highest tier wins on tie** — has Maslow-flavored appeal, but
   the spawn-time all-zero case is precisely where Maslow gives no
   guidance; "tier" is metadata about the *Need*, not a tiebreaker
   for the *Action*. Rejected.
2. **C# implementation default** (`OrderBy`, `Max + IndexOf`, etc.)
   — non-deterministic across LINQ flavors and across `actions[]`
   reorderings in the JSON. Breaks `ScenarioRunner` reproducibility
   (spec §26.2 promises determinism). Rejected.
3. **Declaration order** — the LLM authoring the JSON already
   ordered `actions[]` for human readability; that order *is* a
   reasonable priority signal. Implementer has zero choice;
   ScenarioRunner stays deterministic; reordering the JSON is the
   one knob to tune the tiebreaker. Adopted.

This also gives a designer a single, predictable lever for
"NPC default behavior at spawn before anything has happened" —
just put `Idle` (or whatever the desired default is) at index 0 of
`actions[]`.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.2 Step 5 description extended: "tie-break: action whose `id`
  appears first in the persona's `actions[]` array. The very first
  `Live(dt)` selects per this rule because `behavior` is empty and
  `commitment.bonus` applies to no action."

**Test added**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/Step5_ActionSwitchTests.cs`
gains `Case_AllZeroScores_FirstDeclaredActionWins` and
`Case_FirstLive_NoCommitmentBonus_DeclarationOrderWins`.

## Q-S10 — `force_reset` latch lifecycle while engine is locked (Gemini ninth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

The latch contract from Q-S5 (§9.7.2.1) says
`_force_reset_pending` is cleared "in exactly one place: right after
Step 4 inside `Live(dt)`". The Lock pipeline contract from Q-S2
(§24.3.1) says Step 4 **runs** during both Hard and Soft Lock; only
Step 5 is skipped. Composing the two: a `Affect(force_reset: true)`
arriving mid-lock has its latch set, then silently consumed by the
locked Step 4, and erased before any post-unlock Step 5 ever runs.
The first frame after unlock evaluates Step 5 with the *full*
commitment cushion intact. The NPC refuses to switch to Flee even
though the game explicitly asked for an emergency interrupt.

**Resolution**: **Gate the post-Step-4 clear on `!is_locked`.** The
latch survives the lock and is consumed only when the first
post-unlock Step 5 actually runs.

```csharp
// Inside Live(dt), at the end of Step 4:
if (!is_locked) {
    _force_reset_pending = false;   // ✅ clear only outside Lock
}
```

Why this is safe:

- The latch is OR-only (§9.7.2.1). It cannot accidentally fall to
  `false` mid-lock; the only descent is the conditional clear above.
- Step 4 already branches on `_force_reset_pending`; one extra
  `is_locked` check is hot-path neutral.
- During Lock, Step 4 still applies "skip commitment_bonus on
  `locked_behavior`" if the latch is set. This is harmless: the
  player cannot observe the locked_behavior's score, so whether the
  cushion is on or off makes no behavior-selection difference. The
  surviving latch is what matters.
- Multiple `Affect(force_reset: true)` calls during Lock OR-merge
  into the same surviving latch; they do not stack. The first
  post-unlock Step 5 honors a single emergency, regardless of how
  many were raised mid-lock.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Suppress the latch entirely during Lock (treat `force_reset`
   as ignored mid-lock)** — matches the old §24.4 wording but
   silently drops the game's emergency request. The whole point of
   `force_reset` is to override stubbornness; making Lock another
   wall it cannot cross neuters the API.
2. **Clear at Step 5 instead of Step 4** — would require shifting
   the §9.7.2 single-clear-point and rippling through every Step-4
   description. The minimal surgery is the conditional gate; the
   single-clear-point is preserved (Step 4 end), with one branch
   added.
3. **Re-fire the latch on unlock automatically** — synthetic
   "ghost" Affect calls leak side-channels; testable surface should
   only contain things the user actually called.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.7.2 Mermaid flow extended: a new `LockGate` decision after
  `Skip` routes the `unlocked` arm to the existing `Reset` (clear)
  node and the `locked (Hard or Soft)` arm to a new `Carry` node
  that explicitly preserves the latch.
- §9.7.2.1 paragraph wording extended: "and only when the engine is
  not locked … survives until the first post-unlock Step 5 consumes
  it (see §24.4.2)".
- §24.4 table rewritten: instead of "Hard: ignored / Soft: Need
  updates", three columns — *Situation*, *latch behavior*,
  *observable effect* — make the contract explicit.
- §24.4.2 added: "force_reset Latch Survives Lock". Includes the
  one-line `if (!is_locked)` snippet, a 4-row state-sequence table,
  and the multi-Affect-during-Lock semantics.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_6, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/LockForceResetCarryoverTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_ForceReset_DuringHardLock_LatchSurvivesUntilUnlock`
  - `Case02_ForceReset_DuringSoftLock_LatchSurvivesUntilUnlock`

Note that direct observation of `_force_reset_pending` requires
either a debug-API exposure or a `MockBus` injection seam; the
Phase_2_4_6 tests pin the *observable* contract — first post-unlock
Step 5 evaluates *without* `commitment.bonus` on the previous
behavior, even though Step 4 ran inside the lock and would
ordinarily have cleared the latch. Phase 3 may add finer-grained
internal-state tests once the Engine implementation lands.

## Q-S11 — `reset_threshold` underflow against the Need `[0, 100]` clamp (Gemini ninth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

§7.3 specified the omitted-field default for `reset_threshold` as
`trigger_threshold - 5.0`. §9.9 mandates that all Need values are
always `Clamp(value, 0, 100)`. A013 (`actions[].tier`) and A010
(`thresholds[].trigger_threshold`) constrain triggers to `[0, 100]`,
but A023 (`trigger_threshold > reset_threshold`) does not require
`reset_threshold >= 0`. So an LLM author writing
`{"trigger_threshold": 3.0}` (no `reset_threshold` field) gets a
default `reset_threshold = -2.0`. The two-stage hysteresis state
machine (§12.3.2) requires `need <= reset_threshold` to descend back
to `Below` and re-arm — but the Need clamp guarantees the value
*never* reaches `-2.0`. The Threshold fires once and then traps in
`Above` forever. No more `Bus.Publish` for that signal, ever.

**Resolution**: **Floor the omit-default at `0.0`, and reject
explicit user negatives via a new Validator rule A034.**

- *Composer change* (§10.2.3 step 7b, §12.3.4): when
  `reset_threshold` is omitted (null in the deserialized field),
  fill with `Math.Max(0.0, trigger_threshold - 5.0)`. The floor
  applies *only* to omission, not to typed-in values.
- *New Validator rule A034* (§13.1, §3.1): if the JSON literally
  contains `reset_threshold: <negative>`, surface as **Error**.
  Rationale: a typed-in negative is almost certainly an authoring
  typo; silent correction would mask the mistake from the LLM
  author. Symmetric with A028 (commitment.bonus negative is also
  Error).

**Why split into Composer-floor and Validator-error**:

| Source of negative | Treatment | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Field omitted (null) | Composer fills floor `0` | Defaults must be safe; the LLM author asked for "engine choose for me" and the engine cannot produce a deadlocking config |
| Field explicit (`-2.0`) | A034 Error | The author *typed* a value; surface their mistake instead of overwriting their intent |

This mirrors the existing principle "Composer fills missing fields
with safe defaults; Validator catches inconsistent explicit values"
already in use across A011 / A028 / A033.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Always Math.Max-floor regardless of source** — silently fixes
   user typos; loses signal. Rejected.
2. **Always Validator-error regardless of source** — a missing
   field would have to be filled *somewhere* anyway; pushing the
   floor responsibility outside Composer just relocates the
   problem and forces the author to type `reset_threshold: 0`
   explicitly when they wanted to use the default. Rejected.
3. **Drop the default entirely (require explicit
   `reset_threshold`)** — breaks v0.1.4 backward compatibility;
   thousands of LLM-generated JSON files would fail. Rejected.

**Spec changes**:
- §7.3: default formula updated to
  `Math.Max(0.0, trigger_threshold - 5.0)`.
- §12.3.3 sentence updated to match.
- §12.3.4 added: "Why the `Math.Max(0, ...)` Floor". Includes a
  5-row trigger→default→floored table and the Composer/Validator
  split rationale.
- §10.2.3 step 7b added: Composer fills omitted reset_threshold.
- §13.1 A034 row added; §3.1 A034 paragraph added.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_6, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/ResetThresholdFloorTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_OmittedResetThreshold_LowTrigger_FilledAtZeroFloor`
  - `Case02_OmittedResetThreshold_HighTrigger_FilledAtTriggerMinusFive`
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A034_ResetThresholdNegativeTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_ExplicitNegativeResetThreshold_FailsA034`
  - `Case02_OmittedResetThreshold_DoesNotFailA034`

---

## Q-S12 — `Binding.thresholds` null safety: Q-S7's NRE migrates one line down (Gemini ninth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec AND the
implementation**:

Q-S7 (§16.5) hardened `Awake` against `_persona.binding == null` by
having Composer fill a default `Binding`. The §10.2.3 step-7
plan said `new Binding { on_action_change = Const.DEFAULT_..., ... }`
— with the trailing `...` glossing over `thresholds`. The
deserialization-side type in `Scripts/Data.cs` is:

```csharp
public class Binding {
    public string? on_action_change { get; set; }
    public List<Threshold>? thresholds { get; set; }   // ← nullable!
}
```

— which means `new Binding { on_action_change = "..." }` produces a
`Binding` whose `thresholds` is `null`. The §16.6 affected-classes
table promises an Awake-time `_cached_threshold_triggers` cache;
implementing it requires `foreach (var t in binding.thresholds)`,
which immediately NREs. **The Q-S7 line dropped, and the next line
re-introduced the same crash.** Verified by reading `Scripts/Data.cs`
directly — the field is genuinely nullable and uninitialized.

**Resolution**: **Three-layer defense, same shape as Q-S6/Q-S7**:

1. *Type-level* (`Scripts/Data.cs`): change
   `public List<Threshold>? thresholds` to
   `public List<Threshold> thresholds { get; set; } = new();`.
   `new Binding {...}` now produces a non-null empty list by
   default; bypassing Composer no longer creates a null hole.
2. *Composer-level* (§10.2.3 step 7): default-fill explicitly
   includes `thresholds = new List<Threshold>()`. If a hand-built
   `Binding` somehow has `thresholds = null`, Composer normalizes
   it to empty. Every Persona that goes through Composer is
   guaranteed `binding.thresholds != null`.
3. *Awake-level* (§16.5 sample): the threshold-cache loop uses
   `_persona.binding?.thresholds ?? Array.Empty<Threshold>()`.
   Defense in depth for direct-construction Personas (e.g. tests)
   that bypass Composer entirely.

The three layers are not redundant — they protect against three
different bypass paths:

| Layer | Protects against |
|---|---|
| Type default | `new Binding {...}` with field omitted |
| Composer fill | hand-built `Binding` with `thresholds = null` set explicitly |
| Awake `??` | Persona that bypasses Composer entirely (tests, runtime mocking) |

**Why not other options**:

1. **Type default only (drop Composer + Awake guards)** — the type
   default is bypassed if any code path explicitly sets
   `thresholds = null`. Composer + Awake guards make this a
   non-issue regardless. Rejected.
2. **Composer fill only (drop type default + Awake guard)** — Awake
   crashes for any Persona that bypasses Composer (the very class
   of Persona the test suite uses). Rejected.
3. **Awake guard only (drop type default + Composer fill)** — works
   today, but every future Awake-time `binding.X` access has to
   remember to guard. The type default + Composer fill makes the
   non-null property an *invariant* of the system, not a
   per-call-site discipline. Adopted.

**Spec changes**:
- §10.2.3 step 7 rewritten to explicitly include
  `thresholds = new List<Threshold>()` in the default-fill, plus a
  step 7b for the Q-S11 reset_threshold floor.
- §16.5 sample extended: `_cached_threshold_triggers` cache loop
  added with `?? Array.Empty<Threshold>()` guard.
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Binding.thresholds` changed to
  non-nullable + empty-list default. Comment notes Q-S12.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_6, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/BindingThresholdsDefaultFillTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_NullBinding_ComposerFillsBindingWithEmptyThresholdsList`
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/ResetThresholdFloorTests.cs`
  (shared with Q-S11) covers step 7b.

Direct observation of Awake-time crash-or-not requires `MockBus`
injection on Engine ctor — Phase 3 follow-up. Phase_2_4_6 pins the
data-shape contract: after Compose, `binding.thresholds` is never
null.

## Q-S13 — Phase_2_4_6 self-contradiction: Q-S10 broke `force_reset`'s ONE-frame promise (Gemini tenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

§9.7.1 explicitly says `force_reset: true` "for **ONE frame** in the
next Live(), do not add commitment_bonus". Phase_2_4_6's Q-S10
Mermaid in §9.7.2 routes `Step4 (true) → Skip → LockGate → (locked)
Carry`. So while locked, every frame's Step 4 sees
`_force_reset_pending == true`, applies Skip, and only then is
prevented from clearing the latch by LockGate. With a 5-second Soft
Lock at 60 fps, that is 300 frames of commitment-bonus removal — a
multi-frame debuff, not a one-frame interrupt. Worse, Phase_2_4_6's
own decision log waved this away as "unobservable because the
locked_behavior's score is hidden", but `ScenarioRunner` traces and
debug score telemetry (§26.2) make it directly observable. The "ONE
frame" contract was a self-inflicted lie.

**Resolution**: **move `LockGate` upstream of `Skip`** so that while
locked, *neither* the skip *nor* the latch clear runs. The latch
itself (a `bool`) survives, but Step 4 behaves as if
`_force_reset_pending == false` for the duration of the lock. The
first post-unlock Step 4 honors the latch exactly once.

```csharp
// Inside Live(dt), at Step 4:
if (_force_reset_pending && !is_locked) {
    // skip commitment_bonus for the current action
} else {
    // normal commitment_bonus add (covers locked-and-latched too)
}

// At end of Step 4:
if (!is_locked) {
    _force_reset_pending = false;   // ✅ clear only outside Lock
}
```

| State sequence | Frame N (Affect+Lock) | Frame N+1 .. unlock-1 | First post-unlock frame |
|---|---|---|---|
| latch | `true` | `true` (clear suppressed) | consumed in Step 4, cleared after |
| Step 4 commitment skip | **no** (suppressed; bonus added on `locked_behavior`) | **no** | **yes** (on `behavior`) |
| Step 5 | skipped (locked) | skipped (locked) | runs — no commitment cushion → Flee can win |

Why two branches, not one: Q-S10 needed `if (!is_locked)` only at
the latch clear. Q-S13 adds the same gate at the skip itself. Both
are hot-path neutral — Step 4 already branches on
`_force_reset_pending`, and `is_locked` is a single field read.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Keep Phase_2_4_6's Skip-then-Gate ordering and accept
   multi-frame skip** — directly violates §9.7.1's "ONE frame"
   wording. Would also require rewriting §9.7.1 to "while locked,
   skipping is per-frame" — losing the contract's simplicity.
   Rejected.
2. **Suppress the latch entirely during Lock (treat
   `force_reset` as ignored mid-lock, not just deferred)** — reverts
   Q-S10. The whole point of Q-S10 was that the game's emergency
   request *must* survive the lock; dropping it silently brings
   back the original Gemini-9 hit. Rejected.
3. **Move Skip + Reset together to "only on first post-unlock
   frame", erasing both during Lock** — what this resolution
   actually does. Adopted.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.7.2 Mermaid edges rewired: `Step4 (true) → LockGate`, with
  `LockGate -> unlocked → Skip → Reset → End` and `LockGate ->
  locked → Carry → End`. Carry node text updated to "commitment_bonus
  add proceeds normally on locked_behavior".
- §9.7.2 prose paragraph added explaining "LockGate upstream of
  Skip".
- §24.4.2 rewritten end-to-end: two-branch code snippet, updated
  state-sequence table ("Step 4 commitment skip" row now reads
  `no / no / yes`), new "Why two branches, not one" subsection.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_7, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/LockForceResetSkipSuppressionTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_ForceResetDuringLock_SkipSuppressed_LockedBehaviorKeepsBonus`
  - `Case02_LockedFor5Seconds_SkipNeverConsumed_BehaviorOnlyChangesAfterUnlock`

The existing `LockForceResetCarryoverTests` from Phase_2_4_6 still
pin the latch-survives-Lock contract; Q-S13 tests add the
"skip-also-suppressed" half of the contract.

---

## Q-S14 — Same-Need multiple thresholds were structurally forbidden (Gemini tenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec AND the
Phase_2_4_6 Awake sample**:

Two independent collapses prevented multi-milestone modeling on a
single Need:
1. §8.3 Merge Unit table for `thresholds`: "match by `need`,
   last-wins". A Kind that defines `fear=50 → "alerted"` followed
   by a Persona that defines `fear=80 → "panic"` would lose
   "alerted" entirely — Composer's last-wins by `need` overwrites
   the Kind's threshold with the Persona's.
2. §16.5 Awake sample (Phase_2_4_6 wrote this): `_cached_threshold_triggers[t.need] = expanded;`
   — a `Dictionary<string, string>` keyed by Need. Even if both
   thresholds somehow survived Composer (e.g. defined together in
   one Persona's `thresholds[]`), the dict assignment would
   silently overwrite the first with the second.

This made "fear at 50 fires `alerted`, fear at 80 fires `panic`" —
a *standard* Utility-AI staged-emotion pattern — structurally
unrepresentable in Animo. Verified by reading both the §8.3 table
text and the §16.5 code sample directly.

**Resolution**: **change the threshold merge unit and abandon the
by-Need cache key**.

- *Merge unit* (§8.3): `thresholds` now matches by
  `(need, trigger_threshold)` compound key, last-wins. Two
  thresholds on the same Need with different `trigger_threshold`s
  are siblings, not duplicates. A Kind's `fear=50 alerted` and a
  Persona's `fear=80 panic` both survive composition.
- *Awake cache* (§16.5, §16.6): drop the by-Need `Dictionary` and
  add `internal string expanded_trigger` to the `Threshold` class
  in `Scripts/Data.cs`. Awake walks the list once and writes the
  per-instance field; Step 3 walks the same list and reads each
  instance's pre-expanded string. No keying step that can collapse
  siblings.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Pure append (kind's thresholds always concatenated to
   persona's, never overridden)** — loses the cascade's design
   purpose ("Persona overrides Kind"). A designer who wants to
   *replace* a Kind's threshold cannot. Rejected.
2. **Compound key on `(need, trigger)`** — adopted.
3. **Compound key on `(need, trigger, reset)`** — too granular.
   Two thresholds with the same trigger but different resets are
   inconsistent state-machine design; collapsing them via
   last-wins is correct. Rejected.
4. **Keep the Dictionary but key on full `Threshold` object** —
   reference equality on a deep-copied object after Composer is
   fragile and obscure. The per-instance field is simpler. Rejected.

**Spec changes**:
- §8.3 table row for `Array (thresholds)` rewritten with the
  compound key and a multi-milestone example.
- §16.5 code sample rewritten: `_cached_threshold_triggers`
  Dictionary removed; per-Threshold `expanded_trigger` field
  populated in the foreach. Added a `Step3_Thresholds()` snippet
  showing the read side.
- §16.6 affected-classes table: existing `_cached_threshold_triggers`
  row marked as removed; new `Threshold.expanded_trigger` row added.
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Threshold` class gains
  `internal string expanded_trigger = "";`.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_7, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/ThresholdsMultiMilestoneTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_TwoThresholdsOnSameNeed_DifferentTriggers_BothSurviveCompose`
  - `Case02_KindFearLow_PersonaFearHigh_BothSurviveCascade`

Direct observation of the Step-3 firing path with both thresholds
firing in sequence requires Engine implementation (Phase 3); these
tests pin the data-shape contract: post-Compose, both thresholds
exist in the composed `binding.thresholds` list with their
respective `trigger_threshold` values.

## Q-S15 — Validator A023 bypass via Composer omit-fill (Gemini tenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

§13.2 Validation Flow runs Validator on the raw `Root` only —
Composer runs *afterwards*. A023 (`trigger_threshold > reset_threshold`)
sees only raw fields; an omitted `reset_threshold` is `null` and the
comparison is bypassed. Q-S11's Composer fill then sets
`reset_threshold = Math.Max(0.0, trigger_threshold - 5.0)`. For
`trigger_threshold = 0.0`, the floor produces `reset_threshold = 0.0`,
and the post-Compose pair `(0.0, 0.0)` violates A023's strict
`trigger > reset` requirement — but A023 has already run and passed
(it never saw the `null`). With Need clamp `[0, 100]` the value sits
at `0.0` whenever fully drained, satisfying both `need >= trigger`
and `need <= reset` simultaneously, every frame. Bus.Publish chatter,
A023 silently bypassed. Q-S11 created the bypass; A010 (which allows
`trigger_threshold = 0`) admits the entry condition.

**Resolution**: **two-pronged fix — tighten A010 + add post-composition A035**.

- *Tighten A010 (§13.1)*: range from `[0.0, 100.0]` to
  `(0.0, 100.0]`. `trigger_threshold = 0` has no semantic meaning at
  the Need clamp's lower bound — a 0-trigger fires every frame the
  Need stays at 0. Stage-1 Error.
- *New A035 (§13.1, §13.2 stage 2)*: after Composer fills omitted
  defaults, re-assert `trigger > reset` strictly. Catches any
  residual case where post-fill values collapse the gap (e.g. very
  low triggers near the floor, or future edits that change Q-S11's
  formula). Stage-2 Error.

The Validator is now explicitly two-stage: stage 1 on raw `Root`,
stage 2 on the per-Persona composed output. A035 is the first
stage-2 rule; future post-composition checks will live alongside it.

**Why both A010 tightening and A035, not one or the other**:

- A010 alone: closes the `trigger=0` boundary but not the general
  pattern. Future changes to Q-S11's formula (e.g. allowing
  negative-floor results) would re-open the bypass.
- A035 alone: closes the bypass but `trigger=0` would still pass
  validation in odd corners (e.g. `trigger=0, reset=-5` rejected
  by A034, `trigger=0, reset=null` rejected by A035; consistency
  is muddled).
- Both: A010 keeps the boundary clean at the JSON authoring layer,
  A035 keeps the post-fill invariant strict. Defense in depth.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Make Composer reject `trigger=0` instead** — Composer should
   not throw on validation issues; that is the Validator's job.
   Rejected.
2. **Lower-bound A035 to `trigger >= reset + epsilon`** — chatter
   is not just a `==` problem; sub-frame oscillation near the
   boundary is. But picking an epsilon is arbitrary and authoring-
   unfriendly (LLMs cannot reliably account for engine-internal
   epsilon). The strict `>` rule with no epsilon is honest:
   designers who want anti-chatter should specify the gap themselves.
   Rejected.
3. **Remove Q-S11's floor entirely (require explicit
   `reset_threshold` for low triggers)** — breaks v0.1.4 backward
   compat where any `trigger >= 5.0` could omit reset. Rejected.

**Spec changes**:
- §13.1 A010 range tightened; row note records the v0.1.5 change.
- §13.1 A035 row added.
- §13 title: "A000-A034" → "A000-A035".
- §13.2 Validation Flow Mermaid restructured into two-stage with a
  visible `Composer.Compose(...)` node between stage 1 and stage 2.
  Prose paragraph added explaining the split.
- §3.1 paragraphs added for A035 and the A010 tightening.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_7, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A010_TriggerThresholdRangeTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_TriggerThresholdZero_FailsA010_PostQS15`
  (the existing positive-range cases for A010 already cover the
  upper boundary)
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A035_PostComposeTriggerGtResetTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_TriggerZero_OmittedReset_PostComposePairZeroZero_FailsA035`
  - `Case02_TriggerFive_OmittedReset_PostComposePairFiveZero_PassesA035`

## Q-S16 — Need → Tier engine implementation contract (Gemini eleventh review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec AND `Const.cs`**:

§3.5 declares the standard Need tier table (`hunger=1, fatigue=1,
fear=2, frustration=2, loneliness=3, confidence=4, curiosity=5,
idle=5`) and §9.3.4 specifies `max_lower_tier_intensity = max(eff_needs[tier1
needs] / 100, eff_needs[tier2 needs] / 100, ...)`. The formula
requires the Engine to know which Needs belong to which tier. But
reading `Scripts/Const.cs`:

```csharp
public static readonly string[] STANDARD_NEEDS = { "hunger", ..., "frustration" };
public const int NEED_INDEX_HUNGER      = 0;
// ... eight index constants ...
```

— there is a name list and per-Need index constants, **but no
mapping from name (or index) to tier**. The Engine has no way to
compute the formula. The §3.5 table was authoritative
documentation that the Engine could not consume. Gemini's
secondary point — that frustration used only via `influences`
(§25.5.2 pattern) might be excluded from the suppression
calculation — was a legitimate ambiguity since the spec never
spelled out whether the tier-2 set in `max_lower_tier_intensity`
includes Needs that have no `Action`.

**Resolution**: **publish the tier maps as part of `Animo.Const`,
and pin the non-standard / Action-less semantics in the spec**.

```csharp
// Animo.Const additions (Q-S16):
public static readonly Dictionary<string, int> NEED_TIER_BY_NAME = new() {
    { "hunger", 1 }, { "fatigue", 1 },
    { "fear",   2 }, { "frustration", 2 },
    { "loneliness", 3 },
    { "confidence", 4 },
    { "curiosity",  5 }, { "idle", 5 }
};

public static readonly Dictionary<int, int[]> NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER = new() {
    { 1, new[] { NEED_INDEX_HUNGER, NEED_INDEX_FATIGUE } },
    { 2, new[] { NEED_INDEX_FEAR, NEED_INDEX_FRUSTRATION } },
    { 3, new[] { NEED_INDEX_LONELINESS } },
    { 4, new[] { NEED_INDEX_CONFIDENCE } },
    { 5, new[] { NEED_INDEX_CURIOSITY, NEED_INDEX_IDLE } }
};
```

Two complementary maps:
- `NEED_TIER_BY_NAME` for setup-time lookup ("the Persona declared
  a `fear` Need; what tier?").
- `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER` for hot-path summation ("we need
  `max_lower_tier_intensity` for a tier-3 Action; iterate tiers 1
  and 2"). Phase 3 implementations should snapshot this into an
  `int[][]` for zero-allocation use.

**Non-standard Needs are EXCLUDED.** A custom Need not in
`STANDARD_NEEDS` (already an A019 Warning) does not appear in
either map. It does not contribute to or receive Maslow
suppression. Custom Needs participate in `influences` and
`Action.need` references normally; they sit outside the pyramid.

Why excluded rather than defaulted to a tier:

- "Default tier 5" silently puts unknown Needs at the bottom of
  suppression order. For curiosity-flavored Needs this happens to
  be correct; for hunger-flavored Needs it is catastrophic.
- The honest contract is to force authors who want Maslow
  participation to use a standard Need name.

**`frustration` participates fully.** Even when frustration has no
`Action` of its own (§25.5.2 pattern: frustration amplifies fear
via `influences`), it still appears in `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[2]`.
A rising frustration *does* suppress tier-3-or-higher Actions
through `max_lower_tier_intensity`. This was Gemini-11's specific
worry; the contract makes it explicit.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Read tier from `Action.tier` of the first Action that
   references the Need** — fragile (a Need referenced by both a
   tier-1 and a tier-5 Action has no defined tier); breaks the
   §25.5.2 pattern entirely (frustration with no Action gets no
   tier and never suppresses). Rejected.
2. **Allow JSON to declare per-Need tier overrides** — adds a new
   authoring surface that LLMs would have to learn; conflicts with
   "Pure Need-driven" simplicity. Rejected.
3. **Const-level maps, exclude non-standard** — adopted.

**Spec changes**:
- §3.5 gains a new §3.5.1 "Engine Implementation Contract for
  Need → Tier" with the two maps' definitions, the non-standard
  exclusion rule, and the explicit `frustration` participation
  clause.
- §9.3.4 paragraph after the formula updated to point at
  `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER`.
- §3.1 paragraph added documenting the contract.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_8, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/NeedTierMapTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_AllStandardNeedsMappedToTiers` — all eight standard
    Needs appear in `NEED_TIER_BY_NAME` with the §3.5 values
  - `Case02_NeedIndicesByTier_FrustrationAtTier2_NotIsolated`
    — frustration is in tier-2 alongside fear, not orphaned

## Q-S17 — A025 in stage 1 only allows ghost cycles to escape composition (Gemini eleventh review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

§13.2 Mermaid placed A025 cycle detection in stage 1 — it sees
only the raw `kinds[]` and `persona.influences[]` arrays. §8.3
defines the influence merge unit as "match by `source`+`target`,
last-wins"; the merged `influences` graph is the union of all
Kind influences plus the Persona's influences. A "ghost cycle"
synthesized only by overlay (e.g. `kinds[0]` provides
`fear → confidence`; persona provides `confidence → fear`) is
visible in neither input array but exists in the merged graph.
Stage 1 A025 reports "no cycle" and lets the JSON pass. Stage 2
(post-Q-S15) only had A035 — no cycle check. The Engine's Step 2
topological sort at runtime would then throw on the first Live(dt).

**Resolution**: **run A025 in BOTH stages**. Stage 1 keeps the
early-warning behavior (trivially-cyclic raw JSON still fails fast,
pointing the LLM at the offending lines). Stage 2 rebuilds the
composed influences graph and re-applies cycle detection. Both
emit the same A025 Error code; the rule definition expands to
note that it now runs in both stages.

**Why not move A025 entirely to stage 2**:

- The early-warning value is real: a JSON with an obvious cycle in
  one Kind alone is a clear authoring mistake; surfacing it before
  Composer runs gives a better diagnostic ("`kinds[0].influences`
  contains a cycle") than the stage-2 message ("composed
  influences contain a cycle: <merged graph>").
- Stage 1 cost is negligible (the same DAG check runs anyway).
- Defense in depth: even if a future Composer change preserves
  cycles, stage 1 catches the obvious cases.

**Spec changes**:
- §13.2 Mermaid restructured: stage-1 P7 relabeled "A025 cycle
  (raw, early-warning)"; new stage-2 node `P9a` for "A025 cycle
  (composed influences)".
- §13.1 A025 row text expanded to note both-stage running and the
  ghost-cycle scenario.
- New paragraph in §13.2 ("Why A025 runs in BOTH stages")
  explaining the early-warning + authoritative-gate split.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_8, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A025_GhostCycleStage2Tests.cs`:
  - `Case01_KindFearToConf_PersonaConfToFear_CycleOnlyInComposed_FailsA025Stage2`

---

## Q-S18 — Q6's "A011a covers post-composition" was structurally false (Gemini eleventh review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the decision log**:

The original Q6 resolution stated:

> Resolution: Validator emits Error A011a.
> Rationale: A persona with zero usable actions cannot drive an
> agent. The composition pass already runs before the engine
> starts, so this is catchable statically. A011 already covers
> the pre-composition case; the post-composition case is the
> same logical failure.

But A011a is defined (§13.1) as "If no `kind_ids`, the Persona
must have at least one `actions`" — it fires only when *both*
fields are missing in the raw Persona. A011b explicitly *allows*
omitting `actions` when `kind_ids` is non-empty, which is exactly
the case Gemini's scenario triggers: persona omits `actions`,
references a Kind with empty `actions[]`, both A011a and A011b
pass at stage 1, and stage 2 (post-Q-S15) had only A035. The
composed Persona reaches the Engine with `actions = []`, and
Step 5's tie-break (Q-S9: `actions.First(...)`) throws
`InvalidOperationException` on the first `Live(dt)`. Q6's claim
that A011a "covers the post-composition case too" was
**architecturally false** — A011a runs in stage 1 only.

**Resolution**: **add A036 as a stage-2 Error**. After Composer
runs, re-check that the per-Persona `actions[]` list is non-empty.
A036 is the post-composition gate that makes Q6's intent
architecturally true.

**Why a new rule, not "extend A011a to stage 2"**:

- A011a's text is specifically about the *raw* "neither field
  present" case. Re-purposing it for "composed list empty after
  merge" muddles the rule's identity — both meanings would share
  the same code, and the diagnostic message would have to branch.
- A036 has its own clear name and message ("composed actions[] is
  empty after merge"); A011a stays focused on raw input shape.
- Symmetric with A035 (also a stage-2-only post-composition rule
  with its own identity).

**Spec changes**:
- §13.1 A036 row added.
- §13 title: A000-A035 → A000-A036.
- §13.2 Mermaid: new stage-2 node `P9b` "A036 composed actions[]
  non-empty"; flow extended `Compose → P9a → P9b → P9c → Result`.
- §3.1 paragraph added documenting A036.
- Q6's decision log paragraph (§Q6 above, line 70-77) is now
  understood as "intent" rather than implementation; the actual
  enforcement lives in A036.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_8, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A036_ComposedActionsEmptyTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_PersonaOmitsActions_KindActionsEmpty_ComposedEmpty_FailsA036`
  - `Case02_PersonaHasActions_KindActionsEmpty_ComposedNonEmpty_PassesA036`

## Q-S19 — Q-S9 declaration-order tie-break vs §8.3 Kind-first append (Gemini twelfth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against the spec**:

Q-S9 (decision log §Q-S9) commits to "**declaration order wins** —
the action whose `id` appears earliest in the persona's `actions[]`
array is selected" as Step 5's tie-break, and the Phase_2_4_4
rationale explicitly defends this as "the LLM authoring the JSON
already ordered `actions[]` for human readability". §8.3 line 771
defines the actions merge unit as **"match by id, last-wins;
existing id overrides; new id appends"**, which is *Kind-first*:
the merged list starts from Kind cascade order, with the Persona's
new ids appended at the tail.

Concrete bug: a Persona that writes `actions: [Idle, Flee]`
inheriting `kinds[0]: actions: [Flee, Eat]` composes to
`[Flee, Eat, Idle]` — `Flee` overrides at the position the Kind
chose (index 0), and the LLM's intended index-0 default `Idle` is
exiled to the tail. Q-S9's tie-break then picks `Flee`, not `Idle`.
The "deterministic" promise was a lie at the architectural level:
declaration order *of what the LLM wrote* and declaration order
*of what the Engine sees* were two different things.

**Resolution**: **change §8.3's `actions` merge to Persona-first
ordering**:

1. Start from `persona.actions[]` in declared order.
2. For each Kind action whose `id` does not appear in the Persona,
   append at the tail in Kind cascade order.
3. For each Kind action whose `id` *does* appear in the Persona,
   drop the Kind copy entirely. The Persona's `Action` object is
   kept (last-wins on **value**), and its **position** is fixed by
   the Persona (Persona-first on **order**).

The `[Idle, Flee] + Kind:[Flee, Eat]` example now composes to
`[Idle, Flee, Eat]`: `Idle` stays at index 0, `Flee` is the
Persona's value at index 1, `Eat` is the Kind's contribution at
index 2. Q-S9's declaration-order tie-break finally has the input
it always assumed.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Add a `priority` field to Action and let the LLM declare
   index** — extra authoring surface; conflicts with the
   "declaration order *is* the priority" principle that Q-S9
   chose. Rejected.
2. **Change Q-S9 to "Persona's *raw* actions[] order, ignoring
   composed order"** — leaves the composed list out of sync with
   what the Engine reads at hot path; subtle bugs when designers
   inspect composed Personas. Rejected.
3. **Persona-first merge** — adopted. Symmetric with the
   `influences` change in Q-S20, and last-wins-on-value is
   preserved (which is what §8.3's "last-wins" cascade is
   primarily about — the Persona's *values* override the Kind's,
   not the *positions*).

**Spec changes**:
- §8.3 actions row rewritten with Persona-first formulation and an
  explicit before/after example.
- §3.1 paragraph added: "Composer Persona-first ordering
  (Q-S19, Q-S20)" describing the change.
- §0/header table row added: "Composer Persona-first ordering (Q-S19)".

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_9, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/PersonaFirstActionOrderTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_PersonaFirstAtIndexZero_KindOverlap_PersonaIndexZeroPreserved`
  - `Case02_PersonaFlee_KindFleeEat_ComposedIsIdleFleeEat`

**Cross-impact analysis** (the discipline Master demanded after
Q-S18):

- Q-S9 Step 5 tie-break: now reads composed `actions[0]` and gets
  what the LLM authored. Tie-break tests from Phase_2_4_5 still
  pass post-Q-S19 because their fixtures don't involve
  Kind × Persona overlap on the same id; new tests cover the
  overlap case.
- §8.3 last-wins **value** semantics: preserved exactly. Composer
  still keeps Persona's `Action` object when there's an id
  collision; only the *position* convention changed.
- ScenarioRunner determinism (§26.2): strengthened. The composed
  `actions[]` is now a function of the LLM's authored order
  alone, not the inheritance chain.
- A033 kind_ids dedup: orthogonal — that rule is about
  `kind_ids[]`, not `actions[]`. No interaction.
- Q-S14 thresholds compound key: orthogonal — different array.

## Q-S20 — Topological sort + mid-cascade clamp creates non-commutative independent edges (Gemini twelfth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by mathematical analysis against the spec**:

§9.6.2 specifies "topological sort to fix the order" + "single-pass
apply" + "Clamp [0, 100] after each Edge" (§9.6.3). Topological sort
defines a partial order from dependency edges; it does NOT define
order between independent nodes. Combined with mid-cascade clamp,
two edges that both write to the same target Need produce
order-dependent results:

```
C = 90, X = 100, Y = 100
Edges: X → C (+0.5), Y → C (-0.5)        // independent

X → Y order: 90 +50→100 (clamp) -50→ 50
Y → X order: 90 -50→ 40 (clamp) +50→ 90
```

40-unit divergence. §26.2's ScenarioRunner determinism promise
collapses if the implementer's topo-sort flavor is free to choose.

**Resolution**: **stable topological sort over composed influences[]
order**, plus the same Persona-first merge change applied to
`influences[]` (§8.3) so that "composed order" itself is
deterministic.

1. §9.6.2 step 3: "Stable topological sort: respects every
   dependency edge AND, for nodes with no relative dependency,
   preserves the composed `influences[]` order".
2. §8.3 `influences` row: change from "match by source+target,
   last-wins" to "Persona-first preserve, then append unmatched
   Kind entries" — same shape as the Q-S19 actions change.
3. New §9.6.4a paragraph "Independent-Edge Order and Non-Commutativity"
   with the worked example, the determinism table, and the
   pointer to A037.
4. New Validator A037 (Warning): two-or-more edges writing to
   the same target Need. The setup is legal and deterministic
   under Q-S20, but the LLM author may not realize the order
   matters; a Warning surfaces the non-commutative case.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Drop mid-cascade clamp** — reintroduces the v0.1.0 bug
   (§9.6.1, §9.6.3): negative middle values propagate. Rejected.
2. **Forbid multi-edge same-target outright (A037 = Error)** —
   too aggressive; many legitimate designs combine multiple
   stimuli into one Need (e.g. several stress sources increasing
   `fear`). Rejected.
3. **Stable topo sort + Persona-first merge** — adopted. Cost is
   a tiebreaker comparator on the topo sort and the same
   merge-rule change as Q-S19. Both are one-line modifications
   in Phase 3 implementation.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.6.2 step 3 rewritten with "stable" qualifier and pointer to
  composed-order source.
- §9.6.4a NEW: "Independent-Edge Order and Non-Commutativity"
  worked example + determinism table + A037 pointer.
- §8.3 `influences` row rewritten symmetric with the Q-S19
  `actions` change.
- §13.1 A037 row added.
- §13 title: A000-A036 → A000-A037.
- §3.1 paragraph for A037 added.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_9, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Composer/PersonaFirstInfluenceOrderTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_PersonaInfluenceFirst_KindAppendedSecond_OrderPreserved`
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A037_MultiEdgeSameTargetTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_TwoEdgesSameTarget_WarnsA037`
  - `Case02_OneEdgePerTarget_NoA037`

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- §9.6.3 mid-cascade clamp: preserved (the very mechanism that
  makes Q-S20 a problem is also the mechanism that prevents
  §9.6.1's negative-propagation bug; we keep the clamp and fix
  the order instead).
- A025 cycle detection (Q-S17): orthogonal. Cycle detection
  works on graph topology regardless of edge order.
- Q-S19 `actions` merge: symmetric — both `actions` and
  `influences` now use the same Persona-first rule. One mental
  model for authors.
- ScenarioRunner determinism (§26.2): restored. The LLM has
  one knob (JSON `influences[]` order) and full control.
- Engine hot path: stable topo sort costs O(n log n) at startup
  (or O(n) with Kahn's algorithm + queue iteration order keyed
  on input index). Phase 3 implementation note.

## Q-S21 — `MockScene.Tick()` calls `Update` on already-OnDestroy'd components (Gemini twelfth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by direct read of `Tests~/MiniUnity/MockScene.cs` and `MockGameObject.cs`**:

`MockScene.Tick()` (lines 62-69) snapshots `comp_snapshot` per
GameObject and iterates calling `c.Update()`. The outer loop has
`if (!obj.is_active) continue;` at the top, but the inner loop has
no guard. `MockGameObject.Destroy()` (lines 101-111) sets
`is_active = false` AND calls `OnDestroy()` on all components AND
clears the component list — synchronously. So if `CompA.Update()`
calls `Destroy(this.gameObject)`, the very next iteration of the
inner loop calls `CompB.Update()` even though `CompB.OnDestroy()`
just ran and freed its resources. Unity's contract: a GameObject
destroyed mid-frame stops receiving `Update` on its remaining
components for the rest of that frame. MockScene was a
**Unity-lifecycle violation**.

**Resolution**: **add `if (!obj.is_active) break;` at the top of
the inner loop** in `MockScene.Tick()`. One line. Mirrors Unity's
behavior: once destroyed, no more Update for the remaining
components this frame.

```csharp
foreach (MockMonoBehaviour c in comp_snapshot) {
    if (!obj.is_active) break;   // Q-S21
    c.Update();
}
```

**Why not other options**:

1. **Re-snapshot the component list each iteration** — slower
   and misses the point: the bug isn't that components were
   added/removed during iteration (the snapshot already protects
   against that), the bug is that the GameObject itself was
   destroyed. Rejected.
2. **Remove the outer `if (!obj.is_active) continue` and rely on
   per-component checks only** — `MockMonoBehaviour` has no
   `is_active`/`enabled` field, so per-component checks aren't
   available without expanding the mock surface. Rejected.
3. **Add `MockMonoBehaviour.is_active` and check that** —
   premature scope expansion; Unity's `MonoBehaviour.enabled`
   has more semantics than just "alive". Rejected for v0.1.5.
4. **`if (!obj.is_active) break;` inside inner loop** —
   adopted. Surgical, matches Unity's contract.

**Spec changes**:
- None to `docs/animo_spec_*.md`. This is a test-harness fix.
- README v0.1.5 section gains a sentence noting the Q-S21 fix.
- decision log (this entry) records the rationale.

**Code changes**:
- `Tests~/MiniUnity/MockScene.cs`: one `break;` line added with
  comment block explaining the Unity-lifecycle contract.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_9, MiniUnity self-tests, GREEN):

- `Tests~/MiniUnity.SelfTests/MockSceneZombieUpdateTest.cs`:
  - `Case01_DestroyDuringUpdate_LaterComponentsDoNotReceiveUpdate`

This goes into MiniUnity self-tests (Green) rather than
EditModeTests (Red baseline) because it asserts on the test
harness itself, not on Animo Engine logic.

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Existing 4 MiniUnity self-tests: no impact (none of them
  destroy GameObjects during iteration).
- Phase 3 Agent + Engine integration tests: all benefit; once
  Engine is implemented and tests destroy Agent GameObjects
  mid-tick, the harness no longer crashes.
- Animo `Scripts/`: zero impact. The fix is in the test harness,
  not the library.
- Q-S6 (Store duplicate Register): orthogonal. Different
  subsystem.

---

## Q-S22 — Q-S6 left a symmetric hole on `Store.Unregister` (Gemini thirteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against §11.2 + decision log Q-S6**:

§11.2 specifies Unregister behavior only as "if `agent_id` not
found at Unregister: Warning + no-op". No instance-equality
contract. Q-S6 chose "keep first on duplicate Register": Agent B
(same agent_id, different instance from Agent A which won the
Register race) is rejected at Register but still lives in the
scene. When Unity destroys B, B.OnDestroy calls
Store.Unregister(B), and a naive `_agents.Remove(agent.agent_id)`
removes the entry that points to the still-running Agent A.
Symmetric hole: Q-S6 protected entry, Q-S6 left exit unguarded.

**Resolution**: `Unregister(agent)` checks
`ReferenceEquals(_agents[id], agent)` before removing. Different
instance ⇒ Warning + no-op; original keeps its registration.

```csharp
public void Unregister(Animo.Agent agent) {
    if (_agents.TryGetValue(agent.agent_id, out var existing)) {
        if (ReferenceEquals(existing, agent)) {
            _agents.Remove(agent.agent_id);
        } else {
            AnimoLog.Warning(/* duplicate's exit attempt */);
        }
    } else {
        AnimoLog.Warning(/* not registered */);
    }
}
```

**Why not other options**:

1. **Always remove by agent_id** — current spec, the bug. Rejected.
2. **Pass agent_id (not agent) to Unregister and rely on
   convention** — moves the burden to callers; one mistaken caller
   re-introduces the bug. Rejected.
3. **Store maintains a reverse map of (instance → id) and removes
   only valid pairs** — same effect as ReferenceEquals check but
   adds memory overhead and a second source of truth. Rejected.
4. **ReferenceEquals check before remove** — adopted. One condition,
   matches Q-S6's "keep first" semantics symmetrically.

**Spec changes**:
- §11.2 Specs table gains a Q-S22 row for the instance-mismatch case.
- §11.2.2 NEW: "Why instance-equality check on Unregister" with
  the worked scenario, the code template, and the symmetry note.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_10, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Store/StoreUnregisterInstanceCheckTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_DuplicateRejectedThenItsDestroyDoesNotEvictOriginal`
  - `Case02_SameInstance_UnregisterRemovesEntry`

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Q-S6 (Register keep-first): symmetric — both rules now anchor
  on the same "same instance check" semantics.
- §11.2 Affect Warning policy: orthogonal, unchanged.
- Q-S22 only affects the `Unregister` code path; `Affect` and
  `Find` still treat the dictionary as authoritative.
- Phase 3 `Agent.OnDestroy` callsite: unchanged — calls
  `Unregister(this)`, which is now safe regardless of duplicate
  status.

## Q-S23 — Threshold `_needs` / `_effective_needs` disconnect (Gemini thirteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against §16.5 + §16.6 + §9.6.5**:

§16.6 row for `Threshold.need_index` says "needs `_needs[]` in
threshold check". The §16.5 Step3_Thresholds sample reads
`_previous_needs[t.need_index]`. Meanwhile §9.6.5 line 1223 is
`eff.Set(inf.target, ...)` — Influence cascade writes only to
`_effective_needs`, never touches `_needs`. Concrete §25.5.3-style
scenario: `Affect("frustration", +40)` ⇒ frame N+1 Step 2 cascades
into `_effective_needs[anger] = some large value` ⇒ Step 3 reads
`_previous_needs[anger]` (= 0, untouched by cascade) and
`_needs[anger]` (also 0) ⇒ no rise detected ⇒ no Bus signal. But
Step 4's score uses `_effective_needs[action.need]` (line 2278) ⇒
the Action layer sees the cascade and switches behavior. The
Action layer and the Threshold/Bus layer disagree on what Need
state to observe.

**Resolution**: **switch Threshold to `_effective_needs` baseline**.

- Rename Engine field `_previous_needs` → `_previous_effective_needs`
  (purpose change, not just a name change).
- Engine ctor seeds `_previous_effective_needs` by running one
  Step 2 pass over spawn Needs (extends Q-S8 — was just copying
  `_needs`, now produces the same value Step 2 would compute on
  first frame).
- Step 3 compares `_effective_needs` against
  `_previous_effective_needs`.
- End of Step 3: snapshot `_effective_needs` into
  `_previous_effective_needs` for the next frame.

The Action layer (Step 4) and the Threshold layer (Step 3) now
share the same observation surface. Cascade-driven emotional
shifts publish Bus signals as designed in §25.5.3.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Keep Threshold on `_needs`, document the split** — leaves the
   §25.5.3 design pattern unimplementable. Rejected.
2. **Add a *second* Threshold variant that reads `_effective_needs`
   while the original keeps reading `_needs`** — doubles the
   authoring surface and the state arrays. Rejected.
3. **Make `_needs` and `_effective_needs` the same array (drop
   the distinction)** — destroys §9.6 (Influence is meant to be
   non-destructive on the base Need; only effective is amplified).
   Rejected.
4. **Switch Threshold to `_effective_needs`** — adopted.

**Spec changes**:
- §16.5 Step3_Thresholds sample updated to use
  `_previous_effective_needs` and `_effective_needs`, with comment
  block explaining the Q-S23 fix and the §25.5.3 pattern.
- §16.6 affected-classes table:
  - `Engine._previous_needs` row → `_previous_effective_needs`,
    description rewritten with Q-S23 + Q-S8 combined seeding rule.
  - `Threshold.need_index` row description: "needs `_needs[]`" →
    "needs `_effective_needs[]` and `_previous_effective_needs[]`".
- §16.2.2 mermaid sequence: `previous_needs` → `previous_effective_needs`.
- §9.2 Step 3 paragraph + Mermaid label updated.
- Engine init mermaid sequence (line 1473) updated.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_10, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/Step3_ThresholdEffectiveNeedsTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_InfluenceCascadeRaisesEffective_TriggerFires`
    (Affect frustration → cascade lifts eff_anger over trigger →
    Threshold fires; pre-Q-S23 this didn't fire)

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Q-S8 (`_previous_needs` seeding): EXTENDED to seed
  `_previous_effective_needs` through one Step 2 pass. The
  spawn-time spurious-rise prevention is preserved.
- Q-S14 (Threshold expanded_trigger): orthogonal, untouched.
- Q-S15 / A035 (post-fill `trigger > reset`): orthogonal — A035
  validates the threshold pair; Q-S23 changes which Need value
  the threshold *compares against*. Independent.
- §16.1 Zero-allocation Hot Path: same number of float[] arrays
  (renamed); no new allocation per frame.
- §25.5.3 ときメモ系 design pattern: now implementable as
  documented (was secretly unimplementable pre-Q-S23).
- Action score (§9.3.4): already on `_effective_needs`; no change,
  but the Action and Threshold layers now agree on observation.
