## Q-S24 — Need-level topological sort silently violated Q-S20 (Gemini thirteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against §9.6.2**:

§9.6.2 step 1 says "Build dependency graph (DAG) from `influences`
`source → target`". This is a Need (Node) graph: each `Influence`
becomes a directed edge in a graph whose vertices are Needs. A
topological sort over this graph returns an order on **Needs**, not
on **Edges**. To apply edges in the resulting order, an
implementation iterates Needs and, for each Need, applies all edges
with that Need as source. Edges sharing a `source` are bundled,
and edges with different `source`s interleave per the Need order —
the LLM's `influences[]` array order across different sources is
silently violated. Q-S20 promised the array order was the
determinism key for independent edges; that promise is unkeepable
under a Need-level sort.

Concrete: composed `influences[] = [X→A, Y→B, X→C]`. Need topo
sort returns some ordering of {X, Y, A, B, C}; processing X first
applies both X→A and X→C together, processing Y applies Y→B. The
visible apply order is `[X→A, X→C, Y→B]` or `[Y→B, X→A, X→C]`
depending on whether X or Y comes first in the Need order — but
the LLM wrote `[X→A, Y→B, X→C]`, expecting Y→B between X→A and X→C.

**Resolution**: **build the EDGE dependency graph instead**.

- Vertices: each composed `Influence` (Edge).
- Partial-order constraints: `e1 ≺ e2` iff `e1.target == e2.source`
  (e1 writes the Need that e2 reads; e1 must run first).
- Stable topological sort over edges; ties (independent edges)
  fall back to composed `influences[]` order — Persona-first
  per Q-S19/S20.

This makes Q-S20's "array order is the determinism key" actually
implementable. Each independent edge keeps its position from the
LLM's authored sequence.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Keep Need-level sort, document the bundling** — abandons
   Q-S20's array-order promise and re-opens the §26.2 determinism
   gap that Q-S20 was supposed to close. Rejected.
2. **Edge-level sort with `e1 ≺ e2` iff
   `e1.source == e2.target` (reversed)** — that's wrong: e1 reads
   what e2 writes ⇒ e1 needs the *pre-update* value, so e1 should
   run *before* e2 only if e1's read-target hasn't been written
   yet. The correct constraint is `e1 writes what e2 reads` ⇒
   e1 runs first.
3. **Edge-level sort, `e1 ≺ e2` iff
   `e1.target == e2.source`** — adopted.

**Cycle equivalence note**: An edge-level cycle (e1 ≺ e2 ≺ ... ≺ e1)
is mathematically equivalent to a Need-level cycle in the Need
graph. So A025 (cycle detection in stage 1 + stage 2 per Q-S17)
keeps detecting the same cycles regardless of which formulation
the Validator uses internally. The change in §9.6.2 is purely
about the *sort* output, not about *whether* a cycle exists.

**Spec changes**:
- §9.6.2 step 1 rewritten: "Build the EDGE dependency graph" with
  the constraint `e1 ≺ e2 iff e1.target == e2.source`, plus
  explicit "this is NOT the same as the Need dependency graph"
  paragraph and cycle equivalence note.
- §9.6.2 Mermaid: nodes relabeled to "edge dependency graph",
  "edge stable topological sort".
- §9.6.4a "Resolution (Q-S20 + Q-S24)" paragraph adds the edge
  reformulation alongside the stable-sort claim.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_10, Red baseline):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/Step2_EdgeOrderTests.cs`:
  - `Case01_IndependentEdgesPreserveArrayOrder_AcrossDifferentSources`
    (composed [X→A, Y→B, X→C] applies in exactly that order;
    pre-Q-S24 a Need sort would bundle X's edges)

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Q-S17 / A025 cycle detection: cycle equivalence preserved;
  stage-1 + stage-2 detection unchanged.
- A037 (multi-edge same target Warning): independent — A037 is
  about *whether* multiple edges write to one target, not about
  *order*. Same firing condition.
- Q-S19 actions Persona-first: orthogonal — separate array.
- Q-S20 Persona-first influences merge: PRESERVED and now
  actually used as the tie-break key (which it could not be
  under the Need-level sort).
- §9.6.3 mid-cascade Clamp: preserved.
- §26.2 ScenarioRunner determinism: now implementable as
  promised.

## Q-S25 — Threshold hysteresis has no state storage (Gemini fourteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against §12.3.2 + Data.cs + Engine.cs**:

§12.3.2 specifies a state machine with two states (Below / Above)
and four transitions. State machines with two states need 1 bit of
memory per Threshold. Reading `Scripts/Data.cs:96-109`:
`Threshold` has `need`, `trigger_threshold`, `reset_threshold`,
`trigger`, `need_index`, `expanded_trigger` — no `is_above` or
equivalent state field. Reading `Scripts/Engine.cs`: no
`_threshold_states` array. The state machine has no storage at all.

A naive implementation that uses `prev < trigger && curr >= trigger`
cross-detection chatters: a Need oscillating between 75 and 85 with
`trigger=80, reset=70` fires on every up-cross, never reaching
`reset=70` to re-arm. `reset_threshold` becomes dead code. A023's
"trigger > reset" guarantee, Q-S11's reset floor, and A035's
post-fill `trigger > reset` re-check all become decorative.
§12.3.1's old chattering bug returns through the back door despite
the entire §12.3 chapter being written to prevent it.

**Resolution**: **add `internal bool is_above` to `Threshold` and
implement the state machine in Step 3**.

```csharp
// Data.cs Threshold class (Q-S25 addition)
internal bool is_above;
```

Step 3 reads + writes per the §12.3.2 mermaid:

| Branch | Condition | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Below + cross-up | `!is_above && curr >= trigger_threshold` | `is_above = true`; emit OnSignal(expanded_trigger) |
| Below + low | `!is_above && curr < trigger_threshold` | no-op |
| Above + cross-down | `is_above && curr <= effective_reset_threshold` | `is_above = false`; **no fire** |
| Above + high | `is_above && curr > effective_reset_threshold` | no-op (suppress duplicate fires) |

`is_above` is seeded in Engine ctor by reading the spawn-time
`_effective_needs` (the same single Step 2 pass that seeds
`_previous_effective_needs` per Q-S8 + Q-S23). A Persona spawned
with `fear: 80` and a `fear: trigger=70` Threshold starts in
`Above` and does NOT fire on first Live(dt) — matching Q-S8's
"no spurious first-frame fires" goal.

**Why not other options**:

1. **`Engine._threshold_states` `bool[]` keyed by per-Persona index**
   — separates state from data, requires another array allocated in
   ctor. More indirection for no benefit. Rejected.
2. **Encode state in `_previous_effective_needs` (e.g. negate the
   value when Above)** — overloads the float[] semantics; breaks
   sign assumptions; fragile. Rejected.
3. **Per-Threshold `internal bool is_above`** — adopted. Same
   pattern as Q-S14's `expanded_trigger` (per-instance internal
   field on Threshold). Symmetric; no extra allocation; deep-copied
   along with the rest of Threshold by Composer.

**Spec changes**:
- §12.3.2.1 NEW: "Implementation Contract: 1-bit State Per
  Threshold" with the four-branch table, the seeding rule, and
  the explicit warning about chattering without state.
- §16.5 Step3_Thresholds sample rewritten with the real state
  machine (replaces the `// ... fire / reset state machine using ...`
  placeholder).
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Threshold` gains `internal bool is_above;`.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_11):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/ThresholdHysteresisStateTests.cs`
  (NEW, 1 case): chattering case — Need oscillating 75↔85 with
  `trigger=80, reset=70` must fire ONCE on first crossing, then
  stay silent until value drops to ≤ 70 and re-arms.

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Q-S8 / Q-S23 seeding: EXTENDED — same Engine ctor that seeds
  `_previous_effective_needs` also seeds `Threshold.is_above`.
- Q-S14 `expanded_trigger`: orthogonal — different field on the
  same Threshold instance. Both per-instance.
- A023 / Q-S11 / A035 `trigger > reset`: now meaningful for the
  first time. Pre-Q-S25 these guarantees protected a state that
  didn't exist; post-Q-S25 they protect the actual hysteresis.
- §11.4.2 Composer deep copy: `Threshold.is_above` is copied
  along with the rest. Spawn-time ctor seed overrides the copied
  value (which is `false` by default).
- §16.1 zero-allocation hot path: 1 byte per Threshold (bool,
  packed by CLR layout). Negligible.

## Q-S26 — Engine has no output channel for Threshold fires (Gemini fourteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against §12.1 + Engine.cs**:

§12.1 explicitly says: "**The `Agent` (MonoBehaviour) holds the
`Bus` reference via Inspector. Neither `Store` nor `Engine` holds
it.**" Reading `Scripts/Engine.cs` (69 lines): no `event`, no
`Action<string>`, no callback `Func`, no `IBusListener` interface
— Engine has no way to emit Threshold fires to anything outside
itself. Yet §16.5's sample showed
`_bus.Publish(signal_id: t.expanded_trigger)` *inside* Engine's
Step 3. The sample called a `_bus` field that the spec
simultaneously declared could not exist. Threshold fires were
architecturally trapped inside Engine; the LLM's authoring
contract ("set `trigger` and `trigger_event`, get a Bus signal")
was unimplementable.

**Resolution**: **add `public event Action<string>? OnSignal` to
Engine**. Step 3 (Threshold fire) and Step 4 / Step 5 (behavior
change) raise it via `OnSignal?.Invoke(signal_id)` (or via a
protected `RaiseSignal(string)` helper for testability). Agent
subscribes once in `Awake`:

```csharp
void Awake() {
    _engine = new Engine(persona: _composed_persona);
    _engine.OnSignal += signal_id => _bus?.Publish(signal_id: signal_id);
    Animo.Store.Instance.Register(agent: this);
}
```

Engine stays pure-C# (no Bus dependency). Agent is the only
layer that knows about `Germio.Bus`. Tests can subscribe a
`MockBus`-style listener directly to `engine.OnSignal` without
touching Bus / Agent at all.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Add `IBusInterface` and pass it into Engine ctor** — couples
   Engine to a Bus contract; if Germio's Bus shape ever changes
   (or another host wants a different Bus), Engine has to follow.
   Event is decoupled. Rejected.
2. **Have Agent poll Engine for "fired this frame" each tick** —
   wastes CPU per frame even when no fire happened; loses
   ordering when multiple fires happen in one tick. Rejected.
3. **`public event Action<string>? OnSignal`** — adopted. Standard
   .NET pattern; zero coupling; tests trivially subscribe; payload
   is just `string` (the cached `expanded_trigger` /
   `expanded_action_change`).

**Spec changes**:
- §12.1 Bus Reference: prose updated to describe OnSignal; mermaid
  edge "Engine — behavior change → Agent" relabeled "Engine —
  OnSignal event (Q-S26) → Agent"; "Agent → Bus" relabeled
  "Bus.Publish(signal_id)".
- §12.1.1 NEW: "Why Engine raises an event instead of calling
  Bus directly".
- §16.5 sample: both `OnBehaviorChanged` and `Step3_Thresholds`
  now use `RaiseSignal(...)` instead of the architecturally
  impossible `_bus.Publish(...)`.
- `Scripts/Engine.cs`: `public event Action<string>? OnSignal;`
  added; `protected void RaiseSignal(string signal_id)` helper
  added with comment block.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_11, GREEN):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/EngineOnSignalEventTests.cs`
  (NEW, 1 case): subscribe a lambda to `engine.OnSignal`,
  assert it can be subscribed and unsubscribed without throwing.
  This passes in Phase_2_4_11 because `OnSignal` is the only
  thing Q-S26 actually adds at the API level (no implementation
  required).

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- §12.1 contract preserved (Engine has no Bus reference; Agent
  is sole Bus-aware layer). The change is additive.
- §12.2 on_action_change: now also routes through OnSignal,
  unifying the fire mechanism for both Threshold and behavior
  change.
- Q-S22 Store.Unregister: orthogonal — Store doesn't subscribe
  to Engine events.
- Q-S6 Affect: orthogonal — Affect is input, OnSignal is output.
- Q-S25 hysteresis state: integrated — Step 3's state machine
  decides whether to fire; if yes, OnSignal carries the payload.
- §16.1 zero-allocation hot path: `OnSignal?.Invoke(s)` allocates
  nothing on the hot path (event is multicast delegate, payload
  is a cached string from `expanded_trigger`).

---

## Q-S27 — Q-S16 static indices vs Engine dynamic array (Gemini fourteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Gemini's claim, verified by grep against Const.cs + §16.2.2**:

`Const.cs:19` defines `NEED_INDEX_FEAR = 2` and
`Const.cs:24` defines `NEED_INDEX_FRUSTRATION = 7`.
`Const.cs:48-50` `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[2] = [NEED_INDEX_FEAR,
NEED_INDEX_FRUSTRATION]` reads from those constants. §9.3.4's
`max_lower_tier_intensity` formula iterates `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER`
and accesses `_effective_needs[idx]` for each entry.

But §16.2.2's sequence diagram (line 2329) shows Engine ctor
doing "register all Need keys with int index" by iterating the
Persona's Needs dictionary — the example
`{ "hunger": 0, "fear": 2, "frustration": 7, "jealousy": 8 }`
just happens to match the Const indices because all standard
Needs were declared in that order. **The diagram is an example,
not a contract.** A "peaceful villager" Persona declaring only
`{ idle: 30, curiosity: 20, confidence: 50 }` (no fear, no
frustration) gets `_effective_needs.Length = 3` and an index
assignment like `{ idle: 0, curiosity: 1, confidence: 2 }`. Now
`_effective_needs[NEED_INDEX_FEAR=2]` is `confidence`'s value
(cross-Need misread in Maslow tier-2 suppression — confidence
masquerading as fear suppresses tier 3+) and
`_effective_needs[NEED_INDEX_FRUSTRATION=7]` is an
`IndexOutOfRangeException` waiting to fire on the first frame
that touches tier-2 suppression.

**Resolution**: **reserve fixed slots `0..STANDARD_NEEDS.Length-1`
(= 0..7) for the eight standard Needs in every Engine,
regardless of what the Persona declares**. Non-standard Needs
append at index ≥ 8.

```csharp
// Engine ctor (Q-S27)
_need_index = new Dictionary<string, int>();
int extra = _persona.needs.Keys
    .Where(k => Array.IndexOf(Const.STANDARD_NEEDS, k) < 0)
    .Count();
_effective_needs = new float[Const.STANDARD_NEEDS.Length + extra];

// Step 1: reserve standard slots (always present, always 0 if Persona omitted)
for (int i = 0; i < Const.STANDARD_NEEDS.Length; i++) {
    _need_index[Const.STANDARD_NEEDS[i]] = i;
}
// Step 2: append non-standard slots
int next = Const.STANDARD_NEEDS.Length;
foreach (var kv in _persona.needs) {
    if (!_need_index.ContainsKey(kv.Key)) {
        _need_index[kv.Key] = next++;
    }
}
// Step 3: fill values (overwrite the 0 default for any standard
// Need the Persona did declare).
foreach (var kv in _persona.needs) {
    _needs[_need_index[kv.Key]] = kv.Value;
}
```

After this:
- `_effective_needs[NEED_INDEX_FEAR=2]` is **always** the fear
  value (or 0 if Persona omitted fear).
- `NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER` always reads from a guaranteed-existing
  slot — no `IndexOutOfRangeException`, no cross-Need misread.
- Memory cost: 96 bytes per Engine for the pre-reserved slots.
  Negligible at thousands-of-agents scale.

**Why not other options**:

1. **Make Const indices dynamic per Persona** — defeats the entire
   point of Q-S16's static map (whose purpose was zero-lookup
   hot-path access). Rejected.
2. **Throw at ctor if Persona omits any standard Need** — too
   aggressive; many valid designs use a subset. A peaceful
   villager doesn't need a `fear` Action even if `fear` should
   stay reserved-but-zero in the array. Rejected.
3. **Reserve standard slots; non-standard append after** —
   adopted. Tiny memory cost; Q-S16 finally safe.

**Spec changes**:
- §16.2.2 sequence diagram updated: explicit two-step assignment
  (standard slots first, non-standard append).
- §16.2.2.1 NEW: "Standard-Need fixed-slot reservation (Q-S27)"
  with the worked code template, the safety guarantees, and the
  memory-overhead note.
- §3.1 paragraph + header Theme table row.

**Tests added** (Phase_2_4_11):

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/StandardNeedFixedSlotTests.cs`
  (NEW, 1 case): a Persona declaring only non-standard subset
  (e.g. `{idle, curiosity, confidence}`) must still produce an
  Engine where reading `GetNeed("fear")` returns 0 (slot exists,
  default value). Phase 3 implementation will pass this when
  Q-S27 is implemented in ctor.

**Cross-impact analysis**:

- Q-S16 NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER: now safe to use without bounds
  checking. The whole reason for Q-S16 was zero-lookup hot path;
  Q-S27 makes that hot path actually correct.
- A019 Unknown Need Warning: orthogonal — A019 fires for
  Persona Need names not in STANDARD_NEEDS; Q-S27 just decides
  where they live in the array.
- §9.3.4 Maslow suppression: now always safe.
- Q-S8 / Q-S23 / Q-S25 seeding: spawn-time loops over
  `_effective_needs.Length` continue to work; Persona-omitted
  standard Needs are seeded at 0 (which is the correct default).
- §16.6 affected-classes table: implicitly updated by §16.2.2.1.
- Existing NeedTierMapTests (Q-S16): unchanged — they assert on
  Const data only, not on Engine slot assignment.

## Q-S28 — Prefab spawn × JSON-fixed agent_id × Q-S6 keep-first ⇒ 99/100 zombies (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1571` `Register on | Agent.Awake`
- `EN.md:1574-1577` Q-S6 "keep first on duplicate Register" + Q-S22 instance check
- No `Agent.Awake` template-id-vs-runtime-id distinction anywhere in pre-Q-S28 spec.

**Concrete bug**: 100 goblins from one prefab → 100 `Agent.Awake` calls
→ all use JSON `agent_id = "goblin_scout_01"` → Q-S6 keep-first
rejects 99 → 99 Bus-disconnected zombies.

**Resolution**: JSON `agent_id` is a **template id**. `Agent.Awake`
overrides with `$"{template_id}_{GetInstanceID()}"` BEFORE
`Store.Register`. Override happens at host-adapter layer (Agent in
Unity, ScenarioRunner in tests).

**Why not other options**:

1. Composer-side override using a counter — Composer doesn't know
   about Unity InstanceID; not all hosts are Unity.
2. Make `Store.Register` auto-suffix on collision — silent
   uniqueness obscures the spawn count and makes Bus payloads
   unpredictable. Rejected.
3. **Host-adapter override** — adopted. Engine stays content-
   agnostic; each host picks its own uniqueness scheme.

**A002 scope clarification**: A002 (snake_case) applies at
**JSON authoring time only**. Runtime IDs may contain numeric
suffixes (`goblin_scout_47291`). Pinned in §11.4.2.1.

**Cross-impact**:
- Q-S6 / Q-S22: untouched. Each Agent now Registers a unique id.
- `_cached_action_triggers` `{agent_id}` expansion: must run AFTER
  override, so Bus payloads carry the runtime id. Pinned in
  §11.4.1 step 5.
- Q-S29 (PersonaCache): the cache is keyed on TEMPLATE id; all
  100 goblins share one cached entry.

---

## Q-S29 — Awake runs Validator + Composer per Agent (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- §6.3 Task 4-1-c: "Awake loads JSON, runs Validator, runs
  Composer, builds Engine".
- 100 spawn ⇒ 100× full validation including A025 stage 1 + 2.

**Resolution**: introduce `Animo.PersonaCache` (Flyweight). Validator
runs once on Root at `Initialize`; Composer runs once per template
id at first `GetComposed`. Stage-2 rules (A025/A035/A036/A037) run
inside `GetComposed` and are merged into the Initialize-time
`ValidationResult`.

**Why not other options**:

1. Skip Validator at runtime entirely (validate offline only) —
   loses the crash-on-load safety net for hand-edited JSON. Rejected.
2. Cache only the parsed Root, re-run Validate per Agent — Validator
   is the heaviest step (cycle detection); caching it saves more
   than caching parse alone. Rejected.
3. **Validate-once + Compose-per-template-once** — adopted. 96-byte
   memory cost per template; cycle detection cost amortized.

**Bootstrapper pattern (§11.6.5)**: a `MonoBehaviour` with
`[DefaultExecutionOrder(-1000)]` calls `Initialize` before any
Agent's Awake. `ScenarioRunner` ctor calls `Initialize` internally.
Calling `GetComposed` before `Initialize` throws fail-loud per
Master's policy.

**Cross-impact**:
- Q-S15/Q-S17/Q-S18: stage-2 rules now run inside
  `PersonaCache.GetComposed`. Adds `ValidationResult.Merge` API.
- Q-S28: cache keyed on template id; runtime override happens
  AFTER `GetComposed`.
- Phase 3 hot path: untouched.

## Q-S30 — Q-S16 Maslow exclusion contradicts §20.4 genre-agnostic (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- §20.4 line 2965: "needs keys are free / genre custom: longing/jealousy".
- Q-S16 (line 191): "Non-standard Needs (A019) are excluded from
  suppression".
- Direct contradiction between two sections of the same v0.1.5 spec.

**Concrete bug**: survival game declaring `oxygen` tier-1 → can't
suppress higher-tier Actions → NPC suffocates while exploring.

**Resolution**: introduce optional `needs_meta` Persona/Kind field.
Author declares per-Persona tier for non-standard Needs:
`{ "oxygen": { "tier": 1 } }`. Engine ctor builds per-Persona
`_need_tier_indices` extending `Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER`. New
rule **A038** validates declared tiers.

**Why opt-in, not auto-default**:
- A non-standard Need's tier is genuinely unknown to Animo
  (`jealousy` could be tier 2 or tier 4).
- Auto-default risks Q-S16's worry: hunger-like Need silently
  excluded from suppression.
- Opt-in is honest.

**Standard-Need override behavior**: `needs_meta` entry for a
standard Need with a tier disagreeing with §3.5 ⇒ A038 Warning;
**§3.5 still wins at runtime** (the Engine ctor skips standard
Needs in the meta loop). Why: standard Need tier table is the
shared library convention; per-Persona override there would
violate cross-Persona Maslow consistency.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2: new section with worked Engine ctor code.
- §8.3 merge table: new `needs_meta` Dictionary row (last-wins per
  key).
- §13.1: new A038 row (Error/Warning).
- §13 title: A000-A037 → A000-A038.
- §16.6 affected_classes: new `_need_tier_indices` row.
- Q-S27 fixed slots: standard Need slots 0..7 still reserved;
  `needs_meta` extends only the tier-membership map, not the
  array layout.
- A019: stays silent for non-standard Needs that appear in
  `needs_meta` (the author has explicitly declared them).

## Q-S31 — Spawn-time `""` → actions[0] OnSignal storm (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Engine.cs:43` `behavior` property doc: "Empty before the first
  Live()".
- §16.5 sample (pre-Q-S31): `OnBehaviorChanged(string new_behavior)`
  with no previous-state check.
- Q-S26: Step 4/5 raise OnSignal on every behavior change.

**Concrete bug**: 100 NPCs spawn → frame 1 Step 5 picks `actions[0]`
for each (Q-S9 tie-break) → 100 simultaneous `animo_*_idle`
signals to Bus.

**Resolution**: silent first-transition contract. `OnBehaviorChanged`
takes both `previous_behavior` and `new_behavior`; if
`previous_behavior == ""` return without raising OnSignal.

**Why this signature, not a separate `OnFirstBehavior` event**:
- One method = one mental model for tests / Phase 3.
- `previous == ""` is a unique sentinel: it can only happen on
  Engine's literal first Step 5.
- Symmetric with how Q-S8/Q-S25 handle Threshold first-frame
  silence.

**Cross-impact**:
- §16.6 affected_classes: new Engine `_previous_behavior` field
  (defaults to `""`).
- §9.2 Step 5 mermaid: note added.
- Q-S26 OnSignal: same channel; just gated.
- Threshold (Q-S25) is independently silent on frame 1 by Q-S8 +
  Q-S25 seeding; both layers unified.

## Q-S32 — ScenarioRunner has no Engine state-readout API (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Engine.cs` only public read API: `GetNeed(string)`, `behavior`,
  `is_locked`, `locked_behavior`.
- §26.3 `TraceFrame` declares `effective_needs` and `action_scores`
  as `Dictionary<string, float>` — Engine has no API to populate.

**Concrete bug**: ScenarioRunner can't fill `TraceFrame.action_scores`
or `effective_needs` without reflection-into-private-state.

**Resolution**: 4 `internal` accessors on Engine, gated by
`InternalsVisibleTo("Animo.Tools")` declared in
`Scripts/AssemblyInfo.cs`:

```csharp
internal float GetEffectiveNeed(string need);
internal float GetActionScore(string action_id);
internal IReadOnlyList<string> GetAllNeedNames();
internal IReadOnlyList<string> GetAllActionIds();
```

**Why internal, not public**:
- `GetActionScore` returns the latest computed score; calling it
  per frame from non-trace code would invite misuse.
- Cold-path semantics are explicit by the `internal` access modifier.
- `Animo.Tools.ScenarioRunner` is the only intended consumer in
  v0.1.5; Phase 3 may add more.

**Cross-impact**:
- `Scripts/AssemblyInfo.cs`: new file with `InternalsVisibleTo`.
- §13.2.1 Validator API: surface stays public; only Engine
  accessors are internal.
- §16.1 zero-allocation hot path: untouched (these are cold-path).
- Phase 2_5 asmdef polish: AssemblyInfo.cs is part of the
  `Animo` assembly.

## Q-S33 — Runner loop drops `time == duration` events (Gemini fifteenth review, 2026-05-09)

**Verified by grep**:
- §26.3.1 (line 3879 pre-Q-S33): `while (current_time < duration)`.
- Q-S4: list + forward pointer.
- IEEE-754 accumulation: `0.1f × 100` ≠ `10.0f`.

**Concrete bug**: event at `time == duration` never reaches the
inner consumption loop; outer loop exits before final consumption.

**Resolution**: outer condition `<= duration + EPSILON`, inner
`>= time - EPSILON`, EPSILON = `1e-4f`.

**Why 1e-4f**:
- 0.1ms tolerance is well below typical game frame intervals
  (16.67ms for 60fps, 33.33ms for 30fps).
- Larger ε risks early-firing future events.
- Smaller ε risks IEEE-754 drift catching up after long sims.

**Cross-impact**:
- Q-S4 List + forward pointer: untouched.
- Same-time event ordering: preserved (forward pointer still
  consumes in caller-specified order).
- §26.4.1 example (`time: 10.0f, duration: 30.0f`): unaffected
  (event time < duration).
- Q-S5 force_reset OR-latch: orthogonal; Affect path is
  independent of loop boundary.

## Q-S34 — Q-S31 silent contract starves the host's Animator/View (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:206` Q-S31 silent-first-transition contract.
- `Engine.cs:43` `behavior` property starts as `""`.
- §11.4 sequence Mermaid (pre-Q-S34) had no Animator-update step.

**Concrete bug**: 100 NPCs spawn → Q-S31 suppresses 100 OnSignal
init signals, but the host has no other channel to know what
initial Action to play → all 100 characters T-pose until the
second behavior change.

**Resolution**: `Agent.Awake` calls `_engine.Live(dt: 0.0f)` to
seed the initial decision and reads `_engine.behavior` directly
to set the host's Animator state. Q-S31 still applies for OnSignal;
Q-S34 adds the parallel non-Bus View path.

Step 6 of Awake (added by Q-S34):
```csharp
_engine.Live(dt: 0.0f);                       // seed initial behavior
_animator?.Play(stateName: _engine.behavior); // direct push, no Bus
```

`Live(dt: 0.0f)` is safe: Step 1 (decay) is multiplicative-by-dt
so dt=0 is a no-op for Needs; Steps 2-5 still run. Threshold seeding
(Q-S8/Q-S25) ensures no spurious fire.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make Q-S31 conditionally raise OnSignal (host opt-in to
   first-transition signals) — defeats the storm-prevention point;
   100 spawning NPCs would still flood Bus. Rejected.
2. Have Engine push behavior directly to a host-provided
   `IAnimatorAdapter` interface — couples Engine to Unity's
   Animator concept; breaks Engine's pure-C# stance. Rejected.
3. **Awake calls Live(0.0f) + reads behavior** — adopted. Engine
   stays pure C#; Unity-specific code (Animator.Play) lives on
   the Unity side; storm prevention preserved.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4 sequence Mermaid: new step "Live(0.0f) → behavior =
  actions[0] → Animator.Play".
- §11.4.1 code template: step (6) added.
- Q-S31 OnSignal silence: preserved; the OnSignal channel and
  the View channel are now correctly orthogonal.
- Engine.behavior property doc: clarified that Awake's Live(0.0f)
  is what seeds it.

## Q-S35 — Q-S33 EPSILON loop over-shoots when duration is an exact multiple of dt (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- §26.3.1 (Q-S33 form): `while (current_time <= duration + EPSILON)`.
- duration=10.0, dt=0.1: at iteration 100 `current_time == 10.0`,
  `10.0 <= 10.0001` is true → Live runs → current_time = 10.1.
  Total Live calls: 101, expected 100. Off-by-one.

**Concrete bug**: ScenarioRunner over-runs the user-specified
`duration` by exactly one `Live(dt)` call when `duration / dt`
is an integer. Long sims accumulate drift between sim time and
real time.

**Resolution**: outer condition is **strict `<`** (no EPSILON);
inner uses `events[next].time < current_time + dt` (the
upcoming-frame window); plus a post-loop sweep for events at
`time == duration`. Total `Live` calls = `floor(duration / dt)`
exactly.

**Final form**:
```csharp
int next = 0;
while (current_time < duration) {
    while (next < events.Count && events[next].time < current_time + dt) {
        engine.Affect(...);  next++;
    }
    engine.Live(dt);
    current_time += dt;
}
while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= duration) {
    engine.Affect(...);  next++;     // post-loop sweep, no Live
}
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep `<= duration + EPSILON` and add a check inside to skip
   Live when `current_time >= duration` — adds branching to the
   hot loop and is hard to reason about. Rejected.
2. Adjust EPSILON to a tiny value (e.g. `1e-7`) so it doesn't
   matter — IEEE-754 drift can exceed `1e-7` over 100k iterations,
   defeating the purpose. Rejected.
3. **Strict `<` outer + dt-window inner + post-loop sweep** —
   adopted. Each component does one job; total Live count is
   provably `floor(duration / dt)`; boundary events still honored.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3.1 + §26.3.1a: rewritten with the final form trace.
- Q-S33 row in EN/JP header tables: marked "superseded by Q-S35".
- Q-S4 list + forward pointer: untouched (orthogonal).
- Same-time event ordering: preserved.

---

## Q-S36 — needs_meta missing from Scripts/Data.cs (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Data.cs:19-41` (pre-Q-S36): no `needs_meta` property
  on `Persona` or `Kind`; no `NeedMeta` class.
- `EN.md:559-564` (Q-S30): JSON example with `needs_meta`.
- Engine ctor code: `_persona.needs_meta` referenced.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S30 spec was authoritative documentation but
the runtime types didn't exist. Engine ctor's `_persona.needs_meta`
reference is a compile error; A038 has no shape to validate;
Composer §8.3 needs_meta merge has no field to merge.

**Resolution**: `Scripts/Data.cs` gains:
```csharp
public class NeedMeta {
    public int tier { get; set; }
}
public class Persona {
    // ...existing...
    public Dictionary<string, NeedMeta>? needs_meta { get; set; }
}
public class Kind {
    // ...existing...
    public Dictionary<string, NeedMeta>? needs_meta { get; set; }
}
```

`NeedMeta` carries only `tier` in v0.1.5. Future fields (e.g.
per-Need decay-multiplier overrides) can be added without
breaking callers.

**Why a class, not a struct or a typedef'd int**:
- Future-proofing: `NeedMeta` is the natural place for additional
  per-Need metadata (decay rate overrides, label strings, etc.).
- JSON serialization symmetry with the rest of `Data.cs`.
- Nullable `int? tier` would let "tier omitted" be a valid state;
  v0.1.5 chooses "tier required when entry exists" — A038 enforces.

**Cross-impact**:
- Engine ctor (§3.5.2 PHASE A.2 + PHASE C): can now reference
  `_persona.needs_meta`.
- Validator A038: can now validate `meta.Value.tier ∈ [1, 5]`.
- Composer §8.3: can now merge needs_meta as Dictionary last-wins.
- Phase 3 Composer impl: needs to deep-copy the `Dictionary<string, NeedMeta>`.

## Q-S37 — Q-S29 PersonaCache makes Composer-side need_index baking unsafe (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- §10.2.3 (pre-Q-S37): "init in Composer or Engine constructor".
- Q-S27 standard-slot reservation in Engine ctor.
- Q-S29 PersonaCache returns SHARED template Persona to all Agents.

**Concrete bug**: If Composer bakes `Action.need_index` into the
shared template, and one Persona declares non-standard Needs in
order `[oxygen, thirst]` while another declares `[thirst, oxygen]`,
the baked indices reflect only one layout. Sharing the template
across Agents with different layouts → IndexOutOfRange or
cross-Need misread.

**Resolution**: `Action.need_index` and `Threshold.need_index` are
resolved in **Engine ctor (post-DeepCopy)**, NOT in Composer:

```csharp
// Engine ctor PHASE B (after PHASE A builds _need_index)
foreach (var action in _composed_persona.actions) {
    action.need_index = _need_index[action.need];
}
foreach (var threshold in _composed_persona.binding?.thresholds ?? Array.Empty<Threshold>()) {
    threshold.need_index = _need_index[threshold.need];
}
```

The DeepCopy in Agent.Awake step (2) gives this Engine its own
mutable Action/Threshold instances; the indices are local to this
Engine's array layout.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make Composer compute indices and have Engine validate them —
   doubles the work and creates a new failure mode (indices
   inconsistent between Composer's view and Engine's view). Rejected.
2. Lock the non-standard-Need ordering globally (e.g. alphabetical)
   — fragile against adding Needs in future versions; defeats Q-S20
   Persona-first ordering for influences. Rejected.
3. **Engine ctor post-DeepCopy** — adopted. Composer's job shrinks
   to shape composition; index baking is local to one Engine.

**Cross-impact**:
- §16.3.3 Pre-cache Principle: rewritten to specify Engine ctor.
- §3.5.2 PHASE B: explicit ordering.
- Composer responsibility doc: shape composition only.
- Phase 3 Composer impl: must NOT touch need_index; Phase 3
  Engine ctor MUST populate it (PHASE B).
- Q-S29 PersonaCache: now provably safe across Engines.

---

## Q-S38 — PersonaCache stage-2 errors logged but propagated (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- §11.6 (pre-Q-S38) `GetComposed`: stage-2 errors → AnimoLog.Error
  + `_cache[template_id] = composed` + return composed.
- Q-S9 tie-break: `actions.First(...)` on empty list → throws
  InvalidOperationException in middle of Update().

**Concrete bug**: A036 stage-2 catches "composed actions[] empty"
correctly, but PersonaCache logs and returns the broken Persona.
Agent.Awake builds Engine, first Live(dt) hits Q-S9 tie-break,
InvalidOperationException explodes inside Unity's Update loop —
Scene crash.

**Resolution**: `GetComposed` THROWS `InvalidOperationException`
on stage-2 errors:

```csharp
if (stage2.HasErrors) {
    var msg = $"PersonaCache: template '{template_id}' failed stage-2 " +
              $"validation with {stage2.errors.Count} errors " +
              $"(e.g. {stage2.errors[0].rule_id}). Refusing to cache.";
    AnimoLog.Error(msg);
    throw new InvalidOperationException(msg);
}
```

Agent.Awake catches and disables itself:
```csharp
try {
    template = PersonaCache.GetComposed(template_id);
} catch (InvalidOperationException ex) {
    AnimoLog.Error(...);
    enabled = false;
    return;
}
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Return an "empty Persona" sentinel — still lets Engine build
   on garbage data; defers the crash. Rejected.
2. Log and disable the Cache for this template (lazy-fail) —
   subsequent GetComposed calls would silently skip. Master's
   policy is fail-loud, not fail-silent. Rejected.
3. **Throw at GetComposed; Awake catch + disable** — adopted.
   Failure surfaces at the earliest possible moment; Scene
   continues for other Agents.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 Awake code: try/catch wrapping GetComposed.
- A036 + A025 stage-2: now have a concrete enforcement path
  (throw → catch → disable). Before Q-S38 they were "diagnostic
  only".
- Master's fail-loud policy: structurally enforced.

---

## Q-S39 — A019 stage-1 evaluation can't see persona-side needs_meta (Gemini sixteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- §13.1 (pre-Q-S39) A019 row: implied stage 1 (no stage marker
  → stage 1 default).
- §13.2 mermaid: `P4["A013-A019 consistency / format"]` (Stage 1).
- Q-S30: `needs_meta` defined per-Persona, mergeable from Kind.
- Stage 1 evaluates Kinds and Personas SEPARATELY on raw JSON.

**Concrete bug**: Persona declares `needs_meta { oxygen: { tier: 1 } }`,
inherits from Kind that uses `oxygen` in its `actions[]`. Stage 1
evaluates the Kind in isolation: it sees `oxygen` in `actions` but
no `needs_meta` (which lives on the Persona) → fires false-positive
A019 typo Warning. Q-S30 promised "explicit needs_meta suppresses
A019" — pre-Q-S39 that promise was structurally false.

**Resolution**: A019 is now a **Stage 2** rule, evaluated against
the merged Persona post-Composer. The merged shape contains
`needs_meta` (Kind-side + Persona-side merged per §8.3), so the
suppression check works as documented.

Stage-2 A019 algorithm:
```
For each Need name appearing in:
  - composed actions[].need
  - composed influences[].source
  - composed influences[].target
  - composed needs[]:
    if name not in STANDARD_NEEDS AND name not in composed needs_meta:
      emit A019 typo Warning
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep A019 in Stage 1 but pre-load Persona's needs_meta into
   the Kind evaluation context — creates evaluation-order
   dependency; what if Persona's needs_meta itself is invalid
   (A038)? Rejected.
2. Add a separate "A019b" Stage 2 rule that suppresses A019b
   based on needs_meta — duplicates the rule. Rejected.
3. **Move A019 to Stage 2 entirely** — adopted. Cleaner
   semantics; a single rule sees the complete picture.

**Cross-impact**:
- §13.1 A019 row: stage marker + Q-S39 explanation.
- §13.2 mermaid: A019 removed from `P4 [A013-A018]`; new node
  `P9d [Stage 2: A019 typo check on composed Needs]`.
- §13.2 "Why split" prose: new "Why A019 in Stage 2" paragraph.
- §13 title: A000-A038 unchanged (no new rule).
- Phase 3 Validator: A019 implementation moves from stage-1
  pass to stage-2 pass.

## Q-S40 — Q-S35 post-loop sweep is observability black hole (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:4429-4434` (Q-S35 form): `while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= duration) { engine.Affect(...); next++; }`
- No `Live(dt)` and no `RecordTraceFrame` after the sweep.
- ScenarioRunner is a tracing tool: `TraceFrame.action_scores` only gets populated by `RecordTraceFrame` calls inside the Live-and-record loop.

**Concrete bug**: An event scheduled at exactly `time == duration` is consumed by `engine.Affect` (modifies Needs and `force_reset` Threshold latch), but no Live(dt) runs over the post-Affect Needs and no TraceFrame is recorded. The boundary event modifies Engine state invisibly — `TraceResult.frames` shows no evidence of the event. Test harnesses that assert on the final frame's behavior or score get false negatives.

**Resolution**: When the post-loop sweep consumes at least one event, run `engine.Live(dt: 0.0f)` (Step 1 decay is multiplicative-by-dt so dt=0 is a no-op for Needs; Steps 2-5 still run and produce the post-Affect scoring) + `RecordTraceFrame(time: duration)`. Total time-advancing Live calls remains exactly `floor(duration / dt)`; one additional zero-time observation frame is added when the boundary event fires.

```csharp
bool sweep_consumed_any = false;
while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= duration) {
    engine.Affect(...);  next++;
    sweep_consumed_any = true;
}
if (sweep_consumed_any) {
    engine.Live(dt: 0.0f);
    RecordTraceFrame(time: duration);
}
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Always run `Live(dt: 0.0f)` + record post-loop, even when sweep consumed nothing — adds a duplicate-of-last-frame to every TraceResult, polluting tests that count frames. Rejected.
2. Move the boundary check inside the main loop with `<=` outer — re-introduces Q-S33's over-shoot bug Q-S35 just fixed. Rejected.
3. **Conditional final observation pass** — adopted. Total Live calls invariant; observation only when there's something to observe.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3.1 / §26.3.1a: rewritten with Q-S40 sweep_consumed_any flag and post-sweep Live + record.
- Q-S35 invariant `Live calls = floor(duration / dt)` for time-advancing Live: preserved.
- Q-S31 silent first transition: not relevant in Runner context (already past frame 1 of Engine life by the time the post-loop sweep runs).
- TraceResult consumers: now see boundary events; downstream tests asserting on frame counts need to expect +1 frame when boundary events exist.

---

## Q-S41 — A038 orphan check fires false positives on cascade (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:633-636` (pre-Q-S41 spec): `needs_meta entry for a Need that does not exist in needs → Warning A038`. No stage marker → Stage 1 default.
- §8.3 `needs_meta` merge unit: Persona inherits Kind's needs_meta keys.
- A child Persona using only `thirst` from a Kind that declares `needs_meta { oxygen, thirst }` would have composed `needs_meta` containing `oxygen` whose Need is absent from composed `needs[]` → A038 fires per-Persona.

**Concrete bug**: Generic "survival NPC" Kinds declaring broad `needs_meta` for genre coverage cause Warning spam in every child Persona that uses only a subset. Author has done nothing wrong — they've simply specialized a generic Kind.

**Resolution**: Move the orphan check from Stage 1 to Stage 2 AND broaden the existence test:
- Stage 2 evaluates the *composed* Persona, so Kind-side metadata that wasn't actually used post-composition is what we want to flag.
- A Need is "in use" if it appears in composed `needs[]` OR `actions[].need` OR `influences[].source/target` — any of these is real evidence that the Need is alive in this Persona's behavior.

The `[1, 5]` tier range Error stays Stage 1 (it's about the metadata's own validity, not its relevance).

**Why not other options**:
1. Make the orphan check Info instead of Warning — still spams the inspector with non-actionable diagnostics. Rejected.
2. Suppress A038 entirely when needs_meta is inherited from Kind — loses the legitimate signal of typo'd Need names in needs_meta. Rejected.
3. **Stage 2 + broadened existence test** — adopted. Catches genuine orphans; ignores Kind-cascade artifacts.

**Cross-impact**:
- §13.1 A038 row: split into Stage 1 (tier range, standard tier disagreement) and Stage 2 (composed orphan).
- §13.2 stage-2 mermaid: new P9f node "A038 needs_meta orphan check".
- §13.2.1 ValidateStage2 docstring: A038's Stage 2 case listed.
- §3.5.2 Validator rule A038 prose: rewritten with three bullets (Stage 1 tier range, Stage 1 standard disagree, Stage 2 orphan).
- Validator rule count: still 39 (A000-A038). A038's scope split, no new rule.
- A019 Stage 2 (Q-S39): same pass, same composed Persona view — natural pairing.

## Q-S42 — ScenarioRunner override skip hardcodes single-agent (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1989` (pre-Q-S42): "For tests that spawn one Persona, ScenarioRunner skips the override — the JSON agent_id IS the runtime id."
- Q-S6 keep-first uniqueness rule: Store.Register on duplicate id rejects subsequent registration with Warning.
- Two ScenarioRunner.Run() calls with the same agent_id → second call's Engine builds, but Store.Register rejects, leaving the second run as a Store-disconnected zombie that can't receive Affect.

**Concrete bug**: Hardcoding "ScenarioRunner skips the override" makes any multi-agent test impossible: two goblins fighting from one template share `agent_id` and collide on Store.Register. Future multi-agent runs are architecturally blocked by a comment in §11.4.1.

**Resolution**: ScenarioRunner.Run() applies the override unconditionally:
- New optional parameter `agent_id_override: string?` (default null).
- When null, runner generates `$"{template_id}_run_{_seq++}"` with a per-instance counter.
- Caller can pass an explicit value for deterministic test names (e.g. `"goblin_red"`, `"goblin_blue"`).
- Single-Persona tests are unaffected (one Run() call → one unique override → one Store entry).

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep the skip and document "ScenarioRunner is single-agent only" — concretely blocks the v0.2 multi-agent runner work; against the Animo design philosophy of staying composable. Rejected.
2. Always require caller to pass an explicit override — boilerplate for the 99% case (single-Persona tests). Rejected.
3. **Default auto-uniquify with optional explicit override** — adopted. No boilerplate; deterministic when needed; collision-free always.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3 ScenarioRunner.Run signature: new parameter.
- §11.4.1 prose: removed "skips the override" rule, replaced with "every host applies the override".
- Q-S28 prefab override path: parallel; both Unity Agent and ScenarioRunner now follow the same regime.
- Single-Persona tests: behavior unchanged — they get a unique override they don't have to think about.

---

## Q-S43 — (need, trigger_threshold) compound key uses raw float == (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:189` Q-S14 row: "match by `(need, trigger_threshold)` compound key, last-wins". No mention of float comparison strategy.
- C# `Dictionary<(string, float), Threshold>.ContainsKey((need, 80.0001f))` returns false when keyed at `(need, 80.0f)`.
- JSON deserializers (Newtonsoft.Json, System.Text.Json) round-trip floats through ToString-and-Parse for some configurations, occasionally producing `80.0` → `80.0000076...` artifacts.

**Concrete bug**: Persona overriding a Kind's `trigger_threshold: 80.0` with `80.0` (intent: same milestone, change `signal_id`) but parsed to `80.0000076` after JSON round-trip → compound key mismatch → both Threshold instances kept post-merge → both fire. Override silently became duplication.

**Resolution**: Replace raw float `==` with EPSILON tolerance:

```csharp
const float THRESHOLD_KEY_EPSILON = 0.5f;
bool ThresholdsMatch(Threshold a, Threshold b) {
    return a.need == b.need
        && Math.Abs(a.trigger_threshold - b.trigger_threshold) < THRESHOLD_KEY_EPSILON;
}
```

Linear search per Persona threshold (O(N), N ≤ 10 in practice) replaces the fragile float-keyed Dictionary. 0.5f window:
- Wider than IEEE-754 round-trip drift (~1e-7 at scale [0, 100]).
- Narrower than authored milestone spacing (≥ 5 per A035 / Q-S15).

**Why not other options**:
1. Quantize trigger_threshold to integer at parse time — loses sub-integer authoring (e.g. `trigger: 75.5` for fine emotional grading). Rejected.
2. Use `decimal` instead of `float` — rest of Animo is `float`-based for hot-path performance; introducing `decimal` just for this comparison is asymmetric. Rejected.
3. **EPSILON-tolerance linear comparison** — adopted. Robust against JSON drift; preserves authoring precision; trivially fast at N ≤ 10.

**Cross-impact**:
- §8.3 thresholds row: notes Q-S43 EPSILON.
- §8.3.1 (new): explicit pseudocode for ThresholdsMatch.
- A035 / Q-S15 (≥ 5 spacing) is the safety floor: 0.5 < 5, so authored milestones can't collapse.
- Composer impl: switches from Dictionary<(string, float), _> to List<Threshold> + linear scan.

---

## Q-S44 — Animator state name asymmetry between frame 1 and frame 2+ (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1977` (Q-S34): `_animator?.Play(stateName: _engine.behavior);` — passes raw Action id like "Flee".
- §16.5 OnBehaviorChanged: publishes `_cached_action_triggers[new_behavior]` to Bus — template-expanded like "animo_goblin_47291_flee".
- Host's Animator on frame 1: receives "Flee". On frame 2+: receives "animo_goblin_47291_flee" via Bus → Germio → Animator.

**Concrete bug**: The host has to support TWO state-name namespaces in its Animator: raw Action id (frame 1 only, Q-S34 path) and template-expanded id (all later frames, Bus path). This is exactly the kind of asymmetric API that breaks long-term maintenance and forces every Germio adapter to special-case frame 1.

**Resolution**: Add `Engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(string behavior)` cold-path internal accessor that reads `_cached_action_triggers` and returns the same template-expanded string OnBehaviorChanged would publish to Bus. Agent.Awake step (6):

```csharp
_engine.Live(dt: 0.0f);
string trigger = _engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(_engine.behavior);
_animator?.Play(stateName: trigger);
```

The Bus is still not involved (Q-S31 silent contract preserved); the View path uses the same expander to keep the host's state-name namespace consistent.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make Q-S31 raise OnSignal for the first transition too — defeats the storm-prevention purpose; 100 spawning NPCs flood Bus on frame 1. Rejected.
2. Always use raw Action id for Animator everywhere, drop template expansion — breaks Germio's `animo_*` naming scheme that other tools subscribe to. Rejected.
3. **GetExpandedActionTrigger accessor + use template-expanded form on frame 1 too** — adopted. Q-S31 silent intact; namespace symmetric.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 Awake code: Q-S44 step replaces raw `_engine.behavior` with `_engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(...)`.
- §11.4 sequence Mermaid: new step for the expander call.
- §16.5: GetExpandedActionTrigger body next to OnBehaviorChanged for symmetry.
- Engine.cs: new internal accessor with NotImplementedException stub (Phase 3 implementation).
- Q-S26 OnSignal path: untouched.
- Q-S32 internal accessors via InternalsVisibleTo("Animo.Tools"): same mechanism, no new InternalsVisibleTo declaration needed (Animo.Agent is in same assembly as Animo.Engine).

## Q-S45 — Standard Need future-metadata extensibility hard-banned (Gemini seventeenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:704-708` (Phase_2_4_13 PHASE C): `if (Array.IndexOf(Const.STANDARD_NEEDS, meta.Key) >= 0) { ... continue; }`.
- `Scripts/Data.cs` NeedMeta class doc: "Future fields (e.g. per-Need decay-multiplier overrides) can be added without breaking callers."

**Concrete bug**: The `continue` blanket-skips standard Needs in the entire `needs_meta` processing loop. Any future NeedMeta field — `decay_multiplier`, `label`, `category`, anything — can never apply to the eight standard Needs (hunger, thirst, fatigue, fear, pain, frustration, comfort, social). The class's "future fields can be added" promise is structurally false because PHASE C will skip them for all standard Needs.

**Resolution**: Narrow the skip to **tier only**. The §3.5 wins-for-tier rule (Q-S30) still applies, but other NeedMeta fields flow through `ApplyNonTierMetadata` for standard Needs too:

```csharp
if (_persona.needs_meta != null) {
    foreach (var meta in _persona.needs_meta) {
        bool is_standard = Array.IndexOf(Const.STANDARD_NEEDS, meta.Key) >= 0;
        if (!is_standard) {
            // Non-standard: full participation including tier
            int tier = meta.Value.tier;
            if (!_need_tier_indices.ContainsKey(tier)) {
                _need_tier_indices[tier] = new List<int>();
            }
            _need_tier_indices[tier].Add(_need_index[meta.Key]);
        }
        // (Q-S45) Standard Needs skip the tier assignment but still
        // receive non-tier metadata via this hook.
        ApplyNonTierMetadata(_need_index[meta.Key], meta.Value);
    }
}
```

`ApplyNonTierMetadata` is a no-op in v0.1.5 (NeedMeta only has `tier`); it's a reserved extension point for v0.2/v0.3 fields.

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep `continue` and make Q-S36's NeedMeta doc say "tier-only, no future expansion" — concretely abandons NeedMeta's stated contract. Rejected.
2. Expand NeedMeta tier into a Dict<string, object> so consumers can ignore standard-Need entries — loses type safety; defeats Q-S36's typed approach. Rejected.
3. **Narrow skip to tier-only with `ApplyNonTierMetadata` extension hook** — adopted. v0.1.5 runtime unchanged; v0.2/v0.3 path preserved.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2 PHASE C: code rewritten to narrow skip.
- §16.6 Engine fields: no change (no new state).
- v0.1.5 runtime behavior: unchanged (ApplyNonTierMetadata is no-op).
- v0.2 NeedMeta extension: now possible for standard Needs.

## Q-S46 — Engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger reads cache that §16.6 placed on Agent (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Engine.cs:137` Q-S44 declaration: `internal string GetExpandedActionTrigger(string behavior)`.
- `EN.md:3308` (pre-Q-S46): `| Agent | _cached_action_triggers Dictionary | v0.1.1 |`
- `EN.md:3193, 3209` §16.5 actual code: `_cached_action_triggers = new Dictionary<...>(); _cached_action_triggers[action.id] = expanded;` — runs in Engine ctor scope.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S44 added `GetExpandedActionTrigger` to Engine. The body necessarily reads `_cached_action_triggers`. But §16.6 Engine fields table listed this Dictionary as belonging to `Agent` (a `MonoBehaviour`). Engine cannot reach into Agent's fields (no inheritance, no friendship, no shared state). The method body was a confirmed compile error — implementer would face "field does not exist on Engine".

**Resolution**: Move the §16.6 table entry from `Agent` to `Engine`. The cache always lived in Engine's scope per §16.5; only the documentation table was wrong.

```
Pre-Q-S46:
  | Agent  | _cached_action_triggers Dictionary | v0.1.1 |

Post-Q-S46:
  | Engine | _cached_action_triggers Dictionary<string, string> —
            binding.on_action_change template pre-expanded with this
            Engine's runtime-unique agent_id. Built once in Engine ctor
            (after Q-S28 override has set _composed_persona.agent_id);
            read by OnBehaviorChanged (§16.5) and GetExpandedActionTrigger
            (Q-S44 cold-path accessor). | v0.1.1; ownership pinned in v0.1.5 (Q-S46) |
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Move Q-S44 accessor to Agent — Agent.cs is a MonoBehaviour script outside the pure Animo.Core assembly; tools (ScenarioRunner) can't depend on it. Rejected.
2. Add a public Engine method `SetActionTriggerCache(Dictionary)` and have Agent push the cache — adds API surface for what is purely an internal Engine optimization. Rejected.
3. **Pin the table to Engine** — adopted. Documentation matches implementation; no compile error.

**Cross-impact**:
- §16.6 Engine fields table: row pinned to `Engine`.
- §16.5 actual code: unchanged (already in Engine ctor).
- Q-S44 GetExpandedActionTrigger: now buildable.
- Q-S28 agent_id override: still happens BEFORE Engine ctor cache build.

## Q-S47 — Q-S43's THRESHOLD_KEY_EPSILON = 0.5f justified by category error (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1182` (Q-S43): "narrower than authored-milestone spacing (always ≥ 5 by A035 / Q-S15)".
- `EN.md:186` (A035 / Q-S11): "omitted `reset_threshold` defaults to `Math.Max(0, trigger - 5.0)`" — the 5-unit gap is between trigger and reset of the same Threshold.
- No spec rule guarantees sibling-trigger spacing.

**Concrete bug**: An LLM author writing two thresholds for the same Need at adjacent values (e.g. `fear=80.0 → alert`, `fear=80.4 → panic`) would have both collapsed by Q-S43's `0.5f` window — Persona's value wins, only one threshold survives. Q-S43's justification was a category error: A035 protects within a single Threshold's hysteresis, not between sibling Thresholds.

**Resolution**: Refine `EPSILON = 0.01f`:
- Three orders of magnitude over realistic IEEE-754 round-trip drift (~1e-7 at [0, 100] scale).
- Preserves authored distinctions down to 1/100 Need unit.
- Authored authentic duplicates (80.0 + drift `80.0001`) still collapse correctly.

Add new Validator rule **A039** (Stage 2 Warning) to surface sibling pairs within `1.0f` of each other:

```
For each composed Persona's binding.thresholds[]:
  Group by need; sort triggers ascending.
  For each adjacent pair (a, b), if (b.trigger - a.trigger) < 1.0f:
    Emit A039 Warning describing the pair.
```

Window choice:
- 1.0f surface threshold is well above merge-collapse window (0.01f).
- Well below typical authored milestone spacing (most authors space milestones in steps of 5 or 10).
- Tightly-spaced thresholds CAN be intentional (fast-rising stress curves), so A039 is Warning, not Error.

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep `0.5f` and document the limitation — accepts the silent destruction of valid authored milestones. Rejected.
2. Use `0.0f` (raw `==`) — re-introduces Q-S43's original IEEE-754 drift bug. Rejected.
3. **`0.01f` + A039 surface Warning** — adopted. Drift collapses correctly; authored distinctions preserved; tight pairs surfaced.

**Cross-impact**:
- §8.3 thresholds row: Q-S47 reference.
- §8.3.1 prose + pseudocode: rewritten with `0.01f` and corrected justification.
- §13.1 A039 row: new entry.
- §13.2 stage-2 mermaid: P9g node added; connection P9f → P9g → Result.
- §13.2.1 ValidateStage2 docstring: A039 listed.
- §11.6 PersonaCache stage-2 comment: A039/Q-S47 listed.
- README: rule count 39 → 40, mermaid V2 stage-2 list updated.
- §13 title: A000-A038 → A000-A039.

## Q-S48 — Q-S45's ApplyNonTierMetadata call has no declaration (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:844` (Q-S45 PHASE C): `ApplyNonTierMetadata(_need_index[meta.Key], meta.Value);`
- `Scripts/Engine.cs` (pre-Q-S48): no `ApplyNonTierMetadata` declaration.
- §6 / §9 Engine API list: not mentioned.
- Engine has no `float[]` or Dictionary slots for non-tier metadata.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S45 wrote a call site for `ApplyNonTierMetadata` to keep the future-extensibility door open for standard Needs. But no method existed. The PHASE C code as written is a confirmed compile error.

**Resolution**: Add a private no-op stub to `Scripts/Engine.cs`:

```csharp
/// <summary>
/// (Q-S45 + Q-S48) Hook for non-tier NeedMeta fields applied
/// during Engine ctor PHASE C (§3.5.2). Called for both standard
/// and non-standard Needs — only the tier-assignment side of
/// PHASE C skips standard Needs. v0.1.5 has no non-tier NeedMeta
/// fields, so this method is a no-op (Phase 3 implements as an
/// empty body); v0.2 / v0.3 may add fields like
/// `decay_multiplier` or `label` and implement them here.
/// </summary>
private void ApplyNonTierMetadata(int need_index, NeedMeta meta) {
    // v0.1.5: no-op. NeedMeta currently carries only `tier`,
    // which PHASE C handles directly. Future fields apply here.
}
```

The method is `private` because it is purely internal to Engine ctor's PHASE C; no external caller. The signature matches the Q-S45 call site exactly.

**Why not other options**:
1. Remove the Q-S45 call site and tell Q-S36 it can't extend — abandons the "Future fields can be added without breaking callers" promise. Rejected.
2. Make it `public virtual` for subclass override — adds API surface for what is purely an internal extension hook. Rejected.
3. **Private no-op stub** — adopted. v0.1.5 builds; v0.2/v0.3 implement when adding fields.

**Cross-impact**:
- Engine.cs: new private method declaration.
- §3.5.2 PHASE C: comment block now points readers to "Engine.cs declaration".
- §16.5 (JP only): comment block now mentions Q-S48.
- Q-S36 NeedMeta extensibility promise: kept structurally intact.

## Q-S49 — Q-S41 orphan check omitted binding.thresholds[].need (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:696` (Q-S41 spec): "needs[] *or* actions[].need *or* influences[].source/target".
- §12 / §16.5 binding.thresholds usage: Threshold can be the SOLE consumer of a Need (signal-only Need, e.g. `oxygen` → UI alert with no scoring action).

**Concrete bug**: A Need used only via `binding.thresholds[].need` — legitimate signal-only Need pattern — would be A038-orphan-flagged because Q-S41's broadened "in use" list missed thresholds.

**Resolution**: Add `binding.thresholds[].need` as the fourth "in use" site:

```
A038 orphan Warning fires when:
  meta_need ∉ composed needs[]
  AND meta_need ∉ composed actions[].need
  AND meta_need ∉ composed influences[].source/target
  AND meta_need ∉ composed binding.thresholds[].need
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Add a separate "signal-only Need" annotation in `needs_meta` (e.g. `signal_only: true`) — adds API surface for a legitimate pattern that needs no annotation. Rejected.
2. Demote A038 orphan to Info — loses the legitimate signal of typo'd Need names in needs_meta. Rejected.
3. **Include thresholds in "in use" union** — adopted. Cleanest fix; signal-only Needs validate without annotation.

**Cross-impact**:
- §13.1 A038 row: 4-site union explicit.
- §3.5.2 Validator rule A038 prose: 4-site union explicit.
- §13.2 stage-2 mermaid P9f: description "actions/influences/thresholds".
- README mermaid V2: "actions/influences/thresholds — Q-S41+Q-S49".

## Q-S50 — Q-S42's Store.Register justification fails type check (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Store.cs:37` `public void Register(IAnimoAgent agent)`.
- `Scripts/Store.cs:65` `public interface IAnimoAgent { ... }`.
- §26.3 ScenarioRunner: constructs `new Engine(persona)` directly; no IAnimoAgent wrapper.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S42 justified ScenarioRunner's universal override with "Store.Register collisions per Q-S6". But `Store.Register` accepts `IAnimoAgent`, and ScenarioRunner has nothing to pass. The runner cannot interact with Store at the type-system level. The Q-S42 justification was structurally false.

**Resolution**: Document `ScenarioRunner` as **independent of `Store`**. Runner maintains its own `Dictionary<string, Engine>` keyed by the (overridden) `agent_id` for routing `Affect`/`Lock` calls. `Store` remains the Unity-Agent registry, used only by `Animo.Agent : MonoBehaviour`.

Q-S42's runtime-unique override on the runner now has a different (and valid) purpose:
- Unique keys for the runner's internal `Dictionary<string, Engine>`.
- Per-run identifiers in `expanded_action_change` Bus payloads, so multi-run aggregations distinguish frame events.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make `ScenarioRunner` register a fake `IAnimoAgent` wrapper around its Engine — adds wrapper code for no gain; Store doesn't help the runner's routing anyway. Rejected.
2. Make `Store` accept raw `Engine` references — breaks the Unity Agent invariant that Store entries are MonoBehaviours that respond to lifecycle events. Rejected.
3. **Document independence + internal Dictionary** — adopted. Type-clean; runner's routing is local; Store stays Unity-only.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 prose: ScenarioRunner-Store independence explicit; Q-S42 override purpose corrected.
- §26 ScenarioRunner: internal Dictionary documented.
- Q-S6 keep-first uniqueness: Unity Agent only (already implicit).
- Q-S22 instance check: Unity Agent only (already implicit).

## Q-S51 — ScenarioRunner missed t=0 spawn-state observation (Gemini eighteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:4580` (pre-Q-S51 ScenarioRunner loop): `RecordTraceFrame(time: current_time + dt)` — first record at `time = dt`.
- §11.4.1 (Q-S34) Awake: `_engine.Live(dt: 0.0f); _animator?.Play(...)` — Unity Agent gets t=0 spawn state.
- ScenarioRunner has no equivalent.

**Concrete bug**: Trace consumers see simulations that "start at `dt` seconds". The spawn-moment Need values, Q-S9 tie-break initial behavior, and any spawn-time scoring are all invisible in `TraceResult.frames`.

**Resolution**: Add a pre-loop spawn-state observation parallel to Q-S34's Awake step (6):

```csharp
// Before the main loop:
engine.Live(dt: 0.0f);                       // seed initial behavior (parallel to Q-S34)
RecordTraceFrame(time: 0.0f);                // observable spawn-state frame

// Then the existing loop:
int next = 0;
while (current_time < duration) { ... }
```

`Live(dt: 0.0f)` is a no-op for time advancement (Step 1 decay is multiplicative-by-dt) but runs Steps 2-5 over the spawn Needs, producing the initial scoring decision. Same contract as Q-S34's Awake step (6).

Total time-advancing `Live(dt)` calls remain `floor(duration / dt)`. The observation pass adds one zero-time `Live` + one frame at `time = 0.0`.

**Why not other options**:
1. Have callers pre-call Live themselves — silent footgun; trace consumers will keep seeing inconsistent results. Rejected.
2. Move the spawn observation to a `RecordSpawnFrame()` helper — doesn't change the substance, just renames. Rejected.
3. **Pre-loop Live(0.0f) + RecordTraceFrame(0.0f)** — adopted. Mirror of Q-S34's Awake; consistent semantics across Unity and ScenarioRunner.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3.1 loop: pre-loop observation pass added; comments explain Q-S34 parallel.
- TraceResult consumers: now see the spawn frame at `time = 0.0`.
- Total frame count: `1 (spawn) + floor(duration/dt) (loop) + 0/1 (Q-S40 boundary)`.
- Q-S40 boundary observation: still applies; orthogonal to Q-S51.
- Test ScenarioRunnerBoundaryObservability: frame count expectations include +1 for spawn.

## Q-S52 — LINQ in the Hot Path violates §16.1 zero-alloc rule (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:213` (Q-S38 historical citation): `Q-S9's actions.First(...) on an empty composed list`
- `EN.md:2680` (A036 narrative): `Step 5's tie-break (actions.First(...), Q-S9)`
- `EN.md:2296` (PersonaCache comment): `Q-S9 tie-break (actions.First(...))`
- §16.1 design rule predates Q-S9; the spec narrative shorthand silently introduced LINQ into Step 5's description.

**Concrete bug**: A Phase 3 implementer reading the spec sees `actions.First(a => a.score == max_score)` written as a description of how Step 5 works. They write the code that way. `actions.First(...)` allocates an `IEnumerator` + closure. With 100 agents at 60 fps, that is 6000 allocations per second from a single line. §16.1 forbids exactly this pattern.

**Resolution**: 
1. Add explicit "no LINQ" rule to §16.1's enumerated forbidden patterns (was 2 traps, now 3).
2. Add new §16.1.1 "Step 5 Tie-Break: Zero-Alloc For-Loop" pinning the implementation pattern to a single forward for-loop with strict `>` comparison.
3. Rewrite all narrative shorthands away from `actions.First(...)`; remaining mentions are bracketed as historical citations of pre-Q-S52 spec language.

```csharp
float max_score = float.NegativeInfinity;
int   selected_index = -1;
for (int i = 0; i < actions.Count; i++) {
    float s = _action_scores[i];
    if (s > max_score) {                  // strict `>` keeps first-seen on ties
        max_score = s;
        selected_index = i;
    }
}
```

The strict `>` naturally implements Q-S9's first-declaration-wins because any later action whose score equals the current max does not update `selected_index`. Zero alloc, zero LINQ, zero closure.

**Why not other options**:
1. Allow LINQ but document the alloc cost — concretely abandons the zero-alloc promise. Rejected.
2. Use `actions.OrderBy(...).First()` — even worse (sort + alloc). Rejected.
3. **For-loop with strict `>` comparison** — adopted. Idiomatic C#; one pass; zero alloc.

**Cross-impact**:
- §16.1 enumerated rules: 2 traps → 3 traps.
- §16.1.1 (new): explicit pseudocode for Step 5.
- §3.1 historical citations of `actions.First(...)`: bracketed as "the for-loop pinned by Q-S52; pre-Q-S52 spec narrative used the LINQ shorthand `actions.First(...)`".
- Q-S38, A036 narratives: same disambiguation note.
- Phase 3 baseline test `Step5TieBreakZeroAllocTests`: asserts the for-loop pattern.

---

## Q-S53 — String cache initialization split between Engine and Agent (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:3315` (pre-Q-S53): `// Once in Agent.Awake` followed by `void Awake() { ... t.expanded_trigger = t.trigger.Replace(...) }`
- Q-S46 already moved `_cached_action_triggers` ownership to Engine, but the §16.5 sample code block stayed in Agent.Awake — only the §16.6 ownership row was updated.
- ScenarioRunner constructs Engine directly, never calling Agent.Awake.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S46 fixed the table but not the code sample. Q-S46's intent — "cache lives on Engine, accessible from Engine" — was undermined by the sample code still running cache initialization from Agent.Awake. ScenarioRunner-driven Engines had Threshold.expanded_trigger = "" for every Threshold; every fired signal was the empty string. ScenarioRunner traces would show "fire happened" but no `signal_id` to identify which Threshold fired.

**Resolution**: Move the entire string-cache initialization block — both `_cached_action_triggers` foreach AND the per-Threshold `expanded_trigger` foreach — into Engine ctor. Run after Q-S28 agent_id override has been applied to `_composed_persona.agent_id`. Both Unity Agent and ScenarioRunner inherit a fully-populated cache from Engine ctor.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make ScenarioRunner manually run the cache initialization — duplicates logic; first thing maintainers will skip. Rejected.
2. Make `Engine` lazy-initialize the cache on first OnSignal — adds branch in hot path. Rejected.
3. **Engine ctor initializes both caches** — adopted. Symmetric ownership; one place to maintain.

**Cross-impact**:
- §16.5 sample code: `void Awake()` → `public Engine(Persona persona)`.
- Q-S46 ownership row: still correct.
- ScenarioRunner: works without modification (Engine ctor was already called).
- Test `ScenarioRunnerThresholdSignalContentTests`: now exists to verify expanded_trigger non-empty for Runner-driven Engine.

## Q-S54 — GetNeed(string) ambiguous between base and effective (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:589` (§3.2): `Engine.GetNeed(string need)` — returns the current value of the named Need
- `EN.md:1386` (§9.1 table): `read the current value of one Need`
- Q-S23 introduced `_effective_needs` (post-cascade) as the value driving observable behavior; `_needs` is the raw base.
- "Current value" picks neither.

**Concrete bug**: An Influence cascade can amplify `fear` from base 30 to effective 80; the NPC flees because Step 4 reads effective. An inspector tool calling `GetNeed("fear")` and seeing 30 reports "AI is broken — running on fear=30 with no flee threshold". The debug API hides the cascade it was meant to surface.

**Resolution**: 
- Pin `GetNeed(string need)` to **effective_needs** — the value Step 4 actually consumes.
- Add new `GetBaseNeed(string need)` → returns base `_needs` for tools that need both layers.

**Why not other options**:
1. Pin `GetNeed` to base — most callers want "what is the AI thinking", which is effective. Rejected.
2. Make `GetNeed` return a struct `(base, effective)` — adds allocation per call. Rejected.
3. **Default = effective + companion `GetBaseNeed`** — adopted. Default matches "what you'd ask for"; both layers available without alloc.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.2 New Engine API: rewritten with both methods.
- §9.1 table: `GetNeed` row + new `GetBaseNeed` row.
- Engine.cs: added `GetBaseNeed` declaration with NotImplementedException stub.
- Inspector tools: now have a clean two-method API.
