## Q-S55 — ScenarioRunner pre-loop spawn missed t=0 events (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:4715` (pre-Q-S55 Q-S51 code): `engine.Live(dt: 0.0f); RecordTraceFrame(time: 0.0f);` — runs BEFORE the event loop.
- The first iteration's inner sweep tests `events[next].time < current_time + dt`. With `current_time = 0` and `dt = 0.1`, that is `events[next].time < 0.1` — events at exactly `time = 0.0` qualify, BUT they are consumed AFTER the `RecordTraceFrame(time: 0.0f)` already fired.

**Concrete bug**: A test specifying initial state via `events = [{ time: 0.0, ev: Affect("fear", +50) }]` records the t=0 frame with `fear` still at spawn value. The Affect arrives later in the same loop iteration, but the trace frame is already written. Test consumers see "the trace at t=0 shows fear=0, but the player set fear=50 at t=0".

**Resolution**: Sweep `events[next].time <= 0.0f` BEFORE the spawn `Live(0.0f)` + record. The `<=0` form (rather than `==0`) correctly handles any negative-time events a hand-built test might inject — IEEE-754 epsilon around zero is meaningless, so semantic clamping is the right interpretation.

```csharp
int next = 0;
while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= 0.0f) {
    engine.Affect(events[next].ev.need, events[next].ev.delta, events[next].ev.force_reset);
    next++;
}
engine.Live(dt: 0.0f);
RecordTraceFrame(time: 0.0f);   // post-t=0-events
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Reject events at `time = 0.0f` with an error — punishes a legitimate authoring pattern. Rejected.
2. Document "t=0 events are deferred to first frame" — leaves the trace mismatch in place. Rejected.
3. **Pre-spawn sweep with `<=` semantics** — adopted. Trace at t=0 reflects authored initial state.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3.1 ScenarioRunner code: pre-loop sweep added before spawn `Live(0.0f)`.
- Q-S51 spawn-state contract: extended to "post-t=0-events spawn state".
- Q-S40 boundary observation: orthogonal; still applies.
- Test `ScenarioRunnerT0EventVisibilityTests`: asserts trace frame at t=0 shows post-Affect Need.

---

## Q-S56 — ApplyNonTierMetadata loop only iterates needs_meta entries (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:826-853` (pre-Q-S56 Q-S45 code): `if (_persona.needs_meta != null) { foreach (var meta in _persona.needs_meta) { ... ApplyNonTierMetadata(...); } }`
- Q-S45's intent: "apply non-tier NeedMeta fields to standard Needs too" — but only triggered if author wrote that Need into needs_meta.

**Concrete bug**: A Persona using only standard Needs has no reason to write `needs_meta` (the default tier mapping comes from §3.5). With no `needs_meta`, the loop never runs, and `ApplyNonTierMetadata` is never called for any Need. When v0.2 adds a NeedMeta field like `decay_multiplier` and an engine-side default ("multiply all standard Need decay by 0.9"), there's no place to inject it.

**Resolution**: Separate the pass into two:
1. **Step 2 (tier participation)**: still loops `needs_meta` entries to extend `_need_tier_indices` — only non-standard Needs participate (§3.5 wins for tier).
2. **Step 3 (non-tier metadata)**: loops ALL Needs in composed `needs[]`, calls `ApplyNonTierMetadata(idx, explicit_or_default_meta)`. If the Need is in `needs_meta`, use the explicit entry; otherwise use `NeedMeta.DefaultFor(name)`.

`NeedMeta.DefaultFor(string need_name)` is a new factory method that returns per-Need defaults:
- Standard Needs: tier from `Const.NEED_TIER_BY_NAME` (§3.5 lookup).
- Non-standard Needs reaching this point without explicit meta: sentinel `tier=0` (Validator-prevented contradiction; safe by construction).

**Why not other options**:
1. Force authors to write `needs_meta` for all Needs — defeats `needs_meta`'s "opt-in" promise. Rejected.
2. Make `ApplyNonTierMetadata` mutate every Need to a sentinel default in v0.1.5 — futureproofing without futureproofing. Rejected.
3. **Two-pass: tier loop + universal loop** — adopted. Tier semantics preserved (standard skip); future fields reach all Needs with explicit-or-default meta.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2 PHASE C: rewritten as Step 2 (tier) + Step 3 (universal non-tier).
- Data.cs `NeedMeta`: new static `DefaultFor(string)` factory.
- Const.cs: existing `NEED_TIER_BY_NAME` consumed by `DefaultFor`.
- v0.1.5 runtime: unchanged (Step 3 is no-op since NeedMeta has only `tier`).
- v0.2/v0.3 path: future field added to NeedMeta + filled in `DefaultFor` + consumed in `ApplyNonTierMetadata` body.

---

## Q-S57 — A038 orphan union missed `rates` (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:696` (pre-Q-S57 Q-S41+Q-S49): `composed needs[] *or* actions[].need *or* influences[].source/target *or* binding.thresholds[].need`
- Persona has a `Rates` field: `Dictionary<string, float> values` keyed by Need name.
- A "pure-rate Need" pattern (e.g. `poison` decaying via `rates`, read by UI without any Action/Influence/Threshold) is structurally valid.

**Concrete bug**: A Persona with `needs: ["poison"]`, `needs_meta: { poison: { tier: 1 } }`, `rates: { poison: -0.5 }`, and no other reference is correctly authored — `poison` decays naturally over time, UI reads `GetNeed("poison")`. But A038's "in use" union after Q-S49 has 4 sites; `rates` is missing. Validator emits `A038 Warning: poison is orphaned in needs_meta` even though `rates` is the entire point of declaring it.

**Resolution**: Add `rates.keys()` as the 5th "in use" site:

```
"In use" union (post-Q-S57):
  needs[]
  ∪ actions[].need
  ∪ influences[].source/target
  ∪ binding.thresholds[].need
  ∪ rates.keys()
```

A038 orphan Warning fires only when none of the 5 sites reference the Need.

**Why not other options**:
1. Document the pure-rate pattern as "must add to needs[] explicitly" — pure-rate Needs ARE always in `needs[]` (the spec requires declaration); the issue is that `rates` should also count as evidence the Need is alive, not just declared. Rejected.
2. Demote A038 to Info — loses signal of typo'd Need names. Rejected.
3. **Add `rates.keys()` to the union** — adopted. Mirrors how Q-S41 added actions/influences and Q-S49 added thresholds.

**Cross-impact**:
- §13.1 A038 row: 5-site union explicit.
- §3.5.2 Validator rule A038 prose: 5-site union explicit.
- §13.2 stage-2 mermaid P9f: description "actions/influences/thresholds/rates".
- §13.2.1 ValidateStage2 docstring: 5-site list.
- §11.6 PersonaCache stage-2 comment: Q-S57 listed.
- README Validator section + V2 mermaid: 5-site list.

---

## Q-S58 — AnimoBootstrapper.OnDestroy didn't clear Store (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2355-2357` (pre-Q-S58): `void OnDestroy() { Animo.PersonaCache.ClearForTesting(); }`
- Store is a singleton (`Animo.Store.Instance`); under Unity Editor "Enter Play Mode Options (Fast)", static state persists between Play sessions.

**Concrete bug**: Each Play session populates `Store.Instance._agents` via Agent.Awake → Register. On scene unload, only PersonaCache is cleared. Next Play session's Agents register fresh entries, but the previous session's stale entries are still in the dictionary. If a new Agent happens to hit the same `agent_id` as a stale entry (possible because Q-S28 uses GameObject.GetInstanceID() which can repeat across Play sessions), Q-S6's keep-first defense rejects the new Agent — leaving it as a Bus-disconnected zombie. Bug appears non-deterministically and only in Editor with Fast play mode.

**Resolution**: Pair `Store.ResetForTesting()` with `PersonaCache.ClearForTesting()` in `OnDestroy`:

```csharp
void OnDestroy() {
    Animo.PersonaCache.ClearForTesting();   // scene unload cleanup (Q-S29)
    Animo.Store.Instance.ResetForTesting(); // (Q-S58) — paired cleanup
}
```

Both methods are idempotent + cheap (clear a Dictionary).

**Why not other options**:
1. Document "users must call Store.ResetForTesting() manually" — every project would write the same bug. Rejected.
2. Make Store auto-clear on scene unload via a separate singleton MonoBehaviour — adds machinery for what is one line of explicit pairing. Rejected.
3. **Pair the calls in Bootstrapper.OnDestroy** — adopted. One-line fix at the canonical cleanup site.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.5 Bootstrapper code (in EN spec): updated.
- Store.ResetForTesting (already exists since v0.1.4): unchanged.
- "Enter Play Mode Options (Fast)" workflow: now correctly cleans up.
- Test `BootstrapperStoreCleanupTests`: asserts post-OnDestroy Store is empty.

---

## Q-S59 — GetInstanceID() not network-deterministic (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:203` (Q-S28): `recommended: "$\"{template_id}_{GetInstanceID()}\""`
- `EN.md:2168` (Awake code): `_composed_persona.agent_id = $"{_composed_persona.agent_id}_{GetInstanceID()}"`
- Unity docs: GetInstanceID is unique within a session, NOT stable across save/load, NOT identical across hosts.

**Concrete bug**: Networked games where Bus payloads must match between client and server — e.g. server-driven AI replicated to clients, or peer-to-peer NPC simulation — see different `agent_id` values per host. `animo_goblin_47291_flee` on Host A is `animo_goblin_53921_flee` on Host B for the same logical NPC. Bus listeners can't correlate. Multi-client games visibly desync.

**Resolution**: Add explicit warning in §11.4.1 Awake code:
- The recommended formula is single-session-correct.
- Networked / save-load-stable hosts MUST substitute a deterministic id source: `NetworkObject.NetworkObjectId` (Unity Netcode), server-assigned UUID, ECS entity id with stable mapping.
- The spec leaves the choice to the host-adapter layer precisely for this reason — multiplayer hosts can pick a network-safe strategy without forking Engine.

This is documentation strengthening, not a code change. The host-adapter layer was already the right architectural location; the warning makes it explicit where the obvious default fails.

**Why not other options**:
1. Change the default to UUID — UUID requires extra dependency for a feature most single-player games don't need. Rejected.
2. Make the spec require multiplayer hosts to declare strategy — out of scope for v0.1.5. Rejected.
3. **Explicit warning in §11.4.1** — adopted. Documents the limitation; preserves the simple default for single-player.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 Awake code: warning comment block added before the override line.
- §3.1 + ヘッダー Theme 表: Q-S59 paragraph added.
- Q-S28 contract: unchanged (still leaves choice to host adapter).
- v0.2 multiplayer guide: future doc; will reference Q-S59 as the design hook.

---

## Q-S60 — ScenarioRunner internal Dictionary is dead structure for v0.1.5 (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2099` (pre-Q-S60 Q-S50): `runner maintains its own internal Dictionary<string, Engine> keyed by the (overridden) agent_id for routing Affect and Lock calls`
- `Run(string agent_id, ...)` signature accepts ONE template id.
- `TimedAffectEvent` has fields `time, ev` — no target-agent field.
- Therefore the Dictionary always has exactly one entry; "routing" is moot.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S50 over-spec'd internal storage as if multi-agent routing were already a feature. Phase 3 implementer reading the spec might allocate the Dictionary, write per-agent routing code, then realize the API only accepts one agent — leaving dead code that is harder to maintain than the actual single-agent runner.

**Resolution**: Pin v0.1.5's Runner internal field to a single `Engine _engine`. Document the type signature change for v0.2:

> When v0.2 adds multi-agent `Run()` (e.g. `Run(IReadOnlyList<(string template_id, string agent_id_override)> agents, ...)`), the field becomes a `Dictionary<string, Engine>` keyed by override-agent_id; the type changes when the API does, not before.

Q-S42's runtime-unique override on Runner has a different (and still valid) purpose: it gives `expanded_action_change` Bus payloads per-run identifiers in trace output, so multi-`Run()` aggregations can distinguish frame events from different runs even when they're not concurrent.

**Why not other options**:
1. Keep the Dictionary as pre-emptive future-proofing — unused complexity is a maintenance burden. Rejected.
2. Add a multi-agent Run() in v0.1.5 to justify the Dictionary — scope creep. Rejected.
3. **Single Engine field for v0.1.5; type evolves with API** — adopted. YAGNI applied correctly.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 prose: "routing dictionary" → "single Engine field".
- Q-S42 override purpose: per-run trace identifiers (still valid).
- Q-S50 ScenarioRunner-Store independence: still correct (Store is unrelated to single-vs-dictionary).
- Test `ScenarioRunnerSingleEngineFieldTests`: asserts internal field is single Engine.

---

## Q-S61 — Persona-first inheritance is not subtractive (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1267` (Q-S19 actions row): `start from persona.actions[]; for each Kind action whose id does not appear in the Persona, append at the tail`
- No mention of "Persona can omit a Kind Action to remove it".
- Behavior: Kind has `[A, B, C]`, Persona has `[A]`. Q-S19 result: Persona has `[A, B, C]` (B and C appended).

**Concrete bug**: Author intent could be "start from Kind A, but my Persona doesn't want Action B". Current spec gives no way to express that — Q-S19 always appends Kind Actions whose `id` is not in Persona.

But this is ALSO an intentional safety property: a child Persona cannot accidentally remove a Kind's `Idle` (or other critical fallback) by forgetting to mention it. The behavior is correct; the spec just didn't say so.

**Resolution**: Add explicit design note to §8.3 actions row and §3.1:
- Inheritance is **additive, never subtractive**.
- A child Persona cannot remove a Kind Action by omission.
- To author "use Kind A but without one of its Actions", split Kind A into Kind A_core (without that Action) and Kind A_extra (with it); inherit only the slice you need.
- This keeps removal explicit at the JSON-authoring layer where it is reviewable.

This is documentation strengthening, not a code change. The behavior is the safe-by-construction choice.

**Why not other options**:
1. Add a `kind_action_omissions: ["B"]` field — adds API surface for an edge case. Risks accidental over-removal. Rejected.
2. Change Q-S19 to "Kind action included only if explicitly listed in Persona" — breaks every existing JSON; child Personas would suddenly lose all Kind Actions. Rejected.
3. **Document additive-only as design property + offer Kind-split workaround** — adopted. Behavior preserved; intent clarified; workaround offered.

**Cross-impact**:
- §8.3 actions row: design note added (membership is additive-only).
- §3.1 + ヘッダー Theme 表: Q-S61 paragraph.
- Q-S19 contract: unchanged.
- JSON authoring guide (future): Kind-splitting pattern.

---

## Q-S62 — Hard Lock Step 4 appears wasteful (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:4147-4153` (Lock × Step table): Hard lock runs Step 1, 2, 3, 4; skips Step 5.
- `EN.md:4256` (post-table): Justifies Step 3 (Bus signals must keep firing) but says nothing about Step 4 (which produces scores nobody uses while locked).

**Concrete bug**: Reader (or a performance-conscious implementer) reasonably asks "if Step 5 doesn't switch, why is Step 4 still calculating scores? Wasted work per locked frame."

The behavior is correct; the rationale is missing. Three reasons make Step 4 the right design despite Step 5 ignoring its output:

1. **`commitment.bonus` continuity for the post-unlock frame.** The instant the lock expires, Step 5 in the same `Live(dt)` reads `_action_scores[locked_behavior_index]`. If Step 4 had been skipping calculation throughout the lock, the score would be stale; the post-unlock decision would pivot on whatever stale frame happened to be the last pre-lock one — defeating the smooth-out-of-lock guarantee in §24.2.
2. **Trace observability.** With Trace mode on, `TraceFrame.action_scores` records all action scores per frame, including locked-behavior frames. Authors debug "why did the boss flip to Flee one frame after unlock?" by reading this trace; skipping Step 4 would blank the frames most worth inspecting.
3. **Deterministic 5-step structure.** Conditionally skipping interior steps based on Lock state introduces branching that has to be re-justified for every future feature interaction (Threshold under Lock, Affect during Lock, etc.). Skip only Step 5 keeps the pipeline straightforward.

**Resolution**: Add the three-reason rationale to §24.3 in the Lock × Step table area. Documentation strengthening only.

**Cost**: One Step 4 pass per frame per locked agent. With 100 agents × 60 fps × ~10 actions, ~60 000 score calculations per second. Negligible against modern CPU budgets, and entirely absent on unlocked frames.

**Why not other options**:
1. Skip Step 4 under Hard lock — breaks all three reasons above. Rejected.
2. Skip Step 4 only when Trace mode is off — branching for branching's sake; Bus-driven subscribers might also rely on continuous score data. Rejected.
3. **Document the three reasons + accept the cost** — adopted. Correct behavior; clear rationale.

**Cross-impact**:
- §24.3 Lock × Step section: rationale paragraph added after the existing Step 3 justification.
- §3.1 + ヘッダー Theme 表: Q-S62 paragraph.
- Q-S2 Step 4 contract: unchanged.

## Q-S63 — Needs.Clamp() dead code with NotImplementedException trap (Gemini nineteenth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Data.cs:68` (pre-Q-S63): `public void Clamp() => throw new System.NotImplementedException();`
- §16.2 (v0.1.2): hot path uses flat `float[] _needs` with `Mathf.Clamp` directly, never instantiating Needs class.
- The Needs class survives only as a JSON-bridge shape (deserialization target).
- Engine code doesn't call `Needs.Clamp()`.

**Concrete bug**: A tool author exploring the API discovers `Needs.Clamp()`, calls it expecting "force values into [0, 100]", and gets `NotImplementedException`. Confusion ensues. The method has been dead code since v0.1.2 — a trap, not a feature.

**Resolution**: 
1. Remove `public void Clamp() => throw new System.NotImplementedException();` from `Scripts/Data.cs`.
2. Remove `+void Clamp()` from §6.1 class diagram.
3. Update §6.1 history table to mark Clamp as removed in v0.1.5 (was added in v0.1.1).
4. Update §6.1 namespace table description: "JSON-bridge shape (Q-S63 removed dead Clamp())".

The other two methods (`Get(need)`, `Normalized(need)`) also throw NotImplementedException but they have legitimate v0.2/v0.3 implementation paths (debug API entry points). Clamp had no such path because the hot path doesn't use the class.

**Why not other options**:
1. Implement Clamp on the Dictionary — adds a method nobody calls; still dead. Rejected.
2. Mark Clamp `[Obsolete]` and keep it — same trap, just noisier. Rejected.
3. **Remove it entirely** — adopted. Dead code deletion is the right answer.

**Cross-impact**:
- Data.cs: method removed.
- §6.1 class diagram: line removed.
- §6.1 history table: marked removed.
- §6.1 namespace table: description updated.
- Hot path: unchanged (already used flat `float[]`).

## Q-S64 — `Persona.DeepCopy()` undeclared (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2286` (Awake step 2): `_composed_persona = template.DeepCopy();`
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Persona` class has no `DeepCopy()` method declaration. Confirmed missing-method compile error.

**Concrete bug**: PersonaCache returns a shared composed template (§11.6 Q-S29 Flyweight). Without DeepCopy, two Agents spawned from the same template id share the same `Needs`, `actions[]`, `binding.thresholds[].expanded_trigger`, etc. One Agent's Q-S28 agent_id override would mutate the shared `_composed_persona.agent_id`, corrupting every sibling. The spec assumed `DeepCopy()` was provided by some unspecified mechanism (extension method? base class?) but neither existed.

**Resolution**: Add `public Persona DeepCopy() => throw new System.NotImplementedException();` to the `Persona` class in `Scripts/Data.cs`. Add `+Persona DeepCopy()` to §6.1 class diagram (EN + JP). Phase 3 implements deep clone of all reference-type fields: `Needs.values`, `Rates.values`, each `Influence`, each `Action` (and action-internal collections), `Commitment`, `Binding` (including `binding.thresholds[]` with their `expanded_trigger` strings), `needs_meta` dictionary entries.

**Why not other options**:
1. Use `MemberwiseClone` and document "shallow only" — defeats the per-Agent isolation purpose. Rejected.
2. Use C# `record with { ... }` syntax — requires Persona to be a `record`, breaking JSON deserialization compatibility. Rejected.
3. Use a `[Serializable]` round-trip — works but adds JSON serialize+deserialize cost per Agent spawn (heavy). Rejected.
4. **Explicit `DeepCopy()` method on Persona** — adopted. Symmetric with the rest of the API; Phase 3 has full control of which fields are deep-cloned vs shared.

**Cross-impact**:
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: declaration added.
- §6.1 class diagram: row added (EN + JP).
- §11.4.1 Awake step (2): comment annotated with Q-S64.
- Test `PersonaDeepCopyIsolationTests`: Red baseline asserts isolation post-Phase-3.

## Q-S65 — PHASE A `_persona.needs ?? new Dictionary` type mismatch (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:905`, `EN.md:927` (PHASE A two loops): `foreach (var kv in _persona.needs ?? new Dictionary<string, float>())`
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Needs` class wraps `Dictionary<string, float> values`; not a Dictionary itself.
- `??` operator requires LHS and RHS to be type-compatible; `Needs ?? Dictionary<...>` is rejected by compiler.

**Concrete bug**: The PHASE A code intends "iterate the Needs dictionary, falling back to empty if Persona has no Needs". But it grabs the wrapper class on the LHS instead of unwrapping to `.values`. Compile error.

**Resolution**: Change both PHASE A loops to:
```csharp
foreach (var kv in _persona.needs?.values ?? new Dictionary<string, float>()) { ... }
```
The `?.values` extracts the inner Dictionary safely (null-propagation if `Needs` is null), and `??` falls back to a fresh empty Dictionary.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make `Needs` itself `IEnumerable<KeyValuePair<string, float>>` — adds complexity to a JSON-bridge shape. Rejected.
2. Add an implicit conversion `Needs → Dictionary<string, float>` — surprising, hides the wrapping. Rejected.
3. **Unwrap to `.values` explicitly** — adopted. Local fix, no API change.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2 PHASE A two loops: corrected (EN + JP).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S65 paragraph added.
- Tests: `PhaseANeedsUnwrapTests` Phase 3 contract (Red baseline).

---

## Q-S66 — PHASE C `_composed_persona.needs.Count` self-introduced compile error (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:986-987` (Q-S56 PHASE C Step 3): `for (int idx = 0; idx < _composed_persona.needs.Count; idx++) { string need_name = _composed_persona.needs[idx]; }`
- `Scripts/Data.cs`: `Needs` class has neither `.Count` property nor an integer indexer.

**Concrete bug**: This is **Q-S56's self-inflicted compile error** introduced by Phase_2_4_16. Q-S56 separated the ApplyNonTierMetadata pass into "iterate all Needs in composed needs[]" — but wrote it as if `Needs` were `List<string>`. The `Needs` class is fundamentally a JSON-bridge wrapping `Dictionary<string, float> values`; it never had list-like semantics. Q-S56 did the right structural reshape but introduced fictional method calls.

**Resolution**: Iterate `_need_index` directly. PHASE A built `_need_index` as `Dictionary<string, int>` populated from composed needs ∪ needs_meta union — exactly the "every Need known to this Engine" canonical list. Each entry has the index already; no fragile re-derivation needed:

```csharp
foreach (var entry in _need_index) {
    string need_name = entry.Key;
    int    idx       = entry.Value;
    NeedMeta meta;
    if (_persona.needs_meta != null
        && _persona.needs_meta.TryGetValue(need_name, out var explicit_meta)) {
        meta = explicit_meta;
    } else {
        meta = NeedMeta.DefaultFor(need_name);
    }
    ApplyNonTierMetadata(idx, meta);
}
```

**Why not other options**:
1. Add `.Count` and `int` indexer to `Needs` — bloats a JSON shape with collection semantics. Rejected.
2. Build a separate `_composed_need_names: List<string>` field — duplicates `_need_index.Keys`. Rejected.
3. **Iterate `_need_index` map directly** — adopted. The canonical source already exists; Q-S66 just uses it.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2 PHASE C Step 3: rewritten (EN + JP).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S66 paragraph.
- Q-S56 contract preserved: every Need still receives `ApplyNonTierMetadata` (the goal).
- Tests: `PhaseCAllNeedsViaNeedIndexTests` Phase 3 contract.

## Q-S67 — `AffectEvent` type undeclared (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:4822`: `public AffectEvent ev { get; }` inside `TimedAffectEvent` struct.
- `EN.md` + `Scripts/`: `AffectEvent` class/struct/record declared NOWHERE.
- `EN.md:1247` §6.1 namespace table: lists `AffectEvent` in `Animo.Tools` since v0.1.4 — but the type itself never declared in any `.cs` file or spec code block.

**Concrete bug**: The §6.1 namespace table promised the type since v0.1.4. Subsequent Q-Ss referenced it (Q-S55, Q-S60). But the actual struct/class declaration never made it into a code block. `TimedAffectEvent.ev` field's type doesn't exist → missing-type compile error.

**Resolution**: Declare in §26.3:
```csharp
public readonly struct AffectEvent {
    public string need         { get; }
    public float  delta        { get; }
    public bool   force_reset  { get; }
    public AffectEvent(string need, float delta, bool force_reset = false) {
        this.need = need;
        this.delta = delta;
        this.force_reset = force_reset;
    }
}
```

Mirrors the argument tuple of `Engine.Affect(need, delta, force_reset)` (§9.4). Readonly struct = zero-alloc per event when stored in an array.

**Why not other options**:
1. Use `(string, float, bool)` tuple inline — loses self-documenting field names. Rejected.
2. Use `Animo.Core.AffectArgs` (some unrelated existing type) — no such type exists. Rejected.
3. **New readonly struct in `Animo.Tools`** — adopted. Matches the namespace table promise.

**Cross-impact**:
- §26.3 declaration added (EN + JP).
- §6.1 namespace table: still correct (no change needed).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S67 paragraph.
- Tests: `AffectEventStructDeclaredTests` (compile-only Green when struct ships).

## Q-S68 — `Agent : MonoBehaviour` missing IAnimoAgent (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2196`: `Animo.Store.Instance.Register(agent: this);` inside Agent.Awake.
- `Scripts/Store.cs:37`: `public void Register(IAnimoAgent agent)` — requires the interface.
- `Scripts/Store.cs:65`: `public interface IAnimoAgent { string agent_id { get; } }` — exists.
- Spec narrative: "Animo.Agent : MonoBehaviour" — no `IAnimoAgent` mention. Spec sample code: `// Animo.Agent.Awake (Q-S28 + Q-S34 + Q-S38)` — no class declaration line at all.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementer writing `public sealed class Agent : MonoBehaviour { ... }` would hit cannot-convert at the `Register(agent: this)` line. The fix is mechanical (add the interface), but the spec never said to.

**Resolution**: Make the class declaration explicit in §11.4.1 Awake:

```csharp
public sealed class Agent : MonoBehaviour, IAnimoAgent {
    [SerializeField] string _persona_template_id = "";
    [SerializeField] Germio.Bus? _bus = null;
    Persona _composed_persona = null!;
    Engine  _engine           = null!;

    public string agent_id => _composed_persona.agent_id;   // satisfies IAnimoAgent

    void Awake() { ... }
    void OnDestroy() { Animo.Store.Instance.Unregister(agent: this); }
}
```

The `agent_id` accessor delegates to the composed Persona's field (post-Q-S28 override). Trivial; reads through the runtime-unique value.

**Why not other options**:
1. Make `Store.Register` accept `MonoBehaviour` — breaks the interface boundary; non-Unity hosts couldn't use Store. Rejected.
2. Make Agent wrap an internal `IAnimoAgent` adapter — adds a shim for one method. Rejected.
3. **Declare `Agent : MonoBehaviour, IAnimoAgent` directly** — adopted. Trivial implementation; matches the existing interface design.

**Cross-impact**:
- §11.4.1 Agent class declaration: explicit (EN + JP).
- IAnimoAgent contract (Store.cs): unchanged (already correct).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S68 paragraph.
- Tests: `AgentImplementsIAnimoAgentTests` reflection assertion.

## Q-S69 — `_need_tier_indices` type clash between field declaration and ctor code (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:3680` (§16.6 fields table): `_need_tier_indices: Dictionary<int, int[]>`
- `EN.md:944` (PHASE C ctor): `_need_tier_indices = new Dictionary<int, List<int>>();` then `.Add(_need_index[meta.Key])`
- §16.1 zero-alloc rule explicitly demands `int[]` for Hot Path iteration over tier indices during `max_lower_tier_intensity` lookup.

**Concrete bug**: Field declaration says `int[]`, ctor builds `List<int>`, ctor calls `List<int>.Add()`. Phase 3 implementer either:
- Changes the field type to `List<int>` → violates §16.1 zero-alloc Hot Path rule.
- Changes the ctor type to `int[]` → can't `.Add()` to a fixed-size array; needs to know the count up-front, which requires a pre-pass over needs_meta.

**Resolution**: Keep `int[]` as the **field** type (correct for §16.1) and use a local **scratch** `Dictionary<int, List<int>>` during ctor. tier participation grows incrementally as `needs_meta` non-standard Needs join. At the end of PHASE C, finalize the scratch into the field by snapshotting each `List<int>` to a `new int[]`:

```csharp
var scratch_tier_indices = new Dictionary<int, List<int>>();
// Step 1: copy from static map
foreach (var kv in Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER) {
    scratch_tier_indices[kv.Key] = new List<int>(kv.Value);
}
// Step 2: extend with needs_meta non-standard Needs (List grows)
if (_persona.needs_meta != null) {
    foreach (var meta in _persona.needs_meta) {
        bool is_standard = Array.IndexOf(Const.STANDARD_NEEDS, meta.Key) >= 0;
        if (is_standard) continue;
        int tier = meta.Value.tier;
        if (!scratch_tier_indices.ContainsKey(tier)) {
            scratch_tier_indices[tier] = new List<int>();
        }
        scratch_tier_indices[tier].Add(_need_index[meta.Key]);
    }
}
// (Q-S69) Finalize: snapshot to int[] for §16.1 Hot Path iteration
_need_tier_indices = new Dictionary<int, int[]>();
foreach (var kv in scratch_tier_indices) {
    _need_tier_indices[kv.Key] = kv.Value.ToArray();
}
```

One alloc per tier at ctor time only (typically 5 entries). Hot Path iteration is then over `int[]` per §16.1 contract.

**Why not other options**:
1. Change the field to `Dictionary<int, List<int>>` — violates §16.1 zero-alloc Hot Path rule. Rejected.
2. Pre-count tier participation, build `int[]` directly — adds a pre-pass over needs_meta for marginal savings. Rejected.
3. **Scratch List during ctor + finalize to int[]** — adopted. Field type stays §16.1-compliant; ctor stays simple.

**Cross-impact**:
- §3.5.2 PHASE C: rewritten with scratch buffer + finalize (EN + JP).
- §16.6 fields table: row updated with Q-S69 finalize note (EN + JP).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S69 paragraph.
- Tests: `NeedTierIndicesIsIntArrayTests` reflection assertion.

---

## Q-S70 — `_lock_remaining` field undeclared (Gemini twentieth review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:1541` (§9.2 mermaid T0): `if (is_locked) _lock_remaining -= dt; if (_lock_remaining ≤ 0) Unlock()`
- `EN.md:1561` (T0 narrative): `on the frame where _lock_remaining reaches zero...`
- `EN.md` §16.6 fields table: NO `_lock_remaining` row.
- `Scripts/Engine.cs`: NO `_lock_remaining` field declaration.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementer writes the T0 timer phase code from §9.2 mermaid pseudocode, hits "_lock_remaining is not defined" compile error. The Lock mechanism (§24.1, v0.1.4) needs both the timer field AND the public `Lock(duration, mode)` / `Unlock()` API to work — Q-S70 closes the field-side gap.

**Resolution**: 
- `Scripts/Engine.cs`: `float _lock_remaining = 0.0f;` (initialized to 0 = no Lock active at spawn).
- `EN.md` + `JP.md` §16.6 fields table: row added describing the field's lifecycle (set by `Lock`, decremented by T0, cleared by Unlock or natural expiry).

**Why not other options**:
1. Use a `bool _is_locked + DateTime _lock_expiry_time` pair — DateTime is heap-allocated and time-zone-dependent. Rejected.
2. Use a separate `LockState` class — overengineering for one float. Rejected.
3. **Single `float _lock_remaining` field** — adopted. Matches the §9.2 mermaid pseudocode directly.

**Cross-impact**:
- `Scripts/Engine.cs`: field declared.
- §16.6 fields table: row added (EN + JP).
- §9.2 mermaid T0: now has a real field to decrement.
- §24.1 Lock(), Unlock() public API: writes to `_lock_remaining` (Phase 3 implementation).
- ヘッダー Theme + §3.1: Q-S70 paragraph.
- Tests: `LockRemainingFieldDeclaredTests` reflection assertion.

## Q-S71 — `Validator.ValidateStage2` missing (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2578` (PersonaCache call site): `var stage2 = Validator.ValidateStage2(composed: composed);`
- `Scripts/Validator.cs:52-58`: only `public static ValidationResult Validate(Root root)` declared. No `ValidateStage2`.

**Concrete bug**: PersonaCache calls a method that doesn't exist. Confirmed missing-method compile error.

**Resolution**: Add `public static ValidationResult ValidateStage2(Persona composed)` stub to Validator.cs with NotImplementedException body. The XML doc summarizes the rules it runs (A019/A025/A035/A036/A037/A038/A039 — Q-S39+Q-S41+Q-S47+Q-S49+Q-S57). Phase 3 implements the bodies.

**Cross-impact**: Validator.cs declares the stub. §11.6.1 PersonaCache call site is now buildable. §13.2.1 ValidateStage2 docstring (already in spec) matches the new declaration.

## Q-S74 — `has_errors` snake_case unified (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Validator.cs:41`: `public bool has_errors => throw new NotImplementedException();` (snake_case)
- `EN.md:2539` and `EN.md:2580`: `_validation.HasErrors` and `stage2.HasErrors` (PascalCase)
- Existing tests (`AssertResult.cs`, `NumericEdgeTests.cs`, `A028_CommitmentBonusWarnTests.cs`): all read `result.has_errors` (snake_case).

**Concrete bug**: C# is case-sensitive. The PascalCase reads from spec sample code would fail to find the property declared in snake_case.

**Resolution**: Unify on snake_case (the established Animo API surface convention — Persona.agent_id, Issue.rule_id, Threshold.expanded_trigger, etc.). Sed over EN+JP spec narrative replaces `HasErrors` → `has_errors`. Validator.cs unchanged; tests unchanged. ValidationResult class diagram (§13.2.1) updated to declare `public bool has_errors` consistently.

**Why snake_case (decision rationale)**:
- Matches every other Animo data class field (`agent_id`, `kind_ids`, `rule_id`, `trigger_threshold`, `force_reset`).
- Matches the JSON shape (Animo's JSON files are snake_case throughout).
- C# style guides recommend PascalCase for properties, but Animo's domain language is data-driven config, where snake_case is standard.
- Changing every existing test from `has_errors` to `HasErrors` would touch ~6 files; updating spec narrative touches 3 lines.

**Cross-impact**:
- `Scripts/Validator.cs`: no change (already snake_case).
- spec EN+JP §11.6.1 PersonaCache code: updated.
- spec EN §13.2.1 ValidationResult class diagram: updated.
- existing tests: no change (already snake_case).

## Q-S75 — `Agent._animator` field missing (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2453`: `_animator?.Play(stateName: trigger);`
- `EN.md:2378-2386` (Agent class declaration): only `_persona_template_id`, `_bus`, `_composed_persona`, `_engine`, `agent_id`. No `_animator`.

**Concrete bug**: Awake step (6) — the Q-S34/Q-S44 initial-behavior push to host Animator without going through Bus — references `_animator`, but the field is undeclared. Compile error.

**Resolution**: Add to Agent class field declarations:
```csharp
[SerializeField] Animator? _animator = null;
```

`SerializeField` exposes to Inspector; nullable `Animator?` lets the wire-up be optional (developers using ECS-driven mesh, custom shaders, or other View backends can leave it null). The `?.Play(...)` null-conditional operator at the call site makes the missing-Animator path a silent no-op rather than a NullReferenceException.

**Why optional, not required**:
- Animo is not Unity-Animator-bound. Hosts can use any View backend; Q-S34/Q-S44 just guarantees that *if* an Animator is wired, the spawn-time push happens with the same template-expanded payload as later Bus-routed transitions.
- A required field would force ECS hosts to instantiate a dummy Animator. Bad ergonomics.
- The `?.` operator is C# idiomatic for "do this only if the field is wired."

---

## Q-S76 — `Animo.Json.Parse` class missing (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2651`: `var root = Animo.Json.Parse(_animo_json.text);`
- `Scripts/`: no `Json.cs` file. No `class Json` anywhere.
- §6.1 namespace table: lists `Animo.Json` since v0.1.4 — promise unmet.

**Concrete bug**: Bootstrapper.Awake calls a class that doesn't exist. Confirmed missing-type compile error.

**Resolution**: New `Scripts/Json.cs`:
```csharp
namespace Animo {
    public static class Json {
        public static Root Parse(string text) {
            throw new System.NotImplementedException();
        }
    }
}
```

Phase 3 wraps Newtonsoft.Json or System.Text.Json depending on build profile. The stub is a thin facade — hosts that prefer a different JSON library can substitute by calling their library's deserializer directly in the bootstrapper.

**Why a wrapper, not direct Newtonsoft import**:
- Animo aims to be host-agnostic. Wrapping JSON as a one-method facade lets implementations swap without touching every caller.
- Phase 3 will pick the underlying serializer based on Unity version, IL2CPP constraints, and .NET runtime profile. A facade keeps Bootstrapper code stable across profiles.

## Q-S77 — Animo.asmdef + package.json missing (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Agent.cs` (spec sample at §11.4.1): `[SerializeField] Germio.Bus? _bus = null;`
- `Scripts/Animo.asmdef`: file does not exist.
- `package.json`: file does not exist.

**Concrete bug**: Agent references the Germio namespace but Animo.asmdef does not declare Germio as a reference. Phase 3 Unity build would fail to resolve `Germio.Bus`.

**Resolution**: Ship minimal asmdef:
```json
{
  "name": "Animo",
  "rootNamespace": "Animo",
  "references": ["Germio"],
  ...
}
```
Plus package.json with:
```json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "com.studiomeowtoon.germio": "0.1.0"
  }
}
```

Sufficient for the Germio cross-reference to resolve. Phase_2_5's broader asmdef polish (autoReferenced flags, definedConstraints, versionDefines, Editor split, Tools asmdef) builds on this foundation.

**Why ship now, not Phase_2_5**:
- Without the asmdef + package.json, Phase 3 cannot start in Unity at all (the Germio reference would fail on day 1).
- The minimal version is 12 lines total — negligible scope.
- Phase_2_5's broader work (Editor split, Tools split, version constraints) is independent enhancement, not blocker.

## Q-S78 — `Store.ResetForTesting` static-call form (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Store.cs:26`: `public static void ResetForTesting() { ... }` — static method.
- `EN.md:2658` (Q-S58 fix): `Animo.Store.Instance.ResetForTesting();` — invokes via instance reference.

**Concrete bug**: C# CS0176 (Compiler Error CS0176): "Member is accessed through an instance; qualify it with a type name instead." Static members must be invoked via the type name, not via an instance reference.

**Resolution**: Correct the call to type-name form:
```csharp
Animo.Store.ResetForTesting();   // (Q-S58 + Q-S78) — type-name form
```

Q-S58's design intent — pair Store cleanup with PersonaCache cleanup — is unchanged. Only the syntax is fixed.

**Why ResetForTesting is static, not instance**:
- The Store singleton's `_agents` Dictionary is a static field on the singleton type (since Singleton-by-static is the C# idiom Animo uses).
- ResetForTesting clears that static state — by definition, it operates on the type, not an instance.
- Phase_2_4_8 introduced this method as static; Q-S58 (Phase_2_4_16) accidentally wrote the call as if it were instance-bound.

## Q-S79 — `Scripts/PersonaCache.cs` file missing (Gemini twenty-first review, 2026-05-10)

**Verified by grep**:
- `EN.md:2520-2620` (§11.6.1): full PersonaCache implementation as spec text.
- `EN.md:2295` (Agent.Awake): `template = Animo.PersonaCache.GetComposed(template_id: _persona_template_id);`
- `Scripts/`: no `PersonaCache.cs` file.

**Concrete bug**: `Animo.PersonaCache` type does not exist at compile time. Agent.Awake's call site fails to resolve the type. Confirmed missing-type compile error.

**Resolution**: Materialize `Scripts/PersonaCache.cs` with method declarations matching §11.6.1's signatures:
```csharp
public static class PersonaCache {
    static Root? _root;
    static ValidationResult? _validation;
    static readonly Dictionary<string, Persona> _cache = new();

    public static void Initialize(Root root) { throw new NotImplementedException(); }
    public static Persona GetComposed(string template_id) { throw new NotImplementedException(); }
    public static void ClearForTesting() {
        _root = null;
        _validation = null;
        _cache.Clear();
    }
}
```

`ClearForTesting` is implemented inline (3 lines) since the test infrastructure has used it since Phase_2_4_x for state cleanup between test cases. The other two methods throw NotImplementedException for Phase 3 to flesh out.

**Cross-impact**:
- New `Scripts/PersonaCache.cs` file.
- §17 Repository Layout: PersonaCache.cs row added (EN + JP).
- All §11.6 references in the spec now resolve.
- Test `PersonaCacheFileExistsTests`: simple file-existence + reflection assertion (Green when file ships).

## Phase_2_4_19 — Gemini 22nd review (12 attacks → 9 adopted, 3 hallucinations rejected)

This phase introduces a new pattern in the protocol: **graceful rejection of hallucinations with grep evidence**. After 21 rounds of grep-verified adoption, Master's discipline matured to "verify each claim before implementing" rather than the inadvertent "always-adopt-everything-Gemini-says" trap. Phase_2_4_19 is the first phase to formally record hallucinations alongside adopted resolutions.

### Hallucinations rejected (recorded for forensic transparency)

#### REJECTED #1 — `_persona.needs.Keys at line 1435`

**Gemini's claim**: §16-area code contained `int extra = _persona.needs.Keys.Where(...).Count();` as a Q-S65 modification gap. The framing was confident: "1435行目を見てください."

**Grep verification**:
```
grep -n "_persona.needs.Keys" docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_JP.md
# Result: ZERO hits.
```

**Verdict**: The line in question (1435) is part of a different code block. Q-S65 has fixed every PHASE A loop; the regex `_persona.needs.Keys` does not appear anywhere in the spec.

**Action**: Recorded as hallucination. No spec change.

#### REJECTED #2 — `Engine.cs missing using System.Linq`

**Gemini's claim**: Cascading from #1, "Engine.cs needs `using System.Linq;` because the line-1435 LINQ call wouldn't resolve." Confident framing: "覚悟！"

**Grep verification**:
```
grep -n "Where\\|Linq" Scripts/Engine.cs
# Result: ZERO hits — Engine.cs uses no LINQ.
```

**The `.Where()` Gemini cited** is in §26 ScenarioRunner *usage documentation*, not Engine.cs. The Engine class is implemented as a pure flat-array hot path (zero LINQ, zero allocations); §16.1 zero-overhead rule explicitly forbids LINQ in the hot path.

**Verdict**: Chain-rejection of a fabricated dependency. Phase 3 may legitimately add `using System.Linq;` to Engine.cs in the future if implementation requires it, but no current spec text demands it.

**Action**: Recorded as cascade hallucination from #1.

#### REJECTED #4 — Agent public properties `behavior`, `is_locked`, `locked_behavior`

**Gemini's claim**: §6.3 / Task 4-1-h "明記" that Agent must expose `behavior`, `is_locked`, `locked_behavior` plus `agent_id` as public properties.

**Grep verification**:
```
grep -n "Public properties\\|behavior, is_locked, locked_behavior" docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md
# §6.3: NO matching requirement.
```

**Reality**: `behavior`, `is_locked`, `locked_behavior` are declared on `Animo.Core.Engine` in §3.4 (Engine API surface). The Agent (MonoBehaviour wrapper) only needs to satisfy `IAnimoAgent.agent_id` — the interface contract from `Scripts/Store.cs:65`.

If a host needs to read engine state from outside the Agent, the conventional Unity pattern is to expose the engine reference (`public Engine engine => _engine;`) or to subscribe to `_engine.OnSignal` — both are existing patterns in the spec. Forcing Agent to mirror Engine's full public surface would couple the two layers redundantly and violate §11.4 ("Agent is the host adapter; Engine is the algorithm").

**Verdict**: Confusion of Engine API surface for Agent API surface. The properties Gemini named exist on Engine, not on Agent.

**Action**: Recorded as hallucination based on category error (Engine vs Agent layer confusion).

### Adopted resolutions (Q-S80–Q-S88)

#### Q-S80 — Agent.Update missing

§11.4.1 sample code declared only Awake() and OnDestroy(). NPCs would seed initial behavior in Awake, then freeze forever — no Live(dt) on subsequent frames. The whole pipeline (decay → effective → threshold → score → switch) was unreachable from the Unity adapter.

**Resolution**: Added `void Update() { _engine.Live(dt: Time.deltaTime); }` to §11.4.1 EN+JP, matching `Scripts/Agent.cs` Update stub (Q-S83).

**Cross-impact**: Agent.Update is the only frame-driver. ScenarioRunner has its own equivalent loop in §26.3.1.

#### Q-S81 — Store.Unregister signature mismatch

`Scripts/Store.cs:42` declares `public void Unregister(IAnimoAgent agent)`. §11.2.2 spec sample wrote `public void Unregister(Animo.Agent agent)`. Phase 3 implementing the spec text would have created a new overload that does NOT satisfy IAnimoAgent — leaving the interface's Unregister wire dangling.

**Resolution**: Unified spec sample on the interface form across EN+JP.

**Cross-impact**: Symmetric to Q-S6 Register signature, which already uses IAnimoAgent. Both sides of the registration lifecycle now align with the interface.

#### Q-S82 — Scripts/Tools/ scaffolding missing

§26.3 contained ScenarioRunner + TraceResult + AffectEvent + TimedAffectEvent as spec text but the directory did not exist. Phase 3 had no source files to compile for the Animo.Tools assembly.

**Resolution**: Created `Scripts/Tools/`:
- `Animo.Tools.asmdef` referencing Animo
- `TraceResult.cs` with TraceFrame + TraceResult class declarations
- `ScenarioRunner.cs` with AffectEvent + TimedAffectEvent structs (Q-S67) and ScenarioRunner class with Run(...) declaration matching §26.3

**Cross-impact**: §17 Repository Layout updated EN+JP to show Tools under Scripts/. animo-runner~ CLI project (separate) references Animo.Tools.dll for headless simulation.

#### Q-S83 — Scripts/Agent.cs missing

§11.4.1 described the Agent MonoBehaviour as spec text. Q-S29/Q-S68/Q-S75/Q-S80 piled features on it. But the file did not exist.

**Resolution**: Created `Scripts/Agent.cs` bracketed in `#if UNITY_5_3_OR_NEWER` so headless dotnet test still compiles. Field declarations + Awake/Update/OnDestroy method stubs all match §11.4.1.

**Cross-impact**: §17 layout updated. The `#if` is necessary because Agent depends on UnityEngine + Germio (Q-S77 asmdef references them), but tests run on .NET 8 without UnityEngine.

#### Q-S84 — ScenarioRunner integer step counter

§26.3.1 wrote `while (current_time < duration) { ... current_time += dt; }`. Repeated `float += dt` accumulates IEEE-754 round-off; over thousands of iterations, current_time drifts ~1e-5 from truth, occasionally causing the predicate to evaluate one iteration off the Q-S35-promised `floor(duration / dt)` total.

**Q-S35 had been mathematically false.**

**Resolution**: Switch to integer step counter:
```csharp
int total_steps = (int)Math.Floor(duration / dt);
for (int i = 0; i < total_steps; i++) {
    float frame_end = (i + 1) * dt;
    // ... events sweep with frame_end ...
    engine.Live(dt);
    RecordTraceFrame(time: frame_end);
}
```

`current_time` is reconstructed as `(i + 1) * dt` for trace records — also preferable for trace consumers because the float values match what authors wrote (`0.1f`, `0.2f`, ...) instead of accumulated drift values.

**Cross-impact**: Q-S35's "exactly floor(duration / dt) Live calls" contract becomes mathematically guaranteed. Q-S40's post-loop sweep is unchanged.

#### Q-S85 — ThresholdsMatch first-occurrence-wins

§8.3.1's `Math.Abs(a-b) < THRESHOLD_KEY_EPSILON` is **not transitive**: A=80.000, B=80.006, C=80.012 has A≈B and B≈C but A≉C. Without an order-handling rule, Composer.MergeThresholds would non-deterministically collapse C depending on input order.

**Resolution**: Codified first-occurrence-wins semantics in the merge loop:
```csharp
foreach (var p_threshold in persona.binding.thresholds) {
    int found = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < merged.Count; i++) {   // first-occurrence wins
        if (ThresholdsMatch(merged[i], p_threshold)) {
            found = i;
            break;
        }
    }
    if (found >= 0) merged[found] = p_threshold;
    else            merged.Add(p_threshold);
}
```

Persona priority preserved (Persona's match always overrides the first-encountered Kind threshold). A039 surfaces sibling-pair Warnings at validate-time so authors can confirm intent.

**Cross-impact**: Composer.cs Compose docstring updated to document the first-occurrence rule. Order is now part of the merge contract.

#### Q-S86 — Step3 hot-path null-coalesce removed

§16.5 Step3_Thresholds wrote `t.reset_threshold ?? Math.Max(0f, t.trigger_threshold - 5f);` per frame. But Q-S11 contracts that Composer.Compose ALWAYS fills `reset_threshold` with the same `Math.Max` formula if author omitted it. By the time Hot Path runs, `reset_threshold` is **never null** — the per-frame `??` was pure dead code violating §16.1's zero-overhead Hot Path rule.

**Resolution**: Replaced with `t.reset_threshold!.Value`. The null-forgiving `!` is safe per Q-S11 contract; a violation would surface as NRE on the FIRST frame, not silent wrong-value.

**Cross-impact**: Composer.cs docstring documents the contract; Engine.cs Affect() neighborhood comment documents the guarantee. Phase 3 implementer of Composer must respect Q-S11 + Q-S86.

#### Q-S87 — MockScene scratch-buffer

`Tests~/MiniUnity/MockScene.cs` Tick allocated `_objects.ToArray()` and `new MockMonoBehaviour[comps.Count]` every frame. A 1-hour Soak Test (216,000 frames @ 60fps) burnt ~432,000 array allocations in the test infrastructure alone, defeating the very Zero-GC contract the harness exists to verify.

**Resolution**: Introduced two reusable List<T> scratch fields with Clear() + AddRange() semantics. Backing arrays grow to peak capacity then stop allocating.

**Cross-impact**: Q-S21 zombie-Update protection (snapshot-then-iterate) preserved exactly. The test layer's snapshot semantics are unchanged from a behavior standpoint.

#### Q-S88 — §16.2.2.1 Q-S27 conceptual sketch marker

§16.2.2.1 contained a Q-S27 explanatory pseudocode parallel to §3.5.2 PHASE A canonical ctor — readers had to reconcile two `_effective_needs = new float[...]` declarations.

**Resolution**: Marked §16.2.2.1's snippet as "conceptual sketch only" with explicit pointer "canonical implementation: §3.5.2 PHASE A". No code change; documentation clarification only.

**Cross-impact**: §3.5.2 PHASE A becomes the unambiguous source of truth for Engine ctor sequence. §16.2.2.1 retains educational value for the Q-S27 fixed-slot rule without competing as a separate spec.

### N-Round Self-Consistency Review (Phase_2_4_19)

- **Round 1**: 2 contradictions caught and fixed.
  - R1-A: README missing Q-S80-S88 cumulative paragraph (added).
  - R1-B: Composer.cs / Engine.cs lacking Q-S85 / Q-S86 contract comments (added).
- **Round 2** (deeper grep — actual C# code blocks vs comment-citations): 0 contradictions. All `Animo.Agent agent`, `reset_threshold ?? Math.Max`, `while (current_time < duration)` mentions in code blocks are inside historical-citation comments documenting the pre-fix narrative. The hallucination targets (`_persona.needs.Keys`, etc.) confirmed at 0 hits.
- **Round 3**: 0 contradictions. Mermaid 6 known mindmap blocks fail (unchanged from prior phases — known limitation of the mermaid validator we use).
- **Round 4**: FIXPOINT REACHED. Zero new contradictions on EN+JP+code+test grep cross-checks.

Total contradictions caught across 4 rounds: 2.
Total fixes applied within Phase_2_4_19: 2.

## Phase_2_4_20 — Gemini 23rd review (6 attacks, 6 adopted, 0 hallucinations)

After Phase_2_4_19's mixed round (9 true / 3 hallucinations), Gemini's 23rd review was tighter — six attacks, all six grep-verified true. The pattern of "spec promised X / repository missing X" dominated: schema declared a feature but didn't allow it, decision log decided a field but the file omitted it, spec narrative named a package but the manifest renamed it. Master's grep-first discipline applied here was straightforward: every claim cited a specific file and line that, on inspection, showed exactly the gap Gemini described.

### Q-S89 — `needs_meta` schema property declaration

**Verified by grep**:
- `Schemas/animo.schema.json:202-222` (`kind`): `additionalProperties: false`, properties list has no `needs_meta`.
- Same for `persona:224-255`.
- Q-S30 introduced `needs_meta` to the data model; Phase_2_4_18 (Q-S77) declared `needs_meta_map` reference in §14.2 spec text but the schema was never updated.

**Concrete bug**: Any spec-compliant `needs_meta` JSON block fails ajv before reaching the C# Validator. The whole Q-S30 LLM-author-genre-customization path is schema-blocked at the gate.

**Resolution**: Added `needs_meta_map` definition (snake_case keys, `need_meta` values), `need_meta` definition (required `tier` ∈ [1, 5]), and `needs_meta` property to both `kind.properties` and `persona.properties`.

**Cross-impact**: schema_version bumped to 1.5 implicitly (the v0.1.5 spec already references "schema_version: 1.3 / 1.4 / 1.5" in §17 Layout). C# Validator stage 2 (A038) verifies tier range; schema validates structure.

### Q-S90 — Stage 2 tests call `ValidateStage2`, not `Validate`

**Verified by grep**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A025_GhostCycleStage2Tests.cs:56`: `Validator.Validate(root: root)`.
- `A035_PostComposeTriggerGtResetTests.cs:53, 77`: same.
- `A036_ComposedActionsEmptyTests.cs:53, 79`: same.
- `A037_MultiEdgeSameTargetTests.cs:51, 72`: same.
- 4 files × ~1.5 cases = 6 sites total all calling Stage 1 entry.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S71 split Validator into `Validate(Root)` (Stage 1 — schema-shape rules: A000-A018 + most of A019-A032) and `ValidateStage2(Persona composed)` (Stage 2 — post-composition rules: A025 ghost-cycle, A035 trigger-vs-reset post-fill, A036 composed-actions-empty, A037 multi-edge-same-target). The 4 test files target Stage 2 rules but call only the Stage 1 entry — the rules under test never fire, the tests stay Red FOREVER even when Phase 3 implements the Stage 2 entry correctly.

**Resolution**: Each case now first calls `Composer.Compose(persona, root)` to build the composed Persona, then `Validator.ValidateStage2(composed)` to run Stage 2.

**Cross-impact**: Test infrastructure now exercises both Validator entries. The composed-Persona construction is the same step PersonaCache.Initialize takes per template, so the test path mirrors the production path.

### Q-S91 — EditMode asmdef references `Animo.Tools`

**Verified by grep**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Animo.Tests.EditMode.asmdef`: `"references": ["Animo", "Animo.Tests.MiniUnity"]` — no `Animo.Tools`.
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Tools/`: 12 test files use `Animo.Tools.ScenarioRunner`, `TraceResult`, `TraceFrame`, `AffectEvent`, `TimedAffectEvent`.

**Concrete bug**: Unity Editor respects asmdef boundaries strictly. Without the reference, all 12 Tools tests fail compilation. (Headless dotnet test happened to work because Animo.csproj packs Scripts/Tools/*.cs into the same Animo.dll, but Unity's runtime asmdef enforcement is stricter.)

**Resolution**: Added `"Animo.Tools"` to the references array.

**Cross-impact**: One-line asmdef change. Unity Editor will now compile all Tools tests. Phase_2_5's asmdef polish (autoReferenced flags, etc.) builds on this.

### Q-S92 — `ScenarioRunner._engine` field declared

**Verified by grep**:
- Q-S60 decision text: "the runner's internal field is a single `Engine _engine`" (decision log line ~1042).
- `Scripts/Tools/ScenarioRunner.cs:56`: `readonly Root _root;` only — no `_engine`.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S60 was a spec-narrative decision; Q-S82's file materialization (Phase_2_4_19) implemented `_root` but skipped `_engine`. Phase 3 implementer building `Run()` and assigning to `_engine` would hit a compile error.

**Resolution**: Added `Engine? _engine;` to ScenarioRunner with comment cross-referencing Q-S60's design rationale and the deferred clause "type changes when API does, not before."

**Cross-impact**: Phase 3 `Run(string agent_id, ...)` body assigns `_engine = new Engine(...)` per call. Multi-Run reuse is clean (re-assignment, not pooling). When v0.2 adds multi-agent `Run()` per Q-S60's deferred clause, `_engine` becomes `Dictionary<string, Engine>` — type changes with API.

### Q-S93 — `TraceResult` analysis API materialized

**Verified by grep**:
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:5269-5273`: `behavior_count`, `behavior_total_time`, `ToCsv()`, `ToJson()` declared.
- `Scripts/Tools/TraceResult.cs`: only `agent_id`, `duration`, `dt`, `frames`. Analysis surface absent.

**Concrete bug**: Spec consumers (regression baselines, occupancy queries, CSV exports) had no API to call. The example test in §26.5 `Assert.True(result.behavior_total_time["Flee"] > 5.0f)` would not compile.

**Resolution**: Added all 4 spec-promised members. Properties get default-empty Dictionaries (Phase 3 populates by single-pass over `frames`); `ToCsv()` and `ToJson()` throw NotImplementedException.

**Cross-impact**: Phase 3 implementer of `ScenarioRunner.Run` populates `behavior_count` + `behavior_total_time` at the END of Run (not per-frame — single pass at end is cheaper and avoids per-frame Dictionary churn). Serialization libraries (Newtonsoft.Json or System.Text.Json) drive `ToJson` body.

### Q-S94 — package namespace unified to `com.studiomeowtoon.*`

**Verified by grep**:
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:79` (Roadmap): `com.meowtoon.animo`.
- Same file lines 4103, 4126, 4136-4137, 4144, 4430: `com.meowtoon.{animo,germio,briko,utilo}`.
- Same in JP at 8 corresponding lines.
- `package.json`: `"name": "com.studiomeowtoon.animo"`, `"dependencies": { "com.studiomeowtoon.germio": "0.1.0" }`.
- Q-S77 (Phase_2_4_18) shipped the latter format.

**Concrete bug**: UPM (Unity Package Manager) cannot resolve dependencies when spec narrative names a package one way and the manifest names it another. The whole G+B+A install path breaks at `com.meowtoon.germio` lookup → not found → Animo install fails.

**Resolution**: sed-unified spec EN+JP narrative on `com.studiomeowtoon.*` (the implementation side, matching the `STUDIO MeowToon` author identity collapsed to lowercase).

**Why studiomeowtoon, not meowtoon**:
- The `package.json` manifest already shipped at Q-S77 with `studiomeowtoon`. Changing the manifest would require updating any installation that already pulled the package from Q-S77.
- The author block in `package.json` is `"name": "h.adachi (STUDIO MeowToon)"` — the studio name has STUDIO + MeowToon, two words. UPM convention is lowercase, no spaces; collapsing to one word is `studiomeowtoon`. Matches author identity.
- Germio repository (the cross-package dependency) shipped its own package.json with `com.studiomeowtoon.germio`. Following Germio's convention is the only way Animo's `dependencies` section in package.json resolves.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP narrative across §1.2 Roadmap + §17 Layout + §1.3 mermaid diagrams + §17 dependency-tree examples — 8 sed-target lines per language. Q-S77's prior fix (asmdef + package.json) is unchanged. The implementation side was correct; the spec narrative was the drift to correct.

## Phase_2_4_21 — Gemini 24th review (6 attacks, 6 adopted, 0 hallucinations) — Q-S100 CENTENNIAL

After Phase_2_4_20's clean 6/6 round, Gemini's 24th review again delivered 6 attacks all grep-verified true. The protocol crosses **Q-S100** — the centennial Q-S finding — with a sustained record of 100 grep-verified Master-vs-Gemini findings since Q-S1, all integrated into the spec without a single phantom fix. Gemini's pattern this round: "missed during Q-S X / Y / Z's earlier sweep" — five of six fixes patch gaps that earlier Q-S decisions implicitly created when their cross-impact analysis was incomplete.

### Q-S95 — A019 test calls `ValidateStage2`, not `Validate`

**Verified by grep**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A019_TypoNeedsKeyTests.cs:21, 26, 31`: all 3 cases call `Validator.Validate(root: root)` — Stage 1 entry only.
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:214` (Q-S39 row): "A019 (typo Warning for unknown Need keys) now runs in **Stage 2** against the COMPOSED Persona."
- Q-S90 (Phase_2_4_20) fixed the same Stage 1/Stage 2 mismatch for A025/A035/A036/A037 but did NOT cover A019.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S39 moved A019 to Stage 2 so Persona-level `needs_meta` could suppress false-positives. The test never updated its call site. Phase 3 implementing the Q-S39 Stage 2 rule correctly would still see Red on this file forever — the rule under test never fires.

**Resolution**: Each of the 3 cases now first calls `Composer.Compose(persona, root)` to build the composed Persona, then `Validator.ValidateStage2(composed)` to run Stage 2.

**Cross-impact**: Q-S90's coverage list now extends to include A019. The pattern of Phase_2_4_20's Stage 1 → Stage 2 sweep is now complete: every Stage 2 rule's test file calls ValidateStage2.

**Why Q-S90 missed A019**: Q-S39 (added Phase_2_4_13) moved A019 BEFORE Q-S71 (Phase_2_4_18) introduced the explicit `Validate(Root)` / `ValidateStage2(Persona)` split. The A019 test was written before the split existed; Q-S90 was scanning for the post-split Stage 2 rules (A025/A035/A036/A037 introduced in Phase_2_4_15+) and didn't go back to A019. Q-S95 closes the historical gap.

### Q-S96 — Agent.OnDestroy NRE land mine

**Verified by grep**:
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:2656`: `Persona _composed_persona = null!;`
- `:2662`: `public string agent_id => _composed_persona.agent_id;` (pre-Q-S96)
- `:2673-2680`: Awake catch block sets `enabled = false; return;` — `_composed_persona` stays null.
- `:2748-2750`: OnDestroy calls `Store.Instance.Unregister(agent: this)`.
- `Scripts/Store.cs:42`: `Unregister(IAnimoAgent agent)` reads `agent.agent_id`.

**Concrete bug**: Unity calls `OnDestroy` on a disabled MonoBehaviour at scene unload. The chain of dereferences is:
1. `OnDestroy` → `Store.Instance.Unregister(this)`
2. `Store.Unregister` → reads `agent.agent_id`
3. `agent_id` getter → reads `_composed_persona.agent_id`
4. `_composed_persona` is null → `NullReferenceException`

This breaks Q-S38's promise: "fail-loud but keep the rest of the scene alive." The very OnDestroy that was supposed to clean up the Agent's registration crashes the scene unload.

**Resolution**: Defense in depth.
1. Make `agent_id` getter null-safe: `_composed_persona?.agent_id ?? "<uninitialized>"`. The sentinel string never collides with real ids (snake_case rule forbids angle brackets).
2. Add early-return in OnDestroy when `_composed_persona == null`. Avoids logging "agent_id not registered" noise for every Awake-failed Agent.

**Why getter null-safe + OnDestroy guard, not just one**:
- The getter is part of `IAnimoAgent` (the public interface). Other call sites (debugging tools, Bus tracing, future custom adapters) might also dereference it on a partially-initialized Agent. Null-safe getter protects all callers.
- The OnDestroy guard is the operational fix for the specific Q-S38 failure path. Without it, the getter null-safe alone produces a "<uninitialized>" key passed to Store.Unregister, which logs a warning that we don't actually want for the expected fail-loud case.

**Cross-impact**: §11.4.1 EN+JP updated. Q-S38 fail-loud guarantee is now mathematically real — Awake-failed Agent is silent at scene unload AND doesn't NRE.

### Q-S97 — Scripts/AnimoBootstrapper.cs materialized

**Verified by grep**:
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:2925-2980`: full AnimoBootstrapper class as spec text.
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Bootstrapper/BootstrapperStoreCleanupTests.cs`: Phase 3 contract test.
- `Scripts/AnimoBootstrapper.cs`: file does not exist.

**Concrete bug**: Same physical-gap pattern as Q-S83 (Agent.cs), Q-S82 (ScenarioRunner.cs/TraceResult.cs), Q-S79 (PersonaCache.cs). The spec described the class but never shipped a .cs file.

The Gemini attack also claimed "test compilation fails." Grep verification: BootstrapperStoreCleanupTests is `Assert.Fail`-only and doesn't reference the AnimoBootstrapper type, so test compilation actually works without the file. The compilation-fails claim is **partially false** — but the underlying physical-gap bug is real and worth fixing for spec/repository consistency.

**Resolution**: Ship `Scripts/AnimoBootstrapper.cs` bracketed in `#if UNITY_5_3_OR_NEWER` (so headless dotnet test still compiles) with:
- `[DefaultExecutionOrder(-1000)]` attribute (ensures Awake before Agents).
- `_animo_json` SerializeField for the JSON TextAsset.
- Awake stub: NotImplementedException, with comment showing Phase 3 body (`Json.Parse` + `PersonaCache.Initialize`).
- OnDestroy stub: empty (Phase 3 calls `PersonaCache.ClearForTesting()` + `Store.ResetForTesting()`).

**Cross-impact**: §17 Repository Layout EN+JP updated to list AnimoBootstrapper.cs.

### Q-S98 — ScenarioRunner Math.Round, not Math.Floor

**Verified by computation**:
- Python IEEE-754 simulation:
  - `float32 (10.0f / 0.1f) = 99.9999985...` → `Floor = 99` (NOT 100)
  - `float32 (30.0f / 0.1f) = 299.9999955...` → `Floor = 299` (NOT 300)
  - `float32 (100.0f / 0.1f) = 999.9999850...` → `Floor = 999` (NOT 1000)
- Q-S84 fix (Phase_2_4_19): `int total_steps = (int)Math.Floor(duration / dt);`

**Concrete bug**: Q-S84 was supposed to fix Q-S35's IEEE-754 drift contract by switching from float-accumulation `current_time += dt` to integer step counting. But Q-S84's Math.Floor argument is `duration / dt` — FLOAT division. float32 has ~7 decimal digits of precision; `10.0f / 0.1f` cannot be represented exactly and rounds DOWN to `99.9999985...`. `Math.Floor` on this slightly-under value gives 99 — the loop runs 99 times instead of the 100 promised.

**Q-S35's "exactly floor(duration / dt) Live calls" contract was STILL mathematically false even after Q-S84.**

**Resolution**: Promote both operands to double precision before division, then use Math.Round (not Floor):
```csharp
int total_steps = (int)Math.Round((double)duration / (double)dt);
```

- `(double)10.0f / (double)0.1f = 100.000000596...` (still not exact, but well above the rounding boundary)
- `Math.Round(100.000000596...) = 100`. Correct.
- For the symmetric drift case (`Math.Round(99.9999985...) = 100`). Also correct.

**Why Round, not Floor + epsilon**:
- Floor + epsilon (`Math.Floor(x + 1e-9)`) requires choosing the epsilon. Different test inputs might need different epsilons. Coupling.
- Math.Round (banker's rounding by default; ToZero/ToEven for ties) handles both directions of drift symmetrically.
- ScenarioRunner consumers pass `duration` as a multiple of `dt` (the standard simulation contract). For inputs in this class, Round === Floor on the double-precision result.
- For non-multiple inputs (e.g. `duration=9.95, dt=0.1` → ratio 99.5), Round gives 100 (banker's, ties-to-even) or 99 (ties-to-odd, MidpointRounding.AwayFromZero, etc.). The behavior is well-defined and documented; Floor would give 99. ScenarioRunner consumers are not expected to pass such inputs, but if they do, the behavior is at least deterministic per .NET rounding mode default.

**Cross-impact**: §26.3.1 EN+JP updated. ScenarioRunner.cs class docstring (line 50) updated to mirror the new form. Q-S35's contract is now mathematically true.

### Q-S99 — ScenarioRunner._seq field declared

**Verified by grep**:
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:5322`: `${_seq++}` in spec sample code.
- `:217` (Q-S42 Theme): "defaulting to `${template_id}_run_{seq++}`".
- `Scripts/Tools/ScenarioRunner.cs`: `_root` and `_engine` declared, no `_seq`.

**Concrete bug**: Same pattern as Q-S92's `_engine` field omission. Q-S42 (Phase_2_4_14) declared the spec contract for the `_seq` post-increment counter, but Q-S82's file materialization (Phase_2_4_19) only added `_root`; Q-S92's correction (Phase_2_4_20) added `_engine` but missed `_seq`. Phase 3 implementer writing the override `agent_id_override ?? $"{template_id}_run_{_seq++}"` would hit a compile error.

**Resolution**: Add `int _seq = 0;` instance field with comment cross-referencing Q-S42's design rationale and #pragma CS0169 suppression for Phase 3 (the field is unused until Run() is implemented).

**Why instance, not static**:
- Different ScenarioRunner instances (different test fixtures, parallel test runs) should not share counters. Static would cause test cross-contamination.
- The post-increment makes the FIRST Run() get `_run_0`, the second `_run_1`, etc. — matching standard Unity test naming where suffixes start at 0.

**Cross-impact**: ScenarioRunner.cs updated. Q-S42 spec narrative is now buildable end-to-end.

## Phase_2_4_22 — Gemini 25th review (1 attack, 1 adopted, 0 hallucinations) — Q-S101 POST-CENTENNIAL FIRST

After Phase_2_4_21's centennial Q-S100 round, Gemini's 25th review delivered a single — but extremely precise — attack. Q-S96 (Agent.OnDestroy null-safe) had been adopted in Phase_2_4_21 with a documentation trail across spec EN narrative §11.4.1, spec JP narrative §11.4.1, decision log Q-S96 entry, and README cumulative paragraph. But the physical `Scripts/Agent.cs` file (shipped as a stub in Q-S83) was never touched. The Phase_2_4_21 N-round consistency review covered EN+JP+code-blocks integrity but never extended its grep to `Scripts/*.cs` files. Q-S101 is the post-centennial first finding, and it exposes a previously unmonitored category of bug: spec ↔ physical file lag.

### Q-S101 — Q-S96 backport to `Scripts/Agent.cs`

**Verified by grep**:
- `Scripts/Agent.cs:36` (pre-Q-S101): `public string agent_id => _composed_persona.agent_id;` — no null-coalesce.
- `:49-52` (pre-Q-S101): `void OnDestroy() { Animo.Store.Instance.Unregister(agent: this); }` — no guard.
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:2662` (post-Q-S96): `_composed_persona?.agent_id ?? "<uninitialized>"` — null-safe.
- `docs/animo_spec_v0.1.5_EN.md:2748+` (post-Q-S96): `if (_composed_persona == null) return;` — early-return.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S96 was real but only existed in three places — spec EN code block, spec JP code block, decision log entry. Phase 3 implementer reading `Scripts/Agent.cs` directly (instead of the spec) would copy the wrong getter and miss the OnDestroy guard. The Q-S38 fail-loud chain crashes scene unload exactly as Q-S96 described — the spec's promise was real but not yet delivered to disk.

**Resolution**: Two-line backport to `Scripts/Agent.cs`:
1. `agent_id` getter: `_composed_persona?.agent_id ?? "<uninitialized>"`
2. OnDestroy: prepend `if (_composed_persona == null) return;`

Plus: extended class docstring with a Q-S96+Q-S101 cross-reference paragraph explaining the fix and the Phase_2_4_21 review-coverage gap that allowed the lag.

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/Agent.cs file content. No public API change (the getter signature and OnDestroy signature are unchanged; only their bodies are now null-safe).

### Process upgrade — N-round review extends to `Scripts/*.cs`

Q-S101 exposed a category Phase_2_4_21's review didn't monitor: **spec narrative ↔ physical Scripts/*.cs file synchronization**. Adding this layer to the N-round consistency review going forward.

Phase_2_4_22's review applied the new layer in Round 2, sweeping all 14 .cs files in `Scripts/` and `Scripts/Tools/`:
- Agent.cs — fixed in Q-S101 (this round).
- AnimoBootstrapper.cs — Q-S97 (Phase_2_4_21) had already shipped this with full body + `#if UNITY_5_3_OR_NEWER` bracket; matches spec §11.6.5.
- ScenarioRunner.cs — Q-S98 Math.Round in docstring (Phase_2_4_21 R2-A fix), Q-S92 `_engine` field, Q-S99 `_seq` field — all present.
- TraceResult.cs — Q-S93 `behavior_count`/`behavior_total_time`/`ToCsv`/`ToJson` API present.
- AnimoLog.cs, Composer.cs, Const.cs, Data.cs, Engine.cs, Json.cs, PersonaCache.cs, Store.cs, Validator.cs — no spec changes since their last sync.

The new layer caught 0 additional contradictions for Phase_2_4_22 — meaning Q-S101 was the only spec-↔-file lag the protocol had accumulated. From Q-S101 forward, every spec patch touching a code block triggers this grep.
