## Phase_2_4_23 — Gemini 26th review (12 attacks, 12 adopted, 0 hallucinations)

After Phase_2_4_22's centennial+1 single-attack round (Q-S101, post-centennial first), Gemini's 26th review delivered a 12-pack — the largest adoption Round since Phase_2_4_19's nine-pack — and like Phase_2_4_20, _21, _22 before it, every single attack grep-verified true. The 12 attacks span an unusually diverse failure surface: Unity Animator semantics (Q-S102), exception-type discipline (Q-S111), schema-vs-Validator dead-rule traps (Q-S108), test-helper false-positive bugs (Q-S106), hot-path defense-in-depth (Q-S107), narrative ↔ signature parameter naming (Q-S109), missed-during-earlier-sweep field declarations (Q-S110), per-frame null guards (Q-S104), spec-promised contracts not honored (Q-S112), empty-fallback NRE chains (Q-S103), pseudocode field-type mismatches (Q-S105), and a brand-new Validator rule (A040 / Q-S113).

The most notable: **Q-S102 partially reverts Q-S44**. The protocol has now self-corrected an earlier fix that, while internally consistent, was wrong about the Unity environment it was meant to support. Animator Controllers use static edit-time state names; runtime-expanded strings containing `GetInstanceID()` will never match a Controller state. Q-S44 dutifully wove the expanded payload through every Animator call site for "consistency" — but consistency was the wrong goal. The two channels (Animator state-name; Bus expanded-trigger) have different consumers and different naming requirements; the asymmetry is a feature, not a bug. Q-S102 is the protocol acknowledging that.

The most subtle: **Q-S111** catches a diagnostic-poisoning class of bug. PersonaCache.GetComposed threw bare `InvalidOperationException` for two architecturally-distinct errors: a missing Bootstrapper (architectural startup bug — scene SHOULD die) and a per-template authoring failure (per-Agent error — only that Agent should die). Awake's union catch labeled both `"Q-S38 stage-2 fail-loud"` — so a Bootstrapper-missing scene logged a message about a completely unrelated stage-2 path, and engineers had to attach a debugger to find the real cause. Two distinct exception types is the correct discipline.

### Q-S102 — Animator state name reverts to raw

**Verified by grep + Unity domain knowledge**:
- spec line 2823 (pre-Q-S102): `string trigger = _engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(_engine.behavior); _animator?.Play(stateName: trigger);`
- Q-S44 row line 219: "Q-S44 routes the first push through `_engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(_engine.behavior)` so the host sees a consistent template-expanded payload throughout."
- Unity Animator Controller spec: state names are static, edit-time identifiers. Runtime concatenation with `GetInstanceID()` yields strings like `"animo_goblin_47291_flee"` that never appear in any Controller.

**Concrete bug**: Every spawn calls `Animator.Play("animo_goblin_47291_flee")`, Unity's Animator silently logs `"no state named ..."` (or in some Unity versions, `Debug.LogWarning`), the Animator stays in its default state, and the NPC freezes in T-pose. Q-S44's promise was "consistency between frame-1 and later frames" — but every later frame ALSO sends the expanded payload, just to Bus subscribers (e.g. the Animator host's wiring code) NOT to Animator.Play directly. The two channels (Animator state-name; Bus event-name) have always had different consumers; Q-S44 conflated them.

**Resolution**: Awake step (6) reverts to `_animator?.Play(stateName: _engine.behavior)` (raw id matching Animator Controller state names). `_engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(...)` accessor remains in the Engine API (Q-S44 + Q-S46) for Bus consumers — but it is not called from Awake step (6) anymore.

**Why "partial revert" not full revert**: Q-S44 also added `internal Engine.GetExpandedActionTrigger(string behavior)` to the Engine API for Bus consumers that want the expanded form (e.g. Bus tracing tools, frame-by-frame replay). That accessor is genuinely useful and Q-S102 keeps it. Only the Awake step (6) call site is reverted.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP §11.4.1 + Q-S44 row + Q-S102 row + §3.1 paragraphs. Engine.cs Q-S44/Q-S46 accessor unchanged.

### Q-S103 — PersonaCache.GetComposed empty fallback → fail-loud throw

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 2955 (pre-Q-S103): `return new Persona { agent_id = template_id };  // empty fallback`
- C# `Persona` class (Data.cs): `actions`, `influences`, `binding` are `null` by default (no initializer in the type).
- Engine ctor pseudocode (line ~4070): `foreach (var action in _composed_persona.actions) { ... }` — direct dereference of `actions`.

**Concrete bug**: Q-S38's "fail-loud but keep the scene alive" promise relies on `GetComposed` either returning a valid composed Persona or throwing — caller catches and disables. The empty-fallback path returns garbage that crashes downstream. The crash happens AFTER Awake's catch already returned, so even the catch can't rescue it.

**Resolution**: Throw `PersonaTemplateRejectedException` (Q-S111) with a descriptive message. Same effect as a stage-2 validation failure (Awake catches, disables this Agent).

**Why throw, not "compose a default Persona"**: A default Persona would still be a violation of authoring intent. Authoring errors (typo in `_persona_template_id`, JSON missing the referenced agent_id) deserve loud diagnostics, not silent substitution. The `Composer.Compose(empty, root)` route would also fail at A011a (Persona without kind_ids needs at least one action) — same outcome (Awake catch, disable), more code, more confusion.

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/PersonaCache.cs (the new exception type lives here too via Q-S111), Scripts/Agent.cs (Awake catch routes Q-S111's two types).

### Q-S104 — ScenarioRunner.Run events null guard

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 5425: `IReadOnlyList<TimedAffectEvent>? events = null,` (default null)
- spec line 5499 (pre-Q-S104): `while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= 0.0f) {`
- No `events ??= ...` line anywhere between the signature and the first loop.

**Concrete bug**: Calling `runner.Run(agent_id: "x", duration: 10.0f)` with no events default invocation — the most natural test API call — NREs on iteration 0. Two of the three loops in Run() access `events.Count`/`events[next]`.

**Resolution**: One-line normalization at Run entry: `events ??= System.Array.Empty<TimedAffectEvent>();`. Empty array's `.Count == 0` and the while-loops never enter.

**Why not per-loop null check**: Three loops, three checks, three opportunities to forget. Normalize once at entry — same defense, fewer edges.

**Cross-impact**: spec §26.3.1 EN/JP; Phase 3 ScenarioRunner.cs Run body.

### Q-S105 — A039 pseudocode `trigger_threshold`

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 1871 (pre-Q-S105): `if (next.trigger - prev.trigger) < 1.0f`
- Data.cs `Threshold.trigger` is `public string trigger { get; set; } = "";` (event-name string)
- Data.cs `Threshold.trigger_threshold` is `public float trigger_threshold { get; set; } = 0f;` (numeric)

**Concrete bug**: A naive Phase 3 transcription of the pseudocode hits "operator '-' cannot be applied to operands of type 'string' and 'string'" — a CS error that aborts the build on first attempt to write A039.

**Resolution**: sed-correct pseudocode to `(next.trigger_threshold - prev.trigger_threshold) < 1.0f`. Plus annotative paragraph explaining what changed and why so future readers understand the typo source.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP §13 A039 pseudocode block. JP had a duplicate code block (sed left a residual older copy); duplicate block also removed.

### Q-S106 — `AssertResult.HasError` severity-aware

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Helpers/AssertResult.cs:23`: `Assert.That(result.has_errors, Is.True, ...); Assert.That(result.HasRule(rule_id), Is.True, ...);`
- `Validator.cs` `HasRule` is severity-agnostic — returns true for ANY issue with that rule_id.

**Concrete bug**: A test JSON yielding `[{rule:"A005", severity:Error}, {rule:"A028", severity:Warning}]` would have `has_errors == true` AND `HasRule("A028") == true` — `HasError(result, "A028")` passes despite A028 being a Warning. Tests asserting "A028 fires as Error" pass when A028 actually fires as Warning. Red-baseline integrity destroyed for any test that uses these helpers (which is most of the Validator tests).

**Resolution**: Add `ValidationResult.HasRuleWithSeverity(string rule_id, Severity severity)` — true only if there's an Issue with BOTH the rule_id AND the severity. AssertResult.HasError uses `HasRuleWithSeverity(rule_id, Severity.Error)`; AssertResult.HasWarning uses `HasRuleWithSeverity(rule_id, Severity.Warning)`. Each helper now precisely asserts what its name claims.

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/Validator.cs (new method declaration), Tests~/EditModeTests/Helpers/AssertResult.cs (rewritten to use new method).

### Q-S107 — Step3_Thresholds binding null-coalesce

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 4143-4144 (pre-Q-S107): `void Step3_Thresholds() { foreach (var t in _persona.binding.thresholds) { ...`
- Engine ctor line 4086: `var thresholds = _composed_persona.binding?.thresholds ?? Array.Empty<Threshold>() as IReadOnlyList<Threshold>;` — defensive form already in use.

**Concrete bug**: A hand-built Persona constructed by C# code (bypassing Composer.Compose, which would always fill a Binding default per Q-S7) and passed directly to Engine reaches Engine ctor where the defensive ?-form prevents NRE. But the hot path Step 3 then NREs every frame because it doesn't share the defense.

**Resolution**: Mirror the ctor's null-coalesce form in Step 3. Cost: one extra ?-check per frame per Engine — measurable but well below the noise of the Influence cascade and Action scoring; the alternative (NRE on a wrong-shape Persona) is worse.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP §16.5 Step3_Thresholds pseudocode.

### Q-S108 — Schema `reset_threshold.minimum` removed

**Verified by grep**:
- `Schemas/animo.schema.json` line 196 (pre-Q-S108): `"minimum": 0.0,`
- spec line 191 A034 row: "(v0.1.5, Q-S11) — prevents permanent `Above` traps with low triggers" — A034 is the dedicated Validator rule for explicit-negative `reset_threshold`.

**Concrete bug**: ajv schema validation runs BEFORE the C# Validator. If schema rejects `reset_threshold: -1.0`, A034 never sees the value. A034 is dead code.

**Resolution**: Remove the schema `"minimum": 0.0` so the value flows through ajv to A034, where the human-readable message ("a typed negative is rejected to surface authoring typos") fires. The upper bound `100.0` is preserved (no Validator rule covers "reset above clamp ceiling").

**Cross-impact**: Schemas/animo.schema.json (one-line change with explanatory comment per Q-S108).

### Q-S109 — Q-S42 narrative `agent_id`

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 5422: `string agent_id, // template id from JSON` — parameter name is `agent_id`.
- spec line 5427 (post-Q-S109): `// If null, runner generates `${agent_id}_run_${_seq++}`` — comment matches signature ✅.
- spec lines 217 (Q-S42 row), 553 (paragraph), 2880 (mid-spec) pre-Q-S109: `${template_id}_run_${seq++}` — narrative invents a `template_id` variable that is not in scope.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementer reading the narrative literally writes `agent_id_override ?? $"{template_id}_run_{_seq++}"` — gets "the name `template_id` does not exist" CS error.

**Resolution**: sed-replace all narrative `${template_id}_run_${seq++}` (and variants `_run_{seq++}`, `_run_{_seq++}`) with `${agent_id}_run_${_seq++}` matching the actual signature.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP narrative; Q-S99 row + Q-S109 row keep historical citations intact (they describe the very bug Q-S109 fixes).

### Q-S110 — `_previous_behavior` field declared

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 4181 (§16.6 fields table): "`_previous_behavior` `string` — defaults to `""`. Held alongside the public `behavior` property. Step 5 reads...; Q-S31 silent-first-transition contract checks `previous_behavior == ""` (the only time that condition is true is the very first Step 5)."
- `Scripts/Engine.cs` (pre-Q-S110): only `_persona` (line 25) and `_lock_remaining` (line 37) declared.

**Concrete bug**: Same physical-gap pattern as Q-S70's `_lock_remaining` fix. Phase 3 Step 5 implementation hits a CS error.

**Resolution**: Add `string _previous_behavior = "";` instance field with #pragma CS0414 (the field is initialized but not yet read in v0.1.5 stub; Phase 3 Step 5 will read+write).

**Why empty-string sentinel doubles as Q-S31 first-transition flag**: the only frame where `_previous_behavior == ""` is the very first Step 5 of the Engine's life, which is exactly when the silent-first-transition contract should suppress OnSignal. After Step 5 writes a real action_id once, the sentinel can never reappear (snake_case action ids are non-empty by A009).

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/Engine.cs (one field added).

### Q-S111 — Awake exception type split

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 3003 (pre-Q-S111): `throw new InvalidOperationException("PersonaCache.Initialize(root) must be called once at startup.");`
- spec line 2954 (Q-S38 path within GetComposed): also thrown as `InvalidOperationException` per spec narrative.
- spec line 2768 (Awake catch): `catch (System.InvalidOperationException ex) { ... "Q-S38 stage-2 fail-loud" ... }` — catches the union.

**Concrete bug**: Bootstrapper-missing produces a log message claiming "Q-S38 stage-2 fail-loud" — completely misleading. Engineers reading the log assume a JSON authoring error and look in the wrong place. Diagnosis from logs alone is impossible.

**Resolution**: Two distinctive exception types — `PersonaCacheNotInitializedException` (architectural startup error) and `PersonaTemplateRejectedException` (per-template authoring error). Awake catches only the latter; the former propagates out of Awake and Unity logs it as a hard scene-load error.

**Why both inherit InvalidOperationException**: Backward compatibility — any external code that catches `InvalidOperationException` still catches both subtypes. `is`-pattern match in Awake distinguishes them when needed.

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/PersonaCache.cs (new exception type declarations, GetComposed throw type updated, type narrative in spec EN/JP). Q-S103 also uses `PersonaTemplateRejectedException` (the unknown-template-id case shares the per-template authoring-error category).

### Q-S112 — `Bus == null` log-once Warning

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 3102: "If `Bus` is `null`: log a Warning once, then go silent. Animo can be used without Germio (a valid use case)."
- spec line 2803 (pre-Q-S112): `_engine.OnSignal += signal_id => _bus?.Publish(signal_id: signal_id);` — `?.Publish` silently skips when null; no Warning is emitted.

**Concrete bug**: A Bus reference null-stripped by build pipeline misconfiguration looks identical to an intentionally-Bus-less Animo (no error, no warning, just no signals). Authoring assistance the contract promised never fires.

**Resolution**: Awake start: `if (_bus == null) AnimoLog.Warning("Agent '{name}' has no Germio.Bus assigned ...")` once before the rest of Awake runs. The `?.Publish(...)` line stays the same (no behavior change for the actual publish path); the contract-mandated Warning is now also emitted.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP Awake; Phase 3 Agent.cs Awake body.

### Q-S113 — A040 (composed `actions[].id` uniqueness)

**Verified by grep**:
- All A* rules `^| \\*\\*A0` lines: A009 protects `actions[].id` non-empty. No rule covers uniqueness within Persona.
- A002 (`persona.agent_id` unique), A003 (`kind.kind_id` unique) — these protect identity at outer levels but not within a Persona's actions list.
- Engine ctor pre-expansion (spec line 4071): `_cached_action_triggers[action.id] = expanded;` — Dictionary indexer silently overwrites duplicate keys.

**Concrete bug**: LLM author writes `[{id:"Flee", need:"fear"}, {id:"Flee", need:"hunger"}]` and the JSON passes Stage 1. Engine ctor builds `_cached_action_triggers` overwriting the first Flee with the second. `GetActionScore("Flee")` returns the second action's score; OnBehaviorChanged routes Bus events through the second action's expanded trigger; the first action becomes ghost-data — present in `composed.actions[]` but never referenced anywhere.

**Resolution**: New Stage-2 Error rule A040: "Composed `actions[].id` must be unique within a Persona." Stage 2 because Composer cascade can introduce duplicates that Persona-only inspection misses (Kind defines `Flee`, Persona overrides another action also named `Flee`). Validator rule count: 40 → 41 (A000-A040).

**Why Error not Warning**: Silent overwrites destroy debugger output, replay determinism, and Bus routing semantics. Authors should fix the duplicate before reaching runtime — Error forces the fix.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN/JP §13 ruleset table (one new row, count update); §17 layout (Validator.cs annotation `A000-A039` → `A000-A040`); Phase 3 Validator.ValidateStage2 implementation.

- **Round 2** (NEW LAYER from Q-S101 — code blocks vs all `Scripts/*.cs`): 0 contradictions. Engine.cs has `_previous_behavior` (Q-S110), Validator.cs has HasRuleWithSeverity (Q-S106), PersonaCache.cs has both new exception types (Q-S111). Three Q-S that touch Awake bodies (Q-S102/Q-S111/Q-S112) live entirely in spec narrative because Agent.cs Awake is `throw NotImplementedException()` until Phase 3 — class docstring records the cross-references for Phase 3 to follow.
- **Round 3** (README + decision log cross-check): 2 contradictions caught and fixed.
  - R3-A: README missing Q-S102-S113 cumulative paragraph (added).
  - R3-B: Decision log missing Q-S102-S113 entries (this section, added).
- **Round 4**: FIXPOINT REACHED. Zero new contradictions across the full deepened review.

Total contradictions caught across 4 rounds: 2.
Total fixes applied within Phase_2_4_23: 2.

## Phase_2_4_24 — Gemini 27th review (6 attacks, 6 adopted, 0 hallucinations)

After Phase_2_4_23's diverse-failure 12-pack, Gemini's 27th review delivered a 6-pack notable for two protocol-self-corrections (Q-S114, Q-S119) — the protocol's own prior sweeps left residue that this round caught. The 9th category of self-bug — **process-discipline gaps** — emerged. Gemini's adversarial pressure has begun catching the protocol's own bugs as fast as it catches the spec's bugs.

### Q-S114 — Q-S109 sed C# string-interp pollution

**Verified by grep**:
- spec EN line 5635 (pre-Q-S114): `// If null, runner generates `${agent_id}_run_${_seq++}` so` — Bash/JS template-literal syntax inside a C# code block.
- spec JP line 4503: same pattern.
- Q-S109 narrative (line 284 EN): "sed-unifies on `${agent_id}_run_${_seq++}` everywhere in the narrative" — narrative form, but the sed wasn't constrained to non-code regions.
- Other code blocks (line 217 EN, 565 EN, 3024 EN): use the correct C# form `$"{agent_id}_run_{_seq++}"` — those were untouched by Q-S109's sed because they were in different regex contexts.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementer copying line 5635's comment (or worse, transcribing the pseudocode below it that uses the same form) into ScenarioRunner.cs hits "expected expression" / "$ is not a valid token" CS errors.

**Resolution**: Restore C# string-interp form `$"{agent_id}_run_{_seq++}"` in the code-block comments at line 5635 EN / line 4503 JP. Narrative historical citations (which describe the bug in its original Bash-style form) preserved — those text spans describe what was wrong, not what's right, so the Bash form is contextually correct there.

**Why this is also a self-correction**: Q-S109 was the fix; Q-S114 is the fix-of-the-fix. The protocol's own prior sweep introduced the bug.

**Cross-impact**: Spec EN/JP code-block comments only. No physical file change (the sed was pure-narrative).

### Q-S115 — `ITimeProvider` DI receiving point

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Scripts/Agent.cs:78` (pre-Q-S115): `_engine.Live(dt: Time.deltaTime);` — direct UnityEngine.Time reference.
- `Tests~/MiniUnity/MockTime.cs`: static class with `MockTime.deltaTime` and `MockTime.Step(dt)` — independent test-time abstraction.
- `Tests~/MiniUnity/MockScene.cs`: `Tick(dt)` advances `MockTime.Step(dt)` correctly.
- No `ITimeProvider` interface anywhere in `Scripts/` or `Tests~/`.

**Concrete bug**: A future EditMode test that wires Agents through MockScene and expects MockTime.Step(dt) to advance the simulator finds Agent.Update reading `Time.deltaTime` (= 0 / undefined outside Play mode) and calling `_engine.Live(0.0f)` forever. Simulated time stands still; Step 1 decay never accumulates; Threshold fires never trigger. The existing test infrastructure works because no test currently runs Agents through MockScene; the bug is latent.

**Resolution**: Document an `ITimeProvider` interface as the Phase 3 DI receiving point. v0.1.5 spec §11.4.1 + Agent.cs class docstring + Update method comment all record the contract: Phase 3 introduces `interface ITimeProvider { float deltaTime { get; } }` (or similar), Agent receives it via constructor injection or `[SerializeField]`, default implementation reads UnityEngine.Time, test implementations read MockTime. The v0.1.5 stub keeps the direct `Time.deltaTime` reference because the stub never runs (Awake throws NotImplementedException; Update is never reached). Phase 3 implements the DI seam from day one.

**Why no physical change in v0.1.5**: The stub doesn't run. Adding the interface now without a Phase 3 consumer would be over-engineering and would break the rule that v0.1.5 stubs match the §11.4.1 narrative. The right move is to record the contract; Phase 3 implements both ends together.

**Cross-impact**: Spec EN/JP §11.4.1 narrative; Scripts/Agent.cs class docstring + Update method comment. No interface declaration in v0.1.5.

### Q-S116 — `System.Math.Clamp` in Animo.Core hot path

**Verified by grep**:
- spec EN line 2409 (pre-Q-S116): `eff.Set(inf.target, Mathf.Clamp(eff.Get(inf.target) + delta, 0f, 100f));` — UnityEngine.Mathf in Animo.Core hot path.
- spec EN line 2567 mermaid (pre-Q-S116): `P1 & P2 & P3 & P4 --> C["Mathf.Clamp(value, 0, 100)"]`.
- §5 + asmdef `noEngineReferences: true` (Animo.Core asmdef) forbid UnityEngine references.
- §15.4 line 3977: `Mathf.Clamp(value, 0f, 1f);` — this is the named-parameter example block discussing positional vs named arguments at the API surface, NOT Animo.Core code.
- §17 perf table lines 4423-4424: `Mathf.Clamp` ~1ns / `Mathf.Pow` ~10ns — historical benchmark numbers. Acceptable as historical citation; the corresponding `System.Math` BCL methods have similar JIT-optimized implementations.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementer transcribing §9.6.5 cascade pseudocode into Engine.cs hits "the name `Mathf` does not exist in the current context" — Animo.Core asmdef does not reference UnityEngine.

**Resolution**: §9.6.5 → `System.Math.Clamp(eff.Get(inf.target) + delta, 0f, 100f)`. §9.3 mermaid → `System.Math.Clamp(value, 0, 100)`. Both EN+JP. §15.4 unchanged (it discusses named parameters at the API surface, not Animo.Core implementation). §17 perf table unchanged (historical benchmarks).

**Cross-impact**: Spec EN/JP §9.6.5 + §9.3 mermaid only.

### Q-S117 — `ScenarioRunner.Run` dt<=0 fail-loud

**Verified by source-read + CLI ECMA-335 §III.1.5**:
- spec EN line 5713 (pre-Q-S117): `int next = 0;` immediately follows the comment block describing Q-S98's step computation. No dt guard.
- Python verification of the CLI behavior:
  - `float Infinity bits: 0x7f800000`
  - `(int)float.Infinity = int.MinValue = -2147483648`
  - `for (int i = 0; i < int.MinValue; i++)` predicate `0 < -2147483648 = false` → loop body never runs.

**Concrete bug**: A test `runner.Run(agent_id: "x", duration: 10.0f, dt: 0.0f)` returns an empty `TraceResult` with no exception, no log, no assertion failure. The test "passes" because nothing visibly broke. But the simulator did nothing. Negative `dt` follows the same path through `Math.Round` and the same `(int)NegativeInfinity = int.MinValue` outcome. Worst silent-failure: the test author can't distinguish "simulator correctly produced nothing because nothing should have happened" from "simulator silently bailed because dt was wrong."

**Resolution**: Run entry, before any time math runs:
```csharp
if (dt <= 0.0f) {
    throw new System.ArgumentException(
        $"ScenarioRunner.Run: dt must be strictly positive (was {dt}). " +
        $"Negative or zero dt would silently produce an empty TraceResult " +
        $"due to (int)Infinity = int.MinValue. Use a positive timestep, " +
        $"e.g. the default dt = 0.1f.",
        nameof(dt));
}
```

The exception message names both the symptom (silent empty TraceResult) and the cause (Infinity → int.MinValue) so future debugger sessions don't have to rediscover the CLI ECMA-335 detail.

**Cross-impact**: Spec EN/JP §26.3.1 Run entry. Scripts/Tools/ScenarioRunner.cs class docstring records Q-S117 (Q-S101 NEW LAYER discipline — physical file mention in docstring even though the dt guard body is in spec narrative for Phase 3).

### Q-S118 — `AnimoBootstrapper.OnDestroy` editor-only guard

**Verified by source-read + Unity domain knowledge**:
- spec EN line 3268-3269 (pre-Q-S118): cleanup runs unconditionally in OnDestroy.
- Q-S58 row line 233: "Q-S58 pairs `Store.ResetForTesting()` with the existing `PersonaCache.ClearForTesting()` call. Both are idempotent + cheap." — original justification was Editor Fast Play Mode static-state cleanup.
- Unity Application API: `Application.isEditor` = true under Editor (Play and Edit), `Application.isPlaying` = true during Play (both in Editor and in built player). Combined: `(isEditor, isPlaying) = (true, false)` is *Editor after Stop* (the Q-S58 target case); other combinations are Editor-Play, built-Play, etc.

**Concrete bug**: A shipped game's scene transition triggers OnDestroy on the outgoing scene's bootstrapper GameObject. Pre-Q-S118 the cleanup ran, wiping `Store.Instance._agents`. Companion NPC marked `DontDestroyOnLoad` survived the scene change but its Store registration didn't — the alive-but-unrouted Agent receives no Bus events. The game appears to work for the transition but breaks after.

**Resolution**: Guard the cleanup with `if (!Application.isEditor || Application.isPlaying) return;`. Truth table:
| isEditor | isPlaying | Context | Cleanup runs? |
|---|---|---|---|
| true | false | Editor after Stop (Q-S58 target) | YES |
| true | true | Editor during Play (incl. Play scene transitions) | NO |
| false | false | n/a (Application.isEditor=false implies built player not yet started) | NO |
| false | true | Built player runtime (incl. production scene transitions) | NO |

The guard preserves Q-S58's intent (Fast Play Mode static-state cleanup) while skipping all production paths.

**Cross-impact**: Spec EN §11.6.5 Bootstrapper code; Scripts/AnimoBootstrapper.cs OnDestroy comment + class docstring (records Q-S118 cross-reference). JP narrative paragraphs added.

- `Scripts/Validator.cs:96-104` (pre-Q-S119): ValidateStage2 XML docstring lists A019/A025/A035/A036/A037/A038/A039 — no A040.
- spec EN line 3220 §11.6.2 (pre-Q-S119): same enumeration A019/A025/A035/A036/A037/A038/A039.

- Q-S113 row line 288: introduces A040 to §13 ruleset table.

**Concrete bug**: A Phase 3 implementer reading Validator.cs ValidateStage2's docstring to know what rules to implement doesn't see A040 listed and may forget to implement it. The spec narrative §11.6.2 listing has the same gap. The §17 Layout is correct (Q-S113 updated it) but the per-method docstring and the per-section narrative are stale.

**Resolution**:

**Why the Q-S101 NEW LAYER missed this**: The NEW LAYER (Phase_2_4_22) sweeps spec narrative ↔ physical Scripts/*.cs file equivalence at the FILE level — does the file have the field/method/exception type that the spec references? It does NOT recurse into docstrings within those files to check rule enumerations are current. Q-S119 closes that gap. Process upgrade: every new Validator rule (Q-S113-style) now triggers an additional grep over `ValidateStage2` docstrings, both in spec narrative and in `Scripts/Validator.cs`.

**Why this is also a self-correction**: Q-S113 was the new rule; Q-S119 is the documentation-update Q-S113 forgot. The protocol's own prior fix left a documentation residue.

**Cross-impact**: Scripts/Validator.cs ValidateStage2 docstring; spec EN/JP §11.6.2 + spec EN line 3653 narrative version of the docstring.

## Phase_2_4_25 — Gemini 28th review (12 attacks, 11 adopted, 1 hallucination)

After Phase_2_4_24's 6-pack with two protocol self-corrections, Gemini's 28th review delivered a 12-pack notable for **the protocol's fourth hallucination** (the first new hallucination since Phase_2_4_19, six rounds clean) AND **three direct protocol-self-corrections** out of the 11 adopted attacks. The 9th category (process-discipline gaps) deepened.

### Q-S120 — Step3 test contract drift Q-S54

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/Step3_ThresholdEffectiveNeedsTests.cs:76` (pre-Q-S120): `Assert.That(engine.GetNeed(need: "anger"), Is.EqualTo(expected: 0f).Within(0.001f), ...)` after `engine.Affect(need: "frustration", delta: +80f); engine.Live(dt: 0.016f);` with Influence `frustration → anger, coefficient: +1.0f`.
- spec line 1255 (Q-S54 row): `Engine.GetNeed(string need)` — returns the **effective** value of the named Need (post-Influence-cascade)`.
- spec line 1257 (Q-S54 row): `Engine.GetBaseNeed(string need)` — returns the **base** (pre-cascade) value`.

**Concrete bug**: With Q-S54's effective semantics, the cascade lifts `_effective_needs[anger] ≈ 80` from the frustration pump (intensity = frustration/100 × coefficient × source_value = 1.0 × 1.0 × 80 ≈ 80). `engine.GetNeed("anger")` returns ~80, not 0. The assertion expects 0 with tolerance 0.001 — fails by ~80,000x. Pre-Q-S120 the test docstring at line 28-30 said "GetNeed reads back the BASE Need (= 0)" — the docstring was written before Q-S54 re-defined GetNeed's semantics.

**Resolution**: Switch the assertion to `engine.GetBaseNeed(need: "anger")`. The base array is untouched by Influence per §9.6.5 (Influence writes to `_effective_needs` only); GetBaseNeed reads the base array. The test's documented intent ("confirm BASE _needs[anger] is untouched") is preserved exactly. Q-S120 cross-reference comment added.

**Why Q-S54 missed this**: Q-S54 (Phase_2_4_18) added the `GetBaseNeed` API to spec narrative + the new method declaration in `Scripts/Engine.cs`. The companion test was written PRE-Q-S54 (Phase_2_4_x earlier) using `GetNeed` with the old "current value" semantics. Q-S54's sweep updated the spec but didn't grep test files for `GetNeed("` calls that should have been Q-S54-aware. Same pattern as Q-S119 (Q-S113 added rule, missed docstring listing) and Q-S114 (Q-S109 sed missed C# code blocks): **process-discipline gap — sweep didn't propagate to consumers**.

**Cross-impact**: `Step3_ThresholdEffectiveNeedsTests.cs` Case01 only. Case01 line 57-60 (preconditions, before Affect) still uses `GetNeed` because base==effective==0 at construction; both methods would return 0 there. Functionally equivalent at construction; the post-Affect assertion is the only place where the distinction matters.

### Q-S121 — Schema range constraints generalized to Validator-only

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Schemas/animo.schema.json` (pre-Q-S121): seven range constraints across 9 lines:
  - `need_value`: `minimum: 0.0, maximum: 100.0` (A005)
  - `coefficient`: `minimum: -1.0, maximum: 1.0` (A012)
  - `suppression_factor`: `minimum: 0.0, maximum: 1.0` (A006)
  - `tier`: `minimum: 1, maximum: 5` (A007)
  - `exponent`: `minimum: 0.1, maximum: 5.0` (A008)
  - `commitment.bonus`: `minimum: 0.0, maximum: 50.0` (A028)
  - `trigger_threshold`: `minimum: 0.0, maximum: 100.0` (A010)
  - Plus the local `need_meta.tier`: `minimum: 1, maximum: 5` (A038, redundant with `tier`)
- Q-S108 row (Phase_2_4_22) removed `reset_threshold`'s minimum for the same reason.

**Concrete bug**: Every one of these range checks was permanently-unreachable dead code in the C# Validator. ajv hard-rejected JSON before Phase 3 ran; the Validator's human-readable Error messages for these rules never surfaced to authors. Q-S108 caught one instance; Q-S121 generalizes the principle.

**Resolution**: Schema role split:
- **Schema**: structural concerns — types (`number`, `integer`, `string`), `required`, `additionalProperties: false`, `enum`, snake_case `pattern`, `minLength`, `minItems`. These cannot be expressed elsewhere or are needed at the JSON-shape gate to prevent type confusion.
- **Validator**: semantic concerns — numeric range bounds, cross-field constraints (trigger > reset), composed-shape rules (cycles, orphans, uniqueness). Human-readable Error messages.

All 7 range constraints removed from schema; descriptions updated to point at the corresponding Validator rule. The redundant `need_meta.tier` minimum/maximum (A038-side check that duplicates `tier`'s A007) also removed.

**Backward compatibility**: 
- JSON that was previously valid is **still valid** (schema accepted it before, accepts it now).
- JSON that was previously rejected at the schema gate (e.g. `"tier": 7`) is **now reaching the Validator** and getting rejected there with a human-readable message instead of cryptic ajv text. From the JSON author's standpoint, errors are now more useful.
- Existing test fixtures unaffected (none relied on schema-side rejection of out-of-range values).

**Cross-impact**: `Schemas/animo.schema.json` only. No spec narrative change beyond the per-property descriptions (already auto-included).

### Q-S122 — A039 inclusive boundary `<= 1.0f`

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 2036 (pre-Q-S122): `For each adjacent pair, if (next.trigger_threshold - prev.trigger_threshold) < 1.0f:` — strict less-than.
- spec line 3611 §13 A039 row (pre-Q-S122): `with trigger_threshold values within 1.0f of each other` — English "within X" idiomatically includes the boundary.
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A039_SiblingThresholdProximityTests.cs:21`: `Case01_SiblingTriggersAt78And79_EmitsA039Warning` — diff exactly 1.0.
- `1.0f - 1.0f < 1.0f` evaluates to `0.0f < 1.0f = true` BUT `(79.0f - 78.0f) < 1.0f` is `1.0f < 1.0f = false` (IEEE-754 exact: `1.0f` is representable exactly, no drift). The test cannot pass under strict `<`.

**Concrete bug**: Phase 3 implementing the pseudocode literally would leave the test Red even when the implementation matches the spec. Either the pseudocode is wrong or the test is wrong; the natural reading of "within 1.0f" makes the inclusive form correct.

**Resolution**: Pseudocode → `<=`. Mermaid label, §13 row, and EN+JP all updated with Q-S122 inclusive note. Q-S105 (Phase_2_4_22) had already corrected the pseudocode's `trigger` → `trigger_threshold` typing; Q-S122 closes the boundary semantics.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §13 A039 row, pseudocode block, mermaid diagram. No code change (Phase 3 will implement against the corrected form).

### Q-S123 — ScenarioRunner.Run dead `current_time` line

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 5902 (pre-Q-S123): `float current_time = total_steps * dt;   // for the post-loop sweep boundary check below`.
- spec line 5913 (post-loop while): `while (next < events.Count && events[next].time <= duration) {` — uses `duration` (the function argument), NOT `current_time`.
- No other reference to `current_time` in the rest of the Run pseudocode block.

**Concrete bug**: CS0219 ("variable assigned but never used"). The comment claims it's "for the post-loop sweep boundary check below" but the post-loop sweep doesn't reference it — it uses `duration` directly. Dead code. Animo's spec promises zero compiler warnings; Phase 3 transcribing this would force either a warning emission or a `_ = current_time;` no-op suppression, neither acceptable.

**Resolution**: Remove the line in EN+JP. The post-loop sweep semantics are unchanged because they don't depend on `current_time`. Replacement comment explains the rationale + Q-S123 reference.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §26.3.1 Run pseudocode block only.

### Q-S124 — A019 typo coverage extended to A038's union

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 3589 (pre-Q-S124, A019 row): A019's coverage was implicit/unspecified beyond "needs key" — the spec didn't explicitly enumerate the collection sites.
- spec line 3610 (A038 row): `needs_meta` orphan check sees `needs[]` ∪ `actions[].need` ∪ `influences[].source/target` ∪ `binding.thresholds[].need` ∪ `rates.keys()` — five sites, with Q-S41 (actions/influences), Q-S49 (thresholds), Q-S57 (rates) being incremental additions.
- A019's collection has stayed at "needs key" (~3 sites in the original pre-Q-S39 form: `needs`, `actions`, `influences`).

**Concrete bug**: An LLM author writes a typo `oxigen` (intended `oxygen`) only inside `binding.thresholds[].need` — A019 wouldn't catch it because A019 doesn't scan thresholds. A038 would have caught the typo on a real (non-typo) Need name appearing only in thresholds (orphan check), but A038 doesn't surface the typo specifically — it just says "the need is in needs_meta but not in use." The author could be misled into thinking the issue is the metadata.

**Resolution**: A019's Need-name collection extended to the same union as A038. Spec EN+JP §13 A019 row updated with explicit enumeration + Q-S124 cross-reference. Phase 3 implementation now scans 5 sites for unknown-Need-name typos.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §13 A019 row only. Phase 3 implementation will follow the updated coverage when A019 is wired up.

### Q-S125 — Engine ctor `actions` null-coalesce

**Verified by source-read**:
- spec line 4295 (pre-Q-S125, ctor cache build): `foreach (var action in _composed_persona.actions) {` — raw access.
- spec line 4298: `foreach (var threshold in _composed_persona.binding?.thresholds ?? Array.Empty<Threshold>()) {` — defended.
- spec line 4380 (pre-Q-S125, ctor template-expansion): `foreach (var action in _composed_persona.actions) {` — also raw access.
- Q-S12/Q-S53/Q-S107 (three rounds of hardening) added the threshold defense; the actions loop was overlooked because Composer guarantees actions is non-null when Composer runs.

**Concrete bug**: Composer-bypassing test fixtures or hand-built Personas (e.g. ScenarioRunner-driven Engines that never call Composer; partial fixtures used in unit tests) can have `actions = null`. The ctor NREs immediately on construction — even before any test logic runs. Q-S103 closed the GetComposed empty-fallback path (one specific NRE source) but didn't generalize; Q-S125 generalizes.

**Resolution**: Both foreach loops use `_composed_persona.actions ?? new List<Action>()`. Defense-in-depth consistency with thresholds. Q-S125 cross-reference comment added.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §16 Engine ctor pseudocode (two locations: line 4295 cache build + line 4380 template expansion). No physical Scripts/*.cs change (Phase 3 implementation point).

### Q-S126 — Lock(0) narrative clarification

**Verified by grep**:
- spec line 2172 (pre-Q-S126): `This also makes Lock(0) (Q9) behavior consistent: Lock(duration: 0) is observably equivalent to Unlock() because the very next entry to Live(dt) (or the same call's T0 if still inside it) immediately decrements past zero and releases.`
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Engine/LockEdgeCaseTests.cs:42-43`: `e.Lock(duration: 0f); Assert.That(e.is_locked, Is.False, ...);` — between Lock(0) and the assertion, no Live() call.
- spec §3.4 (Engine API): `bool is_locked` is a read-only property; the v0.1.5 design has `is_locked => _lock_remaining > 0` (computed from a single field).

**Concrete bug**: The pre-Q-S126 narrative reads as if `is_locked` could remain `true` between `Lock(0)` and the next `Live(dt)`. A Phase 3 implementer might add a special-case `if (duration == 0) Unlock();` in `Lock` to satisfy the test's immediate-False requirement. But the property semantics make that special path unnecessary: `Lock(0)` sets `_lock_remaining = 0`, the next `is_locked` getter returns `0 > 0 = false`. The narrative was misleading without being wrong.

**Resolution**: Rewrite §9.2 paragraph (line 2172) to make the property semantics explicit:
- "Lock(0) sets `_lock_remaining = 0`."
- "Because `is_locked` is the property `_lock_remaining > 0` (not a separate field), the getter immediately returns `false` after the assignment — no special path inside `Lock` is required."
- "The Lock timer phase (T0) on the next `Live(dt)` is a no-op (already at 0); the no-op decrement does not flip `is_locked` because nothing crossed zero."
- Q-S126 cross-reference: clarification, implementation contract unchanged.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §9.2 narrative paragraph only. No code change; the existing test passes unchanged once Phase 3 implements `is_locked` as the computed property.

### Q-S127 — `AnimoLog.Error` System.Console qualifier

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Scripts/AnimoLog.cs:30` (pre-Q-S127, comment): `// in editor/runtime, falls back to Console.Error.WriteLine`
- `Scripts/AnimoLog.cs` headers: only `#nullable enable` — no `using System;` directive.
- C#: `Console` is `System.Console`. Without the using, the bare `Console` symbol does not resolve.

**Concrete bug**: A Phase 3 implementer copy-pasting the comment as code would hit CS0103 ("the name `Console` does not exist in the current context"). The comment's intent is correct (fall back to stdout in headless environments) but the code form is unbuildable.

**Resolution**: Comment updated to `System.Console.Error.WriteLine` (fully qualified). Class docstring expanded to record the Q-S127 reasoning so future copy-paste survives. Either form compiles when Phase 3 writes the body; the qualified form is self-contained.

**Cross-impact**: `Scripts/AnimoLog.cs` only. No spec narrative change.

### Q-S128 — Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER read-only hardening

**Verified by source-read**:
- `Scripts/Const.cs:48` (pre-Q-S128): `public static readonly System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary<int, int[]> NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER = new() { ... };`
- C# `readonly` semantics: prevents reassignment of the field itself (`Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER = new Dictionary<...>` is forbidden). Does NOT prevent indexed mutation of the dictionary's values (`Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[1][0] = 99` is allowed).
- The mutable int[] is shared by every Engine in the process; corruption is global.

**Concrete bug**: Any code in the same assembly (or any consumer with reflection access) can corrupt the tier mapping. Tier 1 is `{ NEED_INDEX_HUNGER, NEED_INDEX_FATIGUE }`; an accidental `Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[1][0] = NEED_INDEX_IDLE;` would silently break Maslow tier suppression for every Agent (suddenly tier 1 includes idle, which collapses physiological-tier dominance). Hard to debug because the mutation is in a shared singleton.

**Resolution**: 
- Type widened from `Dictionary<int, int[]>` to `IReadOnlyDictionary<int, IReadOnlyList<int>>`.
- Initialization: `new Dictionary<int, IReadOnlyList<int>> { ... }` with each entry wrapping `Array.AsReadOnly(new int[] { ... })`.
- Public surface: `IReadOnlyDictionary` has no `Add`, no index-setter; `IReadOnlyList<int>` has no `[i] = ...`.

**Consumer cross-impact**:
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/NeedTierMapTests.cs:52` (pre-Q-S128): `int[] tier2 = Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[2];` — would fail with "cannot implicitly convert IReadOnlyList<int> to int[]".
- Updated to: `IReadOnlyList<int> tier2 = Const.NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER[2];` and `.Length` → `.Count`. Semantics unchanged.

**Phase 3 hot-path note**: Engine implementations that need indexed-write access for hot-path snapshots (e.g. baking the tier indices into a flat int[][] for cache locality) create their own copy locally. The Const-side read-only nature only constrains the **shared** view, not Engine-local mutable copies.

**Cross-impact**: `Scripts/Const.cs`, `Tests~/EditModeTests/EdgeCases/NeedTierMapTests.cs`. Phase 3 implementations TBD (Engine.cs ctor will read this).

### Q-S129 — A011a test method name sed completion

- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A011_PersonaActionsRequiredTests.cs:29` (pre-Q-S129): `[Test] public void Case01_NoKindIdsNoActions_FailsA011() {`
- Same file line 34: `AssertResult.HasError(r, rule_id: "A011a");` — Q-S100 sed updated this assertion.
- Method name still reads `FailsA011` (pre-split rule ID); assertion checks `"A011a"` (post-split).

**Concrete bug**: Cosmetic-only — the test runs and asserts correctly. But anyone scanning method names for "which rule does this test target" sees `FailsA011` and thinks "the old A011 rule"; only by reading the assertion does the truth surface. Misleads code review. Q-S100's sed (Phase_2_4_22 centennial) updated only the assertion string.

**Resolution**: Method name sed: `Case01_NoKindIdsNoActions_FailsA011` → `Case01_NoKindIdsNoActions_FailsA011a`. Class docstring expanded to record the Q-S129 cross-reference.

**Why Q-S100 missed this**: Q-S100's sed targeted the assertion string `"A011"` → `"A011a"` specifically; method names containing `A011` weren't part of the search pattern. Same protocol-self-correction pattern as Q-S114 (Q-S109 sed missed C# code blocks), Q-S119 (Q-S113 missed docstring listings), Q-S120 (Q-S54 missed test consumers). The 9th category — process-discipline gaps — keeps surfacing.

**Cross-impact**: `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A011_PersonaActionsRequiredTests.cs` only.

### Q-S130 — EditMode test isolation discipline

**Verified by source-read + Unity NUnit knowledge**:
- `Scripts/AnimoBootstrapper.cs` OnDestroy guard (Q-S118): `if (!Application.isEditor || Application.isPlaying) return;`
- Unity NUnit EditMode test runner: tests run with `Application.isEditor == true` and `Application.isPlaying == false`. The guard does NOT short-circuit; cleanup runs.
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Bootstrapper/BootstrapperStoreCleanupTests.cs`: Phase 3 contract test that exercises OnDestroy directly.

**Concrete bug**: If Test A registers Agents into `Store.Instance`, then Test B (same fixture or a later fixture) instantiates `AnimoBootstrapper` and triggers OnDestroy, Test B's cleanup wipes `Store.Instance._agents`. If Test C (next in execution order) depends on Test A's registrations, Test C fails — but the failure is order-dependent and flaky. The guard is correct for production (DontDestroyOnLoad NPCs survive scene transitions, their Store entries should too) but the production-correct guard happens to also fire under EditMode.

**Resolution**: Two-sided discipline, spec'd in §11.6.5 EN+JP:
1. **Test-side**: Fixtures touching `Store` MUST call `Animo.Store.ResetForTesting()` in `[SetUp]` (or use a shared base class). Each test starts deterministic regardless of what predecessors did.
2. **Bootstrapper-side**: The Q-S118 editor-only guard remains unchanged. Production scene transitions preserve Store; Editor-after-Stop cleans static state.
3. **Bootstrapper-OnDestroy fixtures specifically**: Isolate (separate assembly OR document "assumes empty Store, runs last").

The two concerns separate cleanly. Q-S130 spec-levels what was previously implicit; future test authors don't need to rediscover via flaky failures.

**Cross-impact**: spec EN+JP §11.6.5 (new sub-section). No code change (the discipline applies to test author behavior, not Bootstrapper code).

### Hallucination report (HALLUC #4)

**Recorded with grep evidence**:

Gemini's 12th attack (Round 28): `A035_PostComposeTriggerGtResetTests.Case01` is a "zombie flow" because `trigger: 0.0f` is rejected by Stage 1 A010 (Q-S15 tightening to `(0.0, 100.0]`), so per the Mermaid diagram "if no errors → Composer → Stage 2", Stage 2 should never run on this input. The test bypasses Stage 1 by calling `Composer.Compose` and `Validator.ValidateStage2` directly, which Gemini called "test execution flow diverging from runtime flow."

**Grep verification (rejecting the attack)**:
- spec line 3170-3181 `PersonaCache.Initialize`:
  ```csharp
  _validation = Validator.Validate(root: root);
  if (_validation.has_errors) {
      AnimoLog.Error(
          $"animo.json failed validation with {_validation.errors.Count} errors. ...");
      // Caller decides whether to abort scene load.
  }
  _cache.Clear();
  ```
  Stage-1 has_errors is logged, the caller is told to decide, and the cache continues normally. Stage 2 is **still callable** when GetComposed is invoked.
- `Tests~/EditModeTests/Validator/A035_PostComposeTriggerGtResetTests.cs:23-27` (test docstring): "Note: Case01's input has trigger=0 which Phase_2_4_7 also rejects via A010 (Q-S15 tightening). The intent here is that BOTH rules fire on this input, providing defense in depth — A010 from the stage-1 boundary side, A035 from the stage-2 post-fill side. The test asserts A035 specifically."

The test deliberately exercises the **defense-in-depth fallback path**: caller invoked Stage 2 even though Stage 1 had errors. The Mermaid diagram is the **happy-path** (no errors → smooth Compose+Stage 2 chain), not an exclusive flow specification. Gemini conflated "production happy-path graph" with "all valid execution paths."

**Rejection grounds**:
- spec line 3170-3181 = explicit allowance of Stage-2 invocation post-Stage-1-fail.
- Test docstring = explicit defense-in-depth declaration.
- No phantom fix required.

**Why Gemini missed the runtime-flow detail**: The Mermaid diagram is the visual emphasis; the prose narrative at line 3178 ("Caller decides whether to abort scene load") is easily skimmed past. Master's grep-first discipline checks both.

pattern as Q-S128 (NEED_INDICES_BY_TIER). Spec pseudocode `.Length` →
`.Count` in EN+JP (9 occurrences). New tests: ConstReadOnlyListTests (5 Green).

**Q-S132** — TraceFrame Phase 3 lightweight snapshot contract.
TraceResult.cs TraceFrame docstring documents the OOM risk (216,000 frames ×
4 objects = ~864,000 allocs in a 1-hour soak test) and mandates parallel
float[] arrays with shared string[] key array as the Phase 3 implementation
pattern. Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case05.

**Q-S133** — AnimoBootstrapper JSON parse fail-loud contract.
Phase 3 pattern: catch Exception, emit AnimoLog.Error, re-throw. Swallowing
hides the root cause behind cryptic PersonaCacheNotInitializedException from
every Agent. Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case02.

**Q-S134** — NeedMeta.DeepCopy() stub.
Returns `new NeedMeta { tier = this.tier }`. v0.1.5 value-type-only so
shallow copy is deep copy. Explicit method forces v0.2 implementers to extend
it when new fields arrive. Phase 3 contract: Persona.DeepCopy() calls
meta.DeepCopy() per entry. Tests: NeedMetaDeepCopyTests (4 Green).

Pseudocode updated to `<= 1.0f + SIBLING_THRESHOLD_EPSILON`. Design reasoning
mirrors Q-S47 THRESHOLD_KEY_EPSILON: 0.001f is 100× float32 drift (~1e-5)
and 10× below the merge collapse window (0.01f). Tests: A039 Case02 (Red —
Phase 3 pending).

**Q-S136** — System.Math.Round fully qualified in ScenarioRunner docstring.
Q-S127 pattern: `using System.Collections.Generic` present but no
`using System;`. Phase 3 transcription of the docstring pseudocode would hit
CS0103 on bare `Math`. Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case01.

**Q-S137** — MockScene.Add ITimeProvider DI contract documented.
Q-S115 recorded the Phase 3 DI seam for Agent; Q-S137 pairs it with the test
harness side. MockScene.Add docstring describes the MockTimeProvider injection
pattern and a convenience overload proposal. Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case06.

**Q-S138** — ValidationResult O(1) backing-list design documented.
Validator.cs Emit helper pattern: `List<Issue> _errors`, `_warnings`, `_infos`
pre-sorted at issue-emission time. has_errors, errors, warnings, infos
become O(1) field reads. Merge extends all three backing lists.
Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case03.

**Q-S139** — MiniUnity duplicate #nullable enable removed.
4 files had `#nullable enable` both at file top (line 4) and inside the
namespace body before the class declaration. Second occurrence removed from
MockBus.cs, MockGameObject.cs, MockMonoBehaviour.cs, MockScene.cs.
MockTime.cs was clean. Tests: Round29ContractTests.Case04.

timing. Rejected.

spec line 5938: "runs Steps 2-5 over the spawn (post-t=0-event) Needs" —
explicit design intent. Q-S8 prevents spurious fire from ctor-initialization
artifact (pre-seeding _previous_effective_needs = spawn values). Q-S55
applies a real state change (Affect) after ctor; downstream Threshold fire
is the observable intended result. Two different scenarios, no conflict.
Rejected.

### Q-S144 Design Contract (logging responsibility)

Pre-Q-S144: spec pseudocode showed PersonaCache.GetComposed calling AnimoLog.Error(msg)
then throw, and Agent.Awake catch also calling AnimoLog.Error(...). Same root cause logged twice.

Post-Q-S144 Phase 3 contract:
- PersonaCache.GetComposed: throw PersonaTemplateRejectedException(msg) — NO AnimoLog call.
  The exception message carries the full diagnostic detail.
- Agent.Awake catch (PersonaTemplateRejectedException): log once with Agent context prepended:
  AnimoLog.Error($"Agent '{name}' template '{_persona_template_id}' rejected: {ex.Message}");
  Then: enabled = false; return;
- PersonaCacheNotInitializedException: NOT caught by Agent; propagates to Unity's hard error.

This split ensures exactly one log per failure, with maximum context.

### HALLUC #21 Self-Correction Note

Phase_2_4_26's Q-S135 adoption used Python exhaustive search that computed
float subtraction in float64 precision — incorrect for C# float32 semantics.
Re-run in C# confirms: `1.1f - 0.1f = 1.0f` (Gemini was right for that pair),
but `2.4f - 1.4f = 1.0000001f` proves C# float32 subtraction DOES produce
drift > 1.0 for 12 pairs in [0,100]. Q-S135 EPSILON is justified but for
different pairs than originally stated. Decision log corrected.

### Q-S151 Empirical Verification

```csharp
public class Needs { public Dictionary<string, float> values { get; set; } = new(); }
string json = """{"hunger": 40.0, "fatigue": 20.0}""";
var needs = JsonConvert.DeserializeObject<Needs>(json)!;
// Result: needs.values.Count == 0   ← every Agent spawns with no Needs
```

Solution validated empirically (both Option A `[JsonExtensionData]` and
Option B `Dictionary<string,float>?` work). Option A chosen for minimal
disruption (preserves Q-S65 `.values` pattern in 8 existing tests).
